===== /src/navigator/dictionary.rs =====

   1: //! VisualDictionary — maps file paths to their visual templates.
   2: //!
   3: //! Persists to `.ghostwriter/` inside the destination directory:
   4: //!   .ghostwriter/dictionary.json   — metadata (sidebar bounds, row height, entries)
   5: //!   .ghostwriter/templates/*.png   — one PNG per file
   6: 
   7: use crate::navigator::screen::Rect;
   8: use anyhow::Result;
   9: use image::DynamicImage;
  10: use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
  11: use std::collections::HashMap;
  12: use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
  13: use tracing::{debug, warn};
  14: 
  15: // ─── Persisted metadata ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  16: 
  17: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
  18: pub struct DictMeta {
  19:     pub sidebar_region: Rect,
  20:     /// Measured pixel height of one file-tree row.
  21:     pub row_height_px: u32,
  22:     /// NCC score below which we refuse to click (re-capture instead).
  23:     pub match_threshold: f32,
  24:     pub entries: HashMap<String, EntryMeta>,
  25: }
  26: 
  27: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
  28: pub struct EntryMeta {
  29:     /// Relative path from dest root to the PNG template on disk.
  30:     pub template_file: PathBuf,
  31:     /// Last y coordinate (full screen) where we found this file.
  32:     pub last_known_y: Option<u32>,
  33:     /// Parent directory components ("src", "typer", …).
  34:     pub parent_dirs: Vec<String>,
  35:     /// Last match confidence. Stored so we know if a template is going stale.
  36:     pub confidence: f32,
  37: }
  38: 
  39: // ─── Runtime entry ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  40: 
  41: pub struct DictEntry {
  42:     pub meta: EntryMeta,
  43:     /// Template image loaded into memory.
  44:     pub template: DynamicImage,
  45: }
  46: 
  47: // ─── VisualDictionary ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  48: 
  49: pub struct VisualDictionary {
  50:     root: PathBuf,
  51:     pub sidebar_region: Rect,
  52:     pub row_height_px: u32,
  53:     pub match_threshold: f32,
  54:     entries: HashMap<PathBuf, DictEntry>,
  55: }
  56: 
  57: impl std::fmt::Debug for VisualDictionary {
  58:     fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
  59:         f.debug_struct("VisualDictionary")
  60:             .field("root", &self.root)
  61:             .field("entries", &self.entries.len())
  62:             .finish()
  63:     }
  64: }
  65: 
  66: impl VisualDictionary {
  67:     const META_FILE: &'static str = ".ghostwriter/dictionary.json";
  68:     const TEMPLATES_DIR: &'static str = ".ghostwriter/templates";
  69:     const DEFAULT_THRESHOLD: f32 = 0.82;
  70:     const DEFAULT_ROW_HEIGHT: u32 = 22;
  71: 
  72:     /// Create a new, empty dictionary for `dest_root`.
  73:     pub fn new(dest_root: &Path, sidebar_region: Rect) -> Self {
  74:         Self {
  75:             root: dest_root.to_path_buf(),
  76:             sidebar_region,
  77:             row_height_px: Self::DEFAULT_ROW_HEIGHT,
  78:             match_threshold: Self::DEFAULT_THRESHOLD,
  79:             entries: HashMap::new(),
  80:         }
  81:     }
  82: 
  83:     /// Load from disk if the metadata file exists; otherwise return None.
  84:     pub fn load(dest_root: &Path) -> Result<Option<Self>> {
  85:         let meta_path = dest_root.join(Self::META_FILE);
  86:         if !meta_path.exists() {
  87:             return Ok(None);
  88:         }
  89: 
  90:         let raw = std::fs::read_to_string(&meta_path)?;
  91:         let meta: DictMeta = serde_json::from_str(&raw)?;
  92: 
  93:         let mut entries: HashMap<PathBuf, DictEntry> = HashMap::new();
  94:         for (key, entry_meta) in &meta.entries {
  95:             let template_abs = dest_root.join(&entry_meta.template_file);
  96:             match image::open(&template_abs) {
  97:                 Ok(img) => {
  98:                     entries.insert(
  99:                         PathBuf::from(key),
 100:                         DictEntry {
 101:                             meta: entry_meta.clone(),
 102:                             template: img,
 103:                         },
 104:                     );
 105:                 }
 106:                 Err(e) => {
 107:                     warn!("could not load template {}: {e}", template_abs.display());
 108:                 }
 109:             }
 110:         }
 111: 
 112:         debug!("loaded {} templates from disk", entries.len());
 113:         Ok(Some(Self {
 114:             root: dest_root.to_path_buf(),
 115:             sidebar_region: meta.sidebar_region,
 116:             row_height_px: meta.row_height_px,
 117:             match_threshold: meta.match_threshold,
 118:             entries,
 119:         }))
 120:     }
 121: 
 122:     /// Persist dictionary metadata and all templates to disk.
 123:     pub fn save(&self) -> Result<()> {
 124:         let templates_dir = self.root.join(Self::TEMPLATES_DIR);
 125:         std::fs::create_dir_all(&templates_dir)?;
 126: 
 127:         let entries: HashMap<String, EntryMeta> = self
 128:             .entries
 129:             .iter()
 130:             .map(|(k, v)| (k.to_string_lossy().to_string(), v.meta.clone()))
 131:             .collect();
 132: 
 133:         let meta = DictMeta {
 134:             sidebar_region: self.sidebar_region,
 135:             row_height_px: self.row_height_px,
 136:             match_threshold: self.match_threshold,
 137:             entries,
 138:         };
 139: 
 140:         let json = serde_json::to_string_pretty(&meta)?;
 141:         std::fs::write(self.root.join(Self::META_FILE), json)?;
 142:         Ok(())
 143:     }
 144: 
 145:     /// Insert a new template. Saves the PNG and updates in-memory state.
 146:     pub fn insert(
 147:         &mut self,
 148:         rel_path: &Path,
 149:         template: DynamicImage,
 150:         last_known_y: Option<u32>,
 151:     ) -> Result<()> {
 152:         let safe_name = rel_path.to_string_lossy().replace(['/', '\\', ' '], "_");
 153:         let png_rel = PathBuf::from(Self::TEMPLATES_DIR).join(format!("{safe_name}.png"));
 154:         let png_abs = self.root.join(&png_rel);
 155: 
 156:         std::fs::create_dir_all(png_abs.parent().unwrap())?;
 157:         template.save(&png_abs)?;
 158: 
 159:         let parent_dirs: Vec<String> = rel_path
 160:             .parent()
 161:             .map(|p| {
 162:                 p.components()
 163:                     .filter_map(|c| c.as_os_str().to_str().map(str::to_string))
 164:                     .collect()
 165:             })
 166:             .unwrap_or_default();
 167: 
 168:         self.entries.insert(
 169:             rel_path.to_path_buf(),
 170:             DictEntry {
 171:                 meta: EntryMeta {
 172:                     template_file: png_rel,
 173:                     last_known_y,
 174:                     parent_dirs,
 175:                     confidence: 1.0,
 176:                 },
 177:                 template,
 178:             },
 179:         );
 180: 
 181:         Ok(())
 182:     }
 183: 
 184:     /// Retrieve the runtime entry for a path.
 185:     pub fn get(&self, rel_path: &Path) -> Option<&DictEntry> {
 186:         self.entries.get(rel_path)
 187:     }
 188: 
 189:     #[allow(dead_code)]
 190:     pub fn get_mut(&mut self, rel_path: &Path) -> Option<&mut DictEntry> {
 191:         self.entries.get_mut(rel_path)
 192:     }
 193: 
 194:     pub fn contains(&self, rel_path: &Path) -> bool {
 195:         self.entries.contains_key(rel_path)
 196:     }
 197: 
 198:     /// Update the last known y position after a successful click.
 199:     pub fn update_position(&mut self, rel_path: &Path, y: u32, confidence: f32) {
 200:         if let Some(entry) = self.entries.get_mut(rel_path) {
 201:             entry.meta.last_known_y = Some(y);
 202:             entry.meta.confidence = confidence;
 203:         }
 204:     }
 205: 
 206:     /// Remove an entry so it gets re-captured on next access.
 207:     #[allow(dead_code)]
 208:     pub fn invalidate(&mut self, rel_path: &Path) {
 209:         self.entries.remove(rel_path);
 210:     }
 211: 
 212:     pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
 213:         self.entries.len()
 214:     }
 215: }

===== /src/navigator/mod.rs =====

   1: //! Visual navigator — the self-building system that lets ghostwriter move
   2: //! the mouse to files in VSCode's Explorer sidebar instead of using `code --goto`.
   3: //!
   4: //! ## Setup contract
   5: //!
   6: //! Call `Navigator::bootstrap()` ONCE before playback, pointing it at the
   7: //! COMPLETE source repo.  This:
   8: //!   - Forces VSCode fullscreen (stable pixel dimensions)
   9: //!   - Detects the sidebar region and DPI scale
  10: //!   - Opens every file and captures its visual template
  11: //!   - Saves a persistent dictionary to `.ghostwriter/` in `dest`
  12: //!
  13: //! Subsequent runs load the dictionary from disk and skip calibration entirely.
  14: //!
  15: //! ## Navigation
  16: //!
  17: //! Call `Navigator::open(rel_path)` during playback to move the mouse to a
  18: //! file and click it.  Falls back to `code --goto` if the template match
  19: //! fails after 3 retries.
  20: //!
  21: //! ## Auto-capture
  22: //!
  23: //! Call `Navigator::capture_new_file(rel_path)` immediately after creating a
  24: //! new file during playback.  VSCode highlights the new file automatically;
  25: //! we detect and store its template on the spot.
  26: 
  27: pub mod bootstrap;
  28: pub mod bootstrap_error;
  29: pub mod calibrator;
  30: pub mod dictionary;
  31: pub mod mouse;
  32: pub mod screen;
  33: pub mod sidebar;
  34: pub mod template;
  35: 
  36: use crate::orchestrator::script::DevelopmentScript;
  37: use anyhow::Result;
  38: use bootstrap::{BootstrapResult, VscodeLayout};
  39: use calibrator::capture_new_file as do_capture_new_file;
  40: use dictionary::VisualDictionary;
  41: use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
  42: use std::collections::HashSet;
  43: use tokio::time::{sleep, Duration};
  44: use tracing::{info, warn};
  45: 
  46: pub struct Navigator {
  47:     dict: VisualDictionary,
  48:     pub layout: VscodeLayout,
  49:     dest: PathBuf,
  50:     scale: f32,
  51: }
  52: 
  53: impl Navigator {
  54:     /// Run the bootstrap sequence on `source_root` (the complete repo).
  55:     /// Returns a ready-to-use `Navigator`.
  56:     pub async fn bootstrap(
  57:         script: &DevelopmentScript,
  58:         source_root: &Path,
  59:         dest: &Path,
  60:     ) -> Result<Self> {
  61:         let file_paths = collect_paths(script);
  62:         let BootstrapResult { dictionary, layout } =
  63:             bootstrap::run(source_root, dest, &file_paths).await
  64:                 .map_err(|e| {
  65:                     e.print_fatal();
  66:                     anyhow::anyhow!("{e}")
  67:                 })?;
  68: 
  69:         let scale = layout.scale_factor;
  70:         Ok(Self {
  71:             dict: dictionary,
  72:             layout,
  73:             dest: dest.to_path_buf(),
  74:             scale,
  75:         })
  76:     }
  77: 
  78:     /// Click a file in the Explorer sidebar by visual template matching.
  79:     ///
  80:     /// Retries up to 3 times with sidebar scrolling between attempts.
  81:     /// Falls back to `code --goto` if all visual attempts fail.
  82:     pub async fn open(&mut self, rel_path: &Path) -> Result<()> {
  83:         // Expand parents first.
  84:         sidebar::ensure_parents_expanded(rel_path, &self.dict, self.scale).await?;
  85: 
  86:         for attempt in 0..3u8 {
  87:             match self.try_click(rel_path).await {
  88:                 Ok(()) => return Ok(()),
  89:                 Err(e) if attempt < 2 => {
  90:                     warn!("nav attempt {}: {e} — scrolling and retrying", attempt + 1);
  91:                     let sx = (self.dict.sidebar_region.x
  92:                         + self.dict.sidebar_region.width / 2) as i32;
  93:                     let sy = (self.dict.sidebar_region.y
  94:                         + self.dict.sidebar_region.height / 2) as i32;
  95:                     mouse::scroll_sidebar(sx, sy, 3).await?;
  96:                     sleep(Duration::from_millis(200)).await;
  97:                 }
  98:                 Err(e) => {
  99:                     // All visual attempts failed — fall back to CLI.
 100:                     warn!("visual navigation failed for {}: {e} — using CLI fallback", rel_path.display());
 101:                     let abs = self.dest.join(rel_path);
 102:                     crate::typer::vscode::open_file(&abs)?;
 103:                     sleep(Duration::from_millis(400)).await;
 104:                     return Ok(());
 105:                 }
 106:             }
 107:         }
 108:         Ok(())
 109:     }
 110: 
 111:     /// Capture the template for a file just created during playback.
 112:     pub async fn capture_new_file(&mut self, rel_path: &Path) -> Result<()> {
 113:         do_capture_new_file(rel_path, &mut self.dict, self.scale).await?;
 114:         self.dict.save()?;
 115:         Ok(())
 116:     }
 117: 
 118:     // ── Internal ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 119: 
 120:     async fn try_click(&mut self, rel_path: &Path) -> Result<()> {
 121:         let entry = self.dict.get(rel_path).ok_or_else(|| {
 122:             anyhow::anyhow!("no template for {}", rel_path.display())
 123:         })?;
 124: 
 125:         // Capture the sidebar at logical resolution.
 126:         let screenshot = screen::capture_screen()?;
 127:         let logical = scale_down(&screenshot, self.scale);
 128:         let sb = self.dict.sidebar_region;
 129:         let sidebar_img = logical.crop_imm(sb.x, sb.y, sb.width, sb.height);
 130: 
 131:         let result = match entry.meta.last_known_y {
 132:             Some(hy) => {
 133:                 let local_y = hy.saturating_sub(sb.y);
 134:                 template::find_near(&sidebar_img, &entry.template, local_y, 200)?
 135:             }
 136:             None => template::find(&sidebar_img, &entry.template)?,
 137:         };
 138: 
 139:         if result.score < self.dict.match_threshold {
 140:             anyhow::bail!(
 141:                 "NCC score {:.2} < threshold {:.2} for {}",
 142:                 result.score,
 143:                 self.dict.match_threshold,
 144:                 rel_path.display()
 145:             );
 146:         }
 147: 
 148:         let (cx, cy) = result.centre(entry.template.width(), entry.template.height());
 149:         let screen_x = (sb.x + cx) as i32;
 150:         let screen_y = (sb.y + cy) as i32;
 151: 
 152:         info!(
 153:             "→ click {} at ({},{})  score={:.2}",
 154:             rel_path.display(), screen_x, screen_y, result.score
 155:         );
 156: 
 157:         mouse::click(screen_x, screen_y).await?;
 158:         self.dict.update_position(rel_path, sb.y + cy, result.score);
 159: 
 160:         sleep(Duration::from_millis(120)).await;
 161:         Ok(())
 162:     }
 163: }
 164: 
 165: // ─── Helpers ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 166: 
 167: fn collect_paths(script: &DevelopmentScript) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
 168:     use crate::orchestrator::script::Action;
 169:     let mut seen = HashSet::new();
 170:     for stage in &script.stages {
 171:         for action in &stage.actions {
 172:             let p = match action {
 173:                 Action::CreateFile { path, .. } => Some(path.clone()),
 174:                 Action::EditFile { path, .. } => Some(path.clone()),
 175:                 Action::MoveCursor { path, .. } => Some(path.clone()),
 176:                 _ => None,
 177:             };
 178:             if let Some(path) = p { seen.insert(path); }
 179:         }
 180:     }
 181:     seen.into_iter().collect()
 182: }
 183: 
 184: fn scale_down(img: &image::DynamicImage, scale: f32) -> image::DynamicImage {
 185:     if (scale - 1.0).abs() < 0.01 { return img.clone(); }
 186:     let w = (img.width() as f32 / scale) as u32;
 187:     let h = (img.height() as f32 / scale) as u32;
 188:     img.resize_exact(w, h, image::imageops::FilterType::Lanczos3)
 189: }

===== /src/navigator/mouse.rs =====

   1: //! Human-like mouse movement.
   2: //!
   3: //! Real humans don't move the mouse in a straight line. This module produces
   4: //! a cubic Bézier path between two points with a randomised control point,
   5: //! then steps along it with sub-millisecond sleep intervals to get a
   6: //! smooth, organic-looking arc.
   7: 
   8: use anyhow::Result;
   9: use enigo::{Enigo, Mouse, Settings, Coordinate};
  10: use rand::Rng;
  11: use std::time::Duration;
  12: use tokio::time::sleep;
  13: 
  14: /// Move the mouse from its current position to `(tx, ty)` using a
  15: /// randomised Bézier arc.
  16: ///
  17: /// `duration_ms` controls how long the movement takes in total.
  18: /// A value of 80–200ms looks natural for same-panel navigation;
  19: /// 250–400ms for cross-screen movement.
  20: pub async fn move_to(tx: i32, ty: i32, duration_ms: u64) -> Result<()> {
  21:     let mut enigo = Enigo::new(&Settings::default())
  22:         .map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!("enigo init: {e:?}"))?;
  23: 
  24:     // Get current mouse position.
  25:     let (sx, sy) = enigo.location().map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!("{e:?}"))?;
  26: 
  27:     let steps: usize = ((duration_ms / 8).max(10).min(60)) as usize;
  28: 
  29:     // Randomised control point — offset from the midpoint.
  30:     let mut rng = rand::thread_rng();
  31:     let mid_x = (sx + tx) / 2;
  32:     let mid_y = (sy + ty) / 2;
  33:     let jitter_x: i32 = rng.gen_range(-60..=60);
  34:     let jitter_y: i32 = rng.gen_range(-40..=40);
  35:     let cx = mid_x + jitter_x;
  36:     let cy = mid_y + jitter_y;
  37: 
  38:     let step_delay = Duration::from_millis(duration_ms / steps as u64);
  39: 
  40:     for i in 1..=steps {
  41:         let t = i as f64 / steps as f64;
  42:         // Quadratic Bézier: B(t) = (1-t)²·P0 + 2(1-t)t·P1 + t²·P2
  43:         let x = ((1.0 - t).powi(2) * sx as f64
  44:             + 2.0 * (1.0 - t) * t * cx as f64
  45:             + t.powi(2) * tx as f64) as i32;
  46:         let y = ((1.0 - t).powi(2) * sy as f64
  47:             + 2.0 * (1.0 - t) * t * cy as f64
  48:             + t.powi(2) * ty as f64) as i32;
  49: 
  50:         enigo
  51:             .move_mouse(x, y, Coordinate::Abs)
  52:             .map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!("{e:?}"))?;
  53: 
  54:         sleep(step_delay).await;
  55:     }
  56: 
  57:     Ok(())
  58: }
  59: 
  60: /// Move to `(tx, ty)` and perform a single left click.
  61: /// Adds small random pre/post-click delays to avoid mechanical timing.
  62: pub async fn click(tx: i32, ty: i32) -> Result<()> {
  63:     let mut rng = rand::thread_rng();
  64: 
  65:     // How long to travel to the target.
  66:     let travel_ms: u64 = rng.gen_range(90..=220);
  67:     move_to(tx, ty, travel_ms).await?;
  68: 
  69:     // Brief pause before pressing — humans don't click the instant the cursor lands.
  70:     sleep(Duration::from_millis(rng.gen_range(30..=80))).await;
  71: 
  72:     let mut enigo = Enigo::new(&Settings::default())
  73:         .map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!("enigo init: {e:?}"))?;
  74: 
  75:     enigo
  76:         .button(enigo::Button::Left, enigo::Direction::Click)
  77:         .map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!("{e:?}"))?;
  78: 
  79:     // Brief pause after clicking.
  80:     sleep(Duration::from_millis(rng.gen_range(20..=60))).await;
  81: 
  82:     Ok(())
  83: }
  84: 
  85: /// Scroll the sidebar by `ticks` notches.  Positive = down, negative = up.
  86: pub async fn scroll_sidebar(x: i32, y: i32, ticks: i32) -> Result<()> {
  87:     move_to(x, y, 80).await?;
  88: 
  89:     let mut enigo = Enigo::new(&Settings::default())
  90:         .map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!("enigo init: {e:?}"))?;
  91: 
  92:     for _ in 0..ticks.abs() {
  93:         enigo
  94:             .scroll(ticks.signum(), enigo::Axis::Vertical)
  95:             .map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!("{e:?}"))?;
  96:         sleep(Duration::from_millis(16)).await;
  97:     }
  98:     Ok(())
  99: }

===== /src/navigator/screen.rs =====

   1: //! Screen capture and region extraction.
   2: //!
   3: //! Wraps the `screenshots` crate to capture either the full primary display
   4: //! or a specific sub-rectangle.  All coordinates are in physical pixels.
   5: 
   6: use anyhow::Result;
   7: use image::DynamicImage;
   8: use screenshots::Screen;
   9: 
  10: /// A rectangle in physical screen coordinates.
  11: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)]
  12: pub struct Rect {
  13:     pub x: u32,
  14:     pub y: u32,
  15:     pub width: u32,
  16:     pub height: u32,
  17: }
  18: 
  19: impl Rect {
  20:     pub fn new(x: u32, y: u32, width: u32, height: u32) -> Self {
  21:         Self { x, y, width, height }
  22:     }
  23: 
  24:     #[allow(dead_code)]
  25:     pub fn contains_point(&self, px: u32, py: u32) -> bool {
  26:         px >= self.x
  27:             && px < self.x + self.width
  28:             && py >= self.y
  29:             && py < self.y + self.height
  30:     }
  31: 
  32:     /// Expand by `pad` pixels on all sides, clamped to screen bounds.
  33:     #[allow(dead_code)]
  34:     pub fn padded(&self, pad: u32) -> Self {
  35:         Self {
  36:             x: self.x.saturating_sub(pad),
  37:             y: self.y.saturating_sub(pad),
  38:             width: self.width + pad * 2,
  39:             height: self.height + pad * 2,
  40:         }
  41:     }
  42: }
  43: 
  44: /// Capture the full primary display and return it as a `DynamicImage`.
  45: pub fn capture_screen() -> Result<DynamicImage> {
  46:     let screens = Screen::all()?;
  47:     let primary = screens.into_iter().next().ok_or_else(|| {
  48:         anyhow::anyhow!("no screens found")
  49:     })?;
  50:     let raw = primary.capture()?;
  51:     let w = raw.width();
  52:     let h = raw.height();
  53:     let bytes = raw.into_raw();
  54:     let img = image::RgbaImage::from_raw(w, h, bytes)
  55:         .ok_or_else(|| anyhow::anyhow!("screenshot buffer too small"))?;
  56:     Ok(DynamicImage::ImageRgba8(img))
  57: }
  58: 
  59: /// Capture only a sub-rectangle of the primary display.
  60: /// Crops from a full screenshot — avoids capturing the whole display
  61: /// every time which can be slow on high-DPI screens.
  62: #[allow(dead_code)]
  63: pub fn capture_region(region: Rect) -> Result<DynamicImage> {
  64:     let full = capture_screen()?;
  65:     Ok(full.crop_imm(region.x, region.y, region.width, region.height))
  66: }
  67: 
  68: /// Crop a sub-rectangle out of an already-captured image.
  69: #[allow(dead_code)]
  70: pub fn crop(img: &DynamicImage, region: Rect) -> DynamicImage {
  71:     img.crop_imm(region.x, region.y, region.width, region.height)
  72: }
  73: 
  74: /// Returns `true` if the image appears entirely black — indicates screen
  75: /// recording permission was denied (OS returns a black frame).
  76: pub(crate) fn is_all_black(img: &DynamicImage) -> bool {
  77:     let (w, h) = (img.width(), img.height());
  78:     if w == 0 || h == 0 {
  79:         return true;
  80:     }
  81:     let points = [
  82:         (w / 4, h / 4), (w / 2, h / 4), (3 * w / 4, h / 4),
  83:         (w / 4, h / 2), (w / 2, h / 2), (3 * w / 4, h / 2),
  84:         (w / 4, 3 * h / 4), (w / 2, 3 * h / 4), (3 * w / 4, 3 * h / 4),
  85:     ];
  86:     points.iter().all(|&(x, y)| pixel_luma(img, x, y) < 8)
  87: }
  88: 
  89: fn pixel_luma(img: &DynamicImage, x: u32, y: u32) -> u8 {
  90:     use image::GenericImageView;
  91:     let p = img.get_pixel(x.min(img.width() - 1), y.min(img.height() - 1));
  92:     ((p[0] as u16 * 77 + p[1] as u16 * 150 + p[2] as u16 * 29) >> 8) as u8
  93: }
  94: 
  95: /// Return the DPI scale factor of the primary display.
  96: ///
  97: /// Physical pixels = logical pixels × scale_factor.
  98: /// Screenshots come in physical pixels; mouse coordinates are logical pixels.
  99: /// Always divide screenshot coordinates by this value before clicking.
 100: pub fn scale_factor() -> Result<f32> {
 101:     let screens = Screen::all()?;
 102:     let s = screens.into_iter().next().ok_or_else(|| {
 103:         anyhow::anyhow!("no screens found")
 104:     })?;
 105:     Ok(s.display_info.scale_factor)
 106: }
 107: 
 108: /// Return the bounds of the primary display (in PHYSICAL pixels).
 109: pub fn primary_display_bounds() -> Result<Rect> {
 110:     let screens = Screen::all()?;
 111:     let s = screens.into_iter().next().ok_or_else(|| {
 112:         anyhow::anyhow!("no screens found")
 113:     })?;
 114:     Ok(Rect::new(0, 0, s.display_info.width, s.display_info.height))
 115: }

===== /src/navigator/sidebar.rs =====

   1: //! Sidebar detection and file-tree navigation.
   2: //!
   3: //! Responsibilities:
   4: //!  - Find the VSCode file-explorer region within a screenshot.
   5: //!  - Detect folder expand/collapse state.
   6: //!  - Expand parent directories of a target file.
   7: //!  - Find the "selected row" highlight after a file is opened.
   8: 
   9: use crate::navigator::{
  10:     dictionary::VisualDictionary,
  11:     screen::{capture_screen, Rect},
  12:     template,
  13: };
  14: use anyhow::Result;
  15: use image::{DynamicImage, GenericImageView, Rgba};
  16: use tracing::debug;
  17: 
  18: // ─── Sidebar region detection ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
  19: 
  20: /// Attempt to locate the VSCode file-explorer panel in a screenshot.
  21: ///
  22: /// Strategy:
  23: ///   VSCode always renders the activity bar (narrow icon strip) on the far left,
  24: ///   then the primary sidebar (file tree) to its right.
  25: ///   The activity bar is typically 46–50px wide.
  26: ///   The sidebar is typically 200–320px wide.
  27: ///
  28: /// We return the region of the sidebar (not the activity bar) because that is
  29: /// where file names appear.
  30: ///
  31: /// Falls back to a fixed heuristic if the screenshot width is known.
  32: #[allow(dead_code)]
  33: pub fn detect_sidebar(screenshot: &DynamicImage) -> Rect {
  34:     let _w = screenshot.width();
  35:     let h = screenshot.height();
  36: 
  37:     // Heuristic: scan leftward columns looking for the vertical boundary
  38:     // between the activity bar (very narrow, dark icons) and the sidebar.
  39:     // The sidebar has more horizontal text pixel variance.
  40: 
  41:     let activity_bar_width = find_activity_bar_width(screenshot).unwrap_or(48);
  42:     let sidebar_width = estimate_sidebar_width(screenshot, activity_bar_width).unwrap_or(260);
  43: 
  44:     Rect::new(activity_bar_width, 0, sidebar_width, h)
  45: }
  46: 
  47: /// Find the activity bar width by scanning columns for the first column that
  48: /// has significantly more horizontal pixel variance than the bar to its left.
  49: #[allow(dead_code)]
  50: fn find_activity_bar_width(img: &DynamicImage) -> Option<u32> {
  51:     let w = img.width().min(80); // activity bar is never wider than 80px
  52:     let h = img.height();
  53:     let sample_rows = [h / 4, h / 2, 3 * h / 4];
  54: 
  55:     let col_variance: Vec<f64> = (0..w)
  56:         .map(|x| {
  57:             let vals: Vec<f64> = sample_rows
  58:                 .iter()
  59:                 .map(|&y| {
  60:                     let p = img.get_pixel(x, y);
  61:                     (p[0] as f64 + p[1] as f64 + p[2] as f64) / 3.0
  62:                 })
  63:                 .collect();
  64:             let mean = vals.iter().sum::<f64>() / vals.len() as f64;
  65:             vals.iter().map(|v| (v - mean).powi(2)).sum::<f64>() / vals.len() as f64
  66:         })
  67:         .collect();
  68: 
  69:     // Find the column where variance jumps — that's the sidebar border.
  70:     for x in 20..w as usize {
  71:         let left_avg = col_variance[..x].iter().sum::<f64>() / x as f64;
  72:         let right_avg = col_variance[x..].iter().sum::<f64>() / (w as usize - x) as f64;
  73:         if right_avg > left_avg * 2.5 {
  74:             return Some(x as u32);
  75:         }
  76:     }
  77:     None
  78: }
  79: 
  80: #[allow(dead_code)]
  81: fn estimate_sidebar_width(img: &DynamicImage, start_x: u32) -> Option<u32> {
  82:     // Look for the right edge of the sidebar — typically marked by a thin 1px
  83:     // separator line or a jump in background colour.
  84:     let h = img.height();
  85:     let max_x = (start_x + 400).min(img.width());
  86:     let mid_y = h / 2;
  87: 
  88:     let base = img.get_pixel(start_x + 10, mid_y);
  89: 
  90:     for x in (start_x + 150)..max_x {
  91:         let p = img.get_pixel(x, mid_y);
  92:         // Looking for a significant colour shift — the editor area background.
  93:         let diff = pixel_diff(base, p);
  94:         if diff > 40 {
  95:             return Some(x - start_x);
  96:         }
  97:     }
  98:     None
  99: }
 100: 
 101: #[allow(dead_code)]
 102: fn pixel_diff(a: Rgba<u8>, b: Rgba<u8>) -> u32 {
 103:     let dr = (a[0] as i32 - b[0] as i32).abs() as u32;
 104:     let dg = (a[1] as i32 - b[1] as i32).abs() as u32;
 105:     let db = (a[2] as i32 - b[2] as i32).abs() as u32;
 106:     dr + dg + db
 107: }
 108: 
 109: // ─── Selected-row detection ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
 110: 
 111: /// Find the y coordinate of the currently selected/highlighted row in the
 112: /// sidebar. VSCode renders selected items with a distinct background colour.
 113: ///
 114: /// Returns `None` if no clearly highlighted row is found.
 115: pub fn find_selected_row(sidebar_img: &DynamicImage) -> Option<u32> {
 116:     let h = sidebar_img.height();
 117:     let w = sidebar_img.width();
 118:     if w == 0 || h == 0 {
 119:         return None;
 120:     }
 121: 
 122:     // Sample the background colour from the first few rows (should be unselected).
 123:     let bg = row_mean_colour(sidebar_img, 4);
 124: 
 125:     let mut best_y = None;
 126:     let mut best_diff = 25u32; // minimum difference threshold
 127: 
 128:     for y in 0..h {
 129:         let row_col = row_mean_colour(sidebar_img, y);
 130:         let diff = colour_diff(bg, row_col);
 131:         if diff > best_diff {
 132:             best_diff = diff;
 133:             best_y = Some(y);
 134:         }
 135:     }
 136: 
 137:     best_y
 138: }
 139: 
 140: fn row_mean_colour(img: &DynamicImage, y: u32) -> [u8; 3] {
 141:     if y >= img.height() {
 142:         return [0, 0, 0];
 143:     }
 144:     let w = img.width();
 145:     let mut r = 0u64;
 146:     let mut g = 0u64;
 147:     let mut b = 0u64;
 148:     for x in 0..w {
 149:         let p = img.get_pixel(x, y);
 150:         r += p[0] as u64;
 151:         g += p[1] as u64;
 152:         b += p[2] as u64;
 153:     }
 154:     let n = w.max(1) as u64;
 155:     [(r / n) as u8, (g / n) as u8, (b / n) as u8]
 156: }
 157: 
 158: // ─── Collapse-state detection ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
 159: 
 160: /// Return true if the folder arrow at `(cx, cy)` in `sidebar` appears to be
 161: /// pointing right (collapsed) rather than down (expanded).
 162: ///
 163: /// Heuristic: sample a small region to the right of the arrow tip and a small
 164: /// region below it. A right-pointing (collapsed) arrow has more brightness to
 165: /// its right; a down-pointing (expanded) arrow has more brightness below.
 166: fn is_collapsed(sidebar: &DynamicImage, cx: u32, cy: u32) -> bool {
 167:     let w = sidebar.width();
 168:     let h = sidebar.height();
 169:     // Arrow is just left of the file label — offset ~8px left of the template centre.
 170:     let ax = cx.saturating_sub(8).min(w.saturating_sub(10));
 171:     let ay = cy.min(h.saturating_sub(10));
 172:     let brightness_right = region_mean_brightness(sidebar, ax + 4, ay, 6, 4);
 173:     let brightness_down  = region_mean_brightness(sidebar, ax, ay + 4, 4, 6);
 174:     // Collapsed: arrow points right → more lit pixels to the right.
 175:     brightness_down < (brightness_right - 15.0).max(0.0)
 176: }
 177: 
 178: fn region_mean_brightness(img: &DynamicImage, x: u32, y: u32, w: u32, h: u32) -> f32 {
 179:     let end_x = (x + w).min(img.width());
 180:     let end_y = (y + h).min(img.height());
 181:     if end_x <= x || end_y <= y {
 182:         return 0.0;
 183:     }
 184:     let mut total: u64 = 0;
 185:     let mut count: u64 = 0;
 186:     for py in y..end_y {
 187:         for px in x..end_x {
 188:             let p = img.get_pixel(px, py);
 189:             let luma = (p[0] as u16 * 77 + p[1] as u16 * 150 + p[2] as u16 * 29) >> 8;
 190:             total += luma as u64;
 191:             count += 1;
 192:         }
 193:     }
 194:     if count == 0 { 0.0 } else { total as f32 / count as f32 }
 195: }
 196: 
 197: fn colour_diff(a: [u8; 3], b: [u8; 3]) -> u32 {
 198:     (a[0] as i32 - b[0] as i32).unsigned_abs()
 199:         + (a[1] as i32 - b[1] as i32).unsigned_abs()
 200:         + (a[2] as i32 - b[2] as i32).unsigned_abs()
 201: }
 202: 
 203: // ─── Row crop ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 204: 
 205: /// Crop the row at `y` from `sidebar_img` with a few pixels of vertical padding.
 206: pub fn crop_row(sidebar_img: &DynamicImage, y: u32, row_height: u32) -> DynamicImage {
 207:     let pad: u32 = 2;
 208:     let top = y.saturating_sub(pad);
 209:     let height = (row_height + pad * 2).min(sidebar_img.height() - top);
 210:     sidebar_img.crop_imm(0, top, sidebar_img.width(), height)
 211: }
 212: 
 213: // ─── Folder expansion ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 214: 
 215: /// Ensure all parent directories of `rel_path` are expanded in the sidebar
 216: /// so that the file row becomes visible.
 217: ///
 218: /// Algorithm:
 219: ///   1. Try to find the target file. If found, done.
 220: ///   2. Walk parent components from shallowest to deepest.
 221: ///   3. For each parent directory template in the dictionary: if found,
 222: ///      click it to toggle expansion, wait, retry.
 223: ///   4. If no template for a parent exists, fall back to keyboard shortcut.
 224: pub async fn ensure_parents_expanded(
 225:     rel_path: &std::path::Path,
 226:     dict: &VisualDictionary,
 227:     scale: f32,
 228: ) -> Result<()> {
 229:     // Step 1: if the target file is already visible in the sidebar, nothing to do.
 230:     if let Some(entry) = dict.get(rel_path) {
 231:         let screenshot = capture_screen()?;
 232:         let logical = scale_down(&screenshot, scale);
 233:         let sidebar = logical.crop_imm(
 234:             dict.sidebar_region.x,
 235:             dict.sidebar_region.y,
 236:             dict.sidebar_region.width,
 237:             dict.sidebar_region.height,
 238:         );
 239:         let result = template::find(&sidebar, &entry.template)?;
 240:         if result.score >= dict.match_threshold {
 241:             return Ok(());
 242:         }
 243:     }
 244: 
 245:     // Step 2-3: walk parent components shallowest first, expand any collapsed ones.
 246:     let parents: Vec<std::path::PathBuf> = {
 247:         let mut acc = Vec::new();
 248:         let mut cur = rel_path.parent();
 249:         while let Some(p) = cur {
 250:             if p.components().next().is_some() {
 251:                 acc.push(p.to_path_buf());
 252:             }
 253:             cur = p.parent();
 254:         }
 255:         acc.reverse();
 256:         acc
 257:     };
 258: 
 259:     for parent_dir in &parents {
 260:         if let Some(entry) = dict.get(parent_dir) {
 261:             debug!("expanding parent {}", parent_dir.display());
 262:             let screenshot = capture_screen()?;
 263:             let logical = scale_down(&screenshot, scale);
 264:             let sidebar = logical.crop_imm(
 265:                 dict.sidebar_region.x,
 266:                 dict.sidebar_region.y,
 267:                 dict.sidebar_region.width,
 268:                 dict.sidebar_region.height,
 269:             );
 270: 
 271:             let hint_y = entry.meta.last_known_y;
 272:             let result = match hint_y {
 273:                 Some(hy) => template::find_near(&sidebar, &entry.template, hy, 150)?,
 274:                 None => template::find(&sidebar, &entry.template)?,
 275:             };
 276: 
 277:             if result.score >= dict.match_threshold {
 278:                 let (cx, cy) = result.centre(
 279:                     entry.template.width(),
 280:                     entry.template.height(),
 281:                 );
 282:                 // Only click if collapsed — clicking an expanded folder collapses it.
 283:                 if is_collapsed(&sidebar, cx, cy) {
 284:                     let screen_x = dict.sidebar_region.x + cx;
 285:                     let screen_y = dict.sidebar_region.y + cy;
 286:                     crate::navigator::mouse::click(screen_x as i32, screen_y as i32).await?;
 287:                     tokio::time::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(250)).await;
 288:                 }
 289:             }
 290:         } else {
 291:             // Step 4: no template for this parent — use Command Palette Expand All.
 292:             debug!("no template for parent {}, using keyboard expand", parent_dir.display());
 293:             if let Ok(mut emitter) = crate::typer::input::InputEmitter::new() {
 294:                 let mut ed = crate::typer::editor::Editor::new(&mut emitter);
 295:                 if let Err(e) = ed.expand_all().await {
 296:                     debug!("expand_all fallback failed: {e}");
 297:                 }
 298:             }
 299:         }
 300:     }
 301: 
 302:     Ok(())
 303: }
 304: 
 305: fn scale_down(img: &DynamicImage, scale: f32) -> DynamicImage {
 306:     if (scale - 1.0).abs() < 0.01 {
 307:         return img.clone();
 308:     }
 309:     let w = (img.width() as f32 / scale) as u32;
 310:     let h = (img.height() as f32 / scale) as u32;
 311:     img.resize_exact(w, h, image::imageops::FilterType::Lanczos3)
 312: }

===== /src/navigator/template.rs =====

   1: //! Normalised Cross-Correlation (NCC) template matching.
   2: //!
   3: //! Given a haystack image and a needle (template), finds the (x, y) position
   4: //! within the haystack where the template best matches, along with a
   5: //! confidence score in [0, 1].
   6: //!
   7: //! Uses `imageproc::template_matching` under the hood.
   8: 
   9: use anyhow::Result;
  10: use image::{DynamicImage, GrayImage};
  11: use imageproc::template_matching::{find_extremes, match_template, MatchTemplateMethod};
  12: 
  13: /// Result of a single template search.
  14: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
  15: pub struct MatchResult {
  16:     /// Top-left corner of the best match inside the haystack.
  17:     pub x: u32,
  18:     pub y: u32,
  19:     /// NCC score in [0, 1].  1.0 = pixel-perfect match.
  20:     pub score: f32,
  21: }
  22: 
  23: impl MatchResult {
  24:     /// Centre of the matched region given template dimensions.
  25:     pub fn centre(&self, template_w: u32, template_h: u32) -> (u32, u32) {
  26:         (self.x + template_w / 2, self.y + template_h / 2)
  27:     }
  28: }
  29: 
  30: /// Search `haystack` for `needle` using normalised cross-correlation.
  31: /// Returns the best match position and score.
  32: ///
  33: /// Converts both images to luma (greyscale) before matching; this makes
  34: /// the search robust to slight colour shifts caused by VSCode theme changes
  35: /// between calibration and playback.
  36: pub fn find(haystack: &DynamicImage, needle: &DynamicImage) -> Result<MatchResult> {
  37:     let hay_gray: GrayImage = haystack.to_luma8();
  38:     let needle_gray: GrayImage = needle.to_luma8();
  39: 
  40:     if needle_gray.width() > hay_gray.width() || needle_gray.height() > hay_gray.height() {
  41:         anyhow::bail!(
  42:             "template ({}×{}) is larger than haystack ({}×{})",
  43:             needle_gray.width(),
  44:             needle_gray.height(),
  45:             hay_gray.width(),
  46:             hay_gray.height()
  47:         );
  48:     }
  49: 
  50:     let scores = match_template(
  51:         &hay_gray,
  52:         &needle_gray,
  53:         MatchTemplateMethod::CrossCorrelationNormalized,
  54:     );
  55: 
  56:     let extremes = find_extremes(&scores);
  57:     let (bx, by) = extremes.max_value_location;
  58: 
  59:     Ok(MatchResult {
  60:         x: bx,
  61:         y: by,
  62:         score: extremes.max_value,
  63:     })
  64: }
  65: 
  66: /// Search only a horizontal strip of `haystack` around `hint_y`, falling
  67: /// back to the full image if the hint misses.
  68: ///
  69: /// Using the hint dramatically reduces search time on tall sidebars.
  70: pub fn find_near(
  71:     haystack: &DynamicImage,
  72:     needle: &DynamicImage,
  73:     hint_y: u32,
  74:     search_radius_px: u32,
  75: ) -> Result<MatchResult> {
  76:     let h = haystack.height();
  77:     let top = hint_y.saturating_sub(search_radius_px);
  78:     let bot = (hint_y + search_radius_px).min(h);
  79:     let strip_h = bot - top;
  80: 
  81:     if strip_h < needle.height() {
  82:         return find(haystack, needle);
  83:     }
  84: 
  85:     let strip = haystack.crop_imm(0, top, haystack.width(), strip_h);
  86:     let result = find(&strip, needle)?;
  87: 
  88:     // Adjust y back to full-image coordinates.
  89:     Ok(MatchResult {
  90:         y: result.y + top,
  91:         ..result
  92:     })
  93: }
  94: 
  95: /// Build a normalised luma histogram of an image row.
  96: /// Used to detect the "selected row" highlight by comparing histograms.
  97: #[allow(dead_code)]
  98: pub fn row_mean_brightness(img: &DynamicImage, y: u32) -> f32 {
  99:     let gray = img.to_luma8();
 100:     let w = gray.width();
 101:     if y >= gray.height() || w == 0 {
 102:         return 0.0;
 103:     }
 104:     let sum: u64 = (0..w).map(|x| gray.get_pixel(x, y)[0] as u64).sum();
 105:     sum as f32 / w as f32
 106: }

===== /src/navigator/visual_op.rs =====

   1: //! Visual operation runner — the locate→act→confirm lifecycle.
   2: //!
   3: //! A `VisualOp` describes a single screen-driven action (e.g. "click the row
   4: //! for `src/main.rs`"). `execute()` runs the three phases:
   5: //!
   6: //!   1. **Locate** — snapshot the screen, crop to `haystack_region`, run the
   7: //!      threshold cascade in `template::locate`. Returns absolute logical
   8: //!      screen coordinates if the score meets the strict threshold (or the
   9: //!      soft floor for `LowConfidence`).
  10: //!   2. **Act** — invoke the caller-supplied `act` closure with those
  11: //!      coordinates. The closure is responsible for the side effect
  12: //!      (`mouse::click`, keystroke chord, etc.).
  13: //!   3. **Confirm** — wait `stabilise_ms`, snapshot again, run the
  14: //!      `ConfirmPredicate`. Returns `Ok(MatchResult)` only if the predicate
  15: //!      passes.
  16: //!
  17: //! Phase 3 (this file) ships the no-recovery skeleton: any failure returns a
  18: //! typed `VisualError` immediately. Phase 4 wraps `execute` in a recovery
  19: //! ladder that escalates through qualitatively different strategies. See
  20: //! `docs/VISUAL_VERIFICATION.md` for the full design.
  21: 
  22: use crate::navigator::confirm::{ConfirmError, ConfirmPredicate};
  23: use crate::navigator::screen::{self, Rect};
  24: use crate::navigator::template::{self, LocateOutcome, MatchResult};
  25: use anyhow::Result;
  26: use image::DynamicImage;
  27: use std::path::PathBuf;
  28: use std::time::Duration;
  29: use tokio::time::sleep;
  30: 
  31: // ─── Screen-snapshot abstraction ──────────────────────────────────────────────
  32: 
  33: /// Source of screen snapshots.
  34: ///
  35: /// Production uses [`LiveScreen`]; tests use stubs that return a queue of
  36: /// hand-crafted images so the locate→act→confirm pipeline is fully driven by
  37: /// test data.
  38: pub trait ScreenSource {
  39:     fn snapshot(&mut self) -> Result<DynamicImage>;
  40: }
  41: 
  42: /// Production screen source: capture the primary display, then convert to
  43: /// logical pixels via `scale_down`.
  44: pub struct LiveScreen {
  45:     pub scale: f32,
  46: }
  47: 
  48: impl LiveScreen {
  49:     pub fn new(scale: f32) -> Self {
  50:         Self { scale }
  51:     }
  52: }
  53: 
  54: impl ScreenSource for LiveScreen {
  55:     fn snapshot(&mut self) -> Result<DynamicImage> {
  56:         let raw = screen::capture_screen()?;
  57:         if (self.scale - 1.0).abs() < 0.01 {
  58:             return Ok(raw);
  59:         }
  60:         let w = (raw.width() as f32 / self.scale) as u32;
  61:         let h = (raw.height() as f32 / self.scale) as u32;
  62:         Ok(raw.resize_exact(w, h, image::imageops::FilterType::Lanczos3))
  63:     }
  64: }
  65: 
  66: // ─── Errors ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  67: 
  68: /// All failure modes a `VisualOp::execute` (or its recovery wrapper) can
  69: /// return. Each variant carries the op name for log correlation and an
  70: /// optional `diag` path written by Phase 7.
  71: #[derive(Debug)]
  72: pub enum VisualError {
  73:     /// `template::locate` returned `NotFound` — the target is not visible
  74:     /// at any score above `soft_floor`.
  75:     Locate {
  76:         op: String,
  77:         best_score: f32,
  78:         diag: Option<PathBuf>,
  79:     },
  80: 
  81:     /// The act phase ran successfully but the confirm predicate reported
  82:     /// the post-state was wrong.
  83:     Confirm {
  84:         op: String,
  85:         last: ConfirmError,
  86:         diag: Option<PathBuf>,
  87:     },
  88: 
  89:     /// The act closure itself returned an error (e.g. mouse driver failure).
  90:     Act {
  91:         op: String,
  92:         source: String,
  93:     },
  94: 
  95:     /// All recovery rungs exhausted (Phase 4+).
  96:     RecoveryExhausted {
  97:         op: String,
  98:         attempts: u8,
  99:         last_failure: Box<VisualError>,
 100:         diag: Option<PathBuf>,
 101:     },
 102: 
 103:     /// I/O failure during a snapshot or other primitive.
 104:     Io {
 105:         op: String,
 106:         source: String,
 107:     },
 108: }
 109: 
 110: impl std::fmt::Display for VisualError {
 111:     fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
 112:         match self {
 113:             Self::Locate { op, best_score, .. } => {
 114:                 write!(f, "[{op}] locate failed (best_score={best_score:.2})")
 115:             }
 116:             Self::Confirm { op, last, .. } => {
 117:                 write!(f, "[{op}] confirm failed: {last}")
 118:             }
 119:             Self::Act { op, source } => write!(f, "[{op}] act failed: {source}"),
 120:             Self::RecoveryExhausted { op, attempts, last_failure, .. } => {
 121:                 write!(f, "[{op}] recovery exhausted after {attempts} attempts; last: {last_failure}")
 122:             }
 123:             Self::Io { op, source } => write!(f, "[{op}] I/O error: {source}"),
 124:         }
 125:     }
 126: }
 127: 
 128: impl std::error::Error for VisualError {}
 129: 
 130: // ─── The visual operation ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 131: 
 132: /// Description of a single locate→act→confirm operation. Construct via
 133: /// builders (`for_open`, `for_folder_expand`, etc.) added in Phase 5.
 134: ///
 135: /// `Clone` is derived so the recovery loop can pass a fresh copy into each
 136: /// `execute()` attempt.
 137: #[derive(Clone)]
 138: pub struct VisualOp {
 139:     /// Short identifier used in logs and diagnostic filenames.
 140:     /// Examples: "open(src/main.rs)", "expand(src)", "explorer_focus".
 141:     pub name: String,
 142: 
 143:     /// Logical-pixel screen rectangle to crop before running NCC. The
 144:     /// returned `MatchResult` is converted from haystack-local to absolute
 145:     /// coordinates via `region.x + result.x`, `region.y + result.y`.
 146:     pub haystack_region: Rect,
 147: 
 148:     /// What we're looking for.
 149:     pub needle: DynamicImage,
 150: 
 151:     /// Optional vertical hint inside `haystack_region` (sidebar-local
 152:     /// coords). When present, locate uses `find_near` with the default
 153:     /// radius before falling back to full-image `find`.
 154:     pub hint_y: Option<u32>,
 155: 
 156:     /// Locate threshold: score ≥ strict → `Found`. Default 0.82 from
 157:     /// `VisualDictionary::DEFAULT_THRESHOLD`.
 158:     pub strict_threshold: f32,
 159: 
 160:     /// Locate floor: score < soft_floor → `NotFound`.
 161:     pub soft_floor: f32,
 162: 
 163:     /// How long to wait between act and confirm — gives VSCode time to
 164:     /// re-render before we screenshot again.
 165:     pub stabilise_ms: u64,
 166: }
 167: 
 168: impl VisualOp {
 169:     /// Run the three-phase pipeline once. No recovery on failure — any
 170:     /// problem returns a typed `VisualError` immediately. Phase 4 will add
 171:     /// `execute_with_recovery` that wraps this in a `RecoveryLadder`.
 172:     pub async fn execute<S, A, Fut>(
 173:         self,
 174:         screen: &mut S,
 175:         act: A,
 176:         mut confirm: ConfirmPredicate,
 177:     ) -> Result<MatchResult, VisualError>
 178:     where
 179:         S: ScreenSource,
 180:         A: FnOnce(MatchResult) -> Fut,
 181:         Fut: std::future::Future<Output = anyhow::Result<()>>,
 182:     {
 183:         // ── 1. Pre-act snapshot ────────────────────────────────────────────
 184:         let pre = screen.snapshot().map_err(|e| VisualError::Io {
 185:             op: self.name.clone(),
 186:             source: e.to_string(),
 187:         })?;
 188: 
 189:         // ── 2. Crop to haystack region ─────────────────────────────────────
 190:         let region = self.haystack_region;
 191:         if region.x + region.width > pre.width() || region.y + region.height > pre.height() {
 192:             return Err(VisualError::Io {
 193:                 op: self.name,
 194:                 source: format!(
 195:                     "haystack_region {:?} does not fit in snapshot {}×{}",
 196:                     region, pre.width(), pre.height()
 197:                 ),
 198:             });
 199:         }
 200:         let haystack = pre.crop_imm(region.x, region.y, region.width, region.height);
 201: 
 202:         // ── 3. Locate ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 203:         let outcome = template::locate(
 204:             &haystack,
 205:             &self.needle,
 206:             self.hint_y,
 207:             self.strict_threshold,
 208:             self.soft_floor,
 209:         )
 210:         .map_err(|e| VisualError::Io {
 211:             op: self.name.clone(),
 212:             source: e.to_string(),
 213:         })?;
 214: 
 215:         let local_match = match outcome {
 216:             LocateOutcome::Found(m) | LocateOutcome::LowConfidence(m) => m,
 217:             LocateOutcome::NotFound { best_score } => {
 218:                 return Err(VisualError::Locate {
 219:                     op: self.name,
 220:                     best_score,
 221:                     diag: None,
 222:                 });
 223:             }
 224:         };
 225: 
 226:         // ── 4. Convert to absolute (full-screen logical) coords ────────────
 227:         let absolute = MatchResult {
 228:             x: region.x + local_match.x,
 229:             y: region.y + local_match.y,
 230:             score: local_match.score,
 231:         };
 232: 
 233:         // ── 5. Act ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 234:         act(absolute).await.map_err(|e| VisualError::Act {
 235:             op: self.name.clone(),
 236:             source: e.to_string(),
 237:         })?;
 238: 
 239:         // ── 6. Stabilise ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
 240:         if self.stabilise_ms > 0 {
 241:             sleep(Duration::from_millis(self.stabilise_ms)).await;
 242:         }
 243: 
 244:         // ── 7. Post-act snapshot + confirm ────────────────────────────────
 245:         let post = screen.snapshot().map_err(|e| VisualError::Io {
 246:             op: self.name.clone(),
 247:             source: e.to_string(),
 248:         })?;
 249: 
 250:         confirm(&post).map_err(|last| VisualError::Confirm {
 251:             op: self.name,
 252:             last,
 253:             diag: None,
 254:         })?;
 255: 
 256:         Ok(absolute)
 257:     }
 258: }
 259: 
 260: // ─── VisualError builders ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 261: 
 262: impl VisualError {
 263:     /// Attach a diagnostic-screenshot path to the error variants that carry
 264:     /// one. Variants without a `diag` field (`Act`, `Io`) ignore the attach.
 265:     pub fn with_diag(mut self, diag: PathBuf) -> Self {
 266:         match &mut self {
 267:             Self::Locate { diag: d, .. } => *d = Some(diag),
 268:             Self::Confirm { diag: d, .. } => *d = Some(diag),
 269:             Self::RecoveryExhausted { diag: d, .. } => *d = Some(diag),
 270:             Self::Act { .. } | Self::Io { .. } => {}
 271:         }
 272:         self
 273:     }
 274: 
 275:     /// Get the diagnostic path, if one is attached.
 276:     /// Public surface for callers/tests that want to surface the path.
 277:     #[allow(dead_code)]
 278:     pub fn diag_path(&self) -> Option<&PathBuf> {
 279:         match self {
 280:             Self::Locate { diag, .. }
 281:             | Self::Confirm { diag, .. }
 282:             | Self::RecoveryExhausted { diag, .. } => diag.as_ref(),
 283:             Self::Act { .. } | Self::Io { .. } => None,
 284:         }
 285:     }
 286: }
 287: 
 288: // ─── Diagnostic screenshot helper ────────────────────────────────────────────
 289: 
 290: /// Save a labelled diagnostic screenshot to `diag_dir` and return its path.
 291: ///
 292: /// Filename is `{sanitized_op_name}_{unix_ms}_{label}.png`. Sanitisation
 293: /// replaces non-alphanumeric chars with `_` and caps the op-name part at 60
 294: /// chars so paths stay short. Errors (capture failure, save failure, mkdir
 295: /// failure) are logged at warn and `None` is returned — diagnostics never
 296: /// abort the primary error flow.
 297: ///
 298: /// Used by `Navigator::run_with_recovery` to record what the screen looked
 299: /// like when each attempt failed. The diagnostic path is then attached to
 300: /// the `VisualError` via `with_diag` so the caller can surface it in logs
 301: /// or error messages.
 302: pub fn save_diagnostic(
 303:     diag_dir: &std::path::Path,
 304:     op_name: &str,
 305:     label: &str,
 306: ) -> Option<PathBuf> {
 307:     use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};
 308: 
 309:     if let Err(e) = std::fs::create_dir_all(diag_dir) {
 310:         tracing::warn!("diagnostic mkdir {} failed: {e}", diag_dir.display());
 311:         return None;
 312:     }
 313: 
 314:     let ts = SystemTime::now()
 315:         .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
 316:         .map(|d| d.as_millis())
 317:         .unwrap_or(0);
 318: 
 319:     let safe_name: String = op_name
 320:         .chars()
 321:         .map(|c| if c.is_alphanumeric() || c == '_' { c } else { '_' })
 322:         .take(60)
 323:         .collect();
 324: 
 325:     let path = diag_dir.join(format!("{safe_name}_{ts}_{label}.png"));
 326: 
 327:     match screen::capture_screen() {
 328:         Ok(img) => match img.save(&path) {
 329:             Ok(_) => Some(path),
 330:             Err(e) => {
 331:                 tracing::warn!("diagnostic save {} failed: {e}", path.display());
 332:                 None
 333:             }
 334:         },
 335:         Err(e) => {
 336:             tracing::warn!("diagnostic capture_screen failed: {e}");
 337:             None
 338:         }
 339:     }
 340: }
 341: 
 342: // ─── Recovery ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 343: 
 344: /// A single recovery action that the visual-op runner can apply between
 345: /// failed attempts. Each rung does something *qualitatively different* from
 346: /// the previous one — see the escalation philosophy in
 347: /// `docs/VISUAL_VERIFICATION.md` §7. Application of rungs lives on
 348: /// `Navigator` (Phase 5) which owns the mouse/keyboard/dict state.
 349: ///
 350: /// `ClickOffset` and `Rollback` are reserved for ladder builders that
 351: /// haven't been wired yet; they're matched in `apply_rung` so future
 352: /// builders can drop them in without touching the dispatcher.
 353: #[allow(dead_code)]
 354: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
 355: pub enum RecoveryRung {
 356:     /// Expand any collapsed parent directories of `rel_path` before retrying.
 357:     EnsureParentsExpanded(PathBuf),
 358: 
 359:     /// Scroll the sidebar by `ticks` (positive = down, negative = up).
 360:     ScrollSidebar { ticks: i32 },
 361: 
 362:     /// Bring VSCode back to the foreground — covers focus-drift cases where
 363:     /// the click landed on the right pixel but went to a different window.
 364:     RefocusVscode,
 365: 
 366:     /// Click `dy` rows away from the last computed click coordinate. Cheap
 367:     /// way to test "template centred on the wrong row".
 368:     ClickOffset { dy: i32 },
 369: 
 370:     /// Re-capture the template for `rel_path` from the current screen state
 371:     /// (handles theme drift / sidebar re-render).
 372:     RecaptureTemplate(PathBuf),
 373: 
 374:     /// Open the file via the keyboard fallback (Cmd/Ctrl+P → type filename
 375:     /// → Enter). Breaks the realistic-recording illusion but always works
 376:     /// when the file exists.
 377:     KeyboardFileOpen(PathBuf),
 378: 
 379:     /// Last resort: invoke `vscode::open_file` (`code --goto`). Bypasses
 380:     /// the visual layer entirely.
 381:     CliFallback(PathBuf),
 382: 
 383:     /// Press Escape (and click somewhere safe) to return to a known state
 384:     /// after a `WrongPostState` failure.
 385:     Rollback,
 386: }
 387: 
 388: impl RecoveryRung {
 389:     /// Short name for log lines and diagnostic filenames.
 390:     pub fn label(&self) -> &'static str {
 391:         match self {
 392:             Self::EnsureParentsExpanded(_) => "ensure_parents_expanded",
 393:             Self::ScrollSidebar { .. } => "scroll_sidebar",
 394:             Self::RefocusVscode => "refocus_vscode",
 395:             Self::ClickOffset { .. } => "click_offset",
 396:             Self::RecaptureTemplate(_) => "recapture_template",
 397:             Self::KeyboardFileOpen(_) => "keyboard_file_open",
 398:             Self::CliFallback(_) => "cli_fallback",
 399:             Self::Rollback => "rollback",
 400:         }
 401:     }
 402: }
 403: 
 404: /// Ordered list of `RecoveryRung`s the runner steps through after failures.
 405: /// The cursor advances on each `next()`; `for_*` builders give sensible
 406: /// defaults per operation type.
 407: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
 408: pub struct RecoveryLadder {
 409:     rungs: Vec<RecoveryRung>,
 410:     cursor: usize,
 411: }
 412: 
 413: #[allow(dead_code)] // for_folder_expand / for_explorer_open / peek / rewind etc.
 414:                     // are part of the Phase 4 API surface — they'll be wired
 415:                     // by future callers (folder-expand and bootstrap-step
 416:                     // migrations) without further API changes.
 417: impl RecoveryLadder {
 418:     pub fn new(rungs: Vec<RecoveryRung>) -> Self {
 419:         Self { rungs, cursor: 0 }
 420:     }
 421: 
 422:     /// Default ladder for `Navigator::open(rel)`.
 423:     ///
 424:     /// Rationale:
 425:     ///  1. `EnsureParentsExpanded` — most common cause of missing rows.
 426:     ///  2. Scroll down 3 ticks — file may be below the visible region.
 427:     ///  3. Scroll up 3 ticks — or above (after a previous overshoot).
 428:     ///  4. `RefocusVscode` — focus drift before retry.
 429:     ///  5. `RecaptureTemplate` — template might be stale.
 430:     ///  6. `KeyboardFileOpen` — bypass visual layer, still inside VSCode.
 431:     ///  7. `CliFallback` — last resort.
 432:     pub fn for_open(rel: PathBuf) -> Self {
 433:         Self::new(vec![
 434:             RecoveryRung::EnsureParentsExpanded(rel.clone()),
 435:             RecoveryRung::ScrollSidebar { ticks: 3 },
 436:             RecoveryRung::ScrollSidebar { ticks: -3 },
 437:             RecoveryRung::RefocusVscode,
 438:             RecoveryRung::RecaptureTemplate(rel.clone()),
 439:             RecoveryRung::KeyboardFileOpen(rel.clone()),
 440:             RecoveryRung::CliFallback(rel),
 441:         ])
 442:     }
 443: 
 444:     /// Default ladder for expanding a parent directory.
 445:     ///
 446:     /// Folder rows are usually visible (their existence is what makes the
 447:     /// child visible too), so the ladder is shorter — mostly retries with
 448:     /// small offsets.
 449:     pub fn for_folder_expand(rel: PathBuf) -> Self {
 450:         Self::new(vec![
 451:             RecoveryRung::RefocusVscode,
 452:             RecoveryRung::ScrollSidebar { ticks: -2 },
 453:             RecoveryRung::ClickOffset { dy: 1 },
 454:             RecoveryRung::ClickOffset { dy: -1 },
 455:             RecoveryRung::RecaptureTemplate(rel),
 456:         ])
 457:     }
 458: 
 459:     /// Default ladder for the bootstrap "open Explorer" step.
 460:     pub fn for_explorer_open() -> Self {
 461:         Self::new(vec![
 462:             RecoveryRung::RefocusVscode,
 463:             // The bootstrap code currently clicks the activity-bar Explorer
 464:             // icon on first miss; expressed here as a no-op offset so the
 465:             // existing behaviour is preserved when migrated.
 466:             RecoveryRung::ClickOffset { dy: 0 },
 467:         ])
 468:     }
 469: 
 470:     /// The next rung to try, advancing the cursor. Returns `None` when the
 471:     /// ladder is exhausted.
 472:     pub fn next(&mut self) -> Option<RecoveryRung> {
 473:         let r = self.rungs.get(self.cursor).cloned();
 474:         if r.is_some() {
 475:             self.cursor += 1;
 476:         }
 477:         r
 478:     }
 479: 
 480:     /// Peek without advancing.
 481:     pub fn peek(&self) -> Option<&RecoveryRung> {
 482:         self.rungs.get(self.cursor)
 483:     }
 484: 
 485:     /// Number of rungs remaining (untried).
 486:     pub fn remaining(&self) -> usize {
 487:         self.rungs.len().saturating_sub(self.cursor)
 488:     }
 489: 
 490:     /// Total number of rungs (tried + untried).
 491:     pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
 492:         self.rungs.len()
 493:     }
 494: 
 495:     /// True when no rungs are queued.
 496:     pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
 497:         self.rungs.is_empty()
 498:     }
 499: 
 500:     /// How many rungs have been consumed via `next()`.
 501:     pub fn cursor(&self) -> usize {
 502:         self.cursor
 503:     }
 504: 
 505:     /// Reset the cursor to the start (e.g. for re-running on a fresh op).
 506:     pub fn rewind(&mut self) {
 507:         self.cursor = 0;
 508:     }
 509: }
 510: 
 511: // ─── Tests ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 512: //
 513: // We exercise execute() against a stub ScreenSource that returns predetermined
 514: // images, plus stub act / confirm closures that record their invocations. The
 515: // tests verify the three phases run in order, observe the stabilisation
 516: // delay, and propagate failures from each phase as the right VisualError
 517: // variant.
 518: 
 519: #[cfg(test)]
 520: mod tests {
 521:     use super::*;
 522:     use image::{Rgba, RgbaImage};
 523:     use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
 524:     use std::sync::Arc;
 525:     use std::time::Instant;
 526: 
 527:     /// Stub screen source: returns images from a pre-loaded queue. Panics if
 528:     /// asked for more snapshots than were queued (catches test bugs).
 529:     struct StubScreen {
 530:         queue: Vec<DynamicImage>,
 531:         cursor: usize,
 532:     }
 533: 
 534:     impl StubScreen {
 535:         fn new(images: Vec<DynamicImage>) -> Self {
 536:             Self { queue: images, cursor: 0 }
 537:         }
 538:     }
 539: 
 540:     impl ScreenSource for StubScreen {
 541:         fn snapshot(&mut self) -> Result<DynamicImage> {
 542:             if self.cursor >= self.queue.len() {
 543:                 anyhow::bail!("StubScreen exhausted at index {}", self.cursor);
 544:             }
 545:             let img = self.queue[self.cursor].clone();
 546:             self.cursor += 1;
 547:             Ok(img)
 548:         }
 549:     }
 550: 
 551:     /// Build a synthetic image with a 30×10 distinctive needle patch at a
 552:     /// chosen location. The needle pattern is deterministic so NCC against
 553:     /// it returns score ≈1.0.
 554:     fn synth(needle_x: u32, needle_y: u32) -> DynamicImage {
 555:         let mut img = RgbaImage::new(200, 200);
 556:         for p in img.pixels_mut() {
 557:             *p = Rgba([60, 60, 60, 255]);
 558:         }
 559:         for y in needle_y..(needle_y + 10).min(200) {
 560:             for x in needle_x..(needle_x + 30).min(200) {
 561:                 let v = ((x.wrapping_mul(13)) % 200 + 30) as u8;
 562:                 img.put_pixel(x, y, Rgba([v, v, v, 255]));
 563:             }
 564:         }
 565:         DynamicImage::ImageRgba8(img)
 566:     }
 567: 
 568:     fn extract_needle(haystack: &DynamicImage, x: u32, y: u32) -> DynamicImage {
 569:         haystack.crop_imm(x, y, 30, 10)
 570:     }
 571: 
 572:     fn ok_confirm() -> ConfirmPredicate {
 573:         Box::new(|_| Ok(()))
 574:     }
 575: 
 576:     fn fail_confirm(reason: &'static str) -> ConfirmPredicate {
 577:         Box::new(move |_| {
 578:             Err(ConfirmError::PostStateUnchanged { reason: reason.into() })
 579:         })
 580:     }
 581: 
 582:     // ── Happy path ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 583: 
 584:     #[tokio::test]
 585:     async fn execute_runs_locate_act_confirm_in_order() {
 586:         let pre = synth(50, 80);
 587:         let needle = extract_needle(&pre, 50, 80);
 588:         let post = pre.clone(); // confirm uses post snapshot
 589: 
 590:         let mut screen = StubScreen::new(vec![pre, post]);
 591: 
 592:         let op = VisualOp {
 593:             name: "test_op".into(),
 594:             haystack_region: Rect::new(0, 0, 200, 200),
 595:             needle,
 596:             hint_y: None,
 597:             strict_threshold: 0.82,
 598:             soft_floor: 0.6,
 599:             stabilise_ms: 0,
 600:         };
 601: 
 602:         let act_count = Arc::new(AtomicU32::new(0));
 603:         let act_count_clone = act_count.clone();
 604: 
 605:         let result = op.execute(
 606:             &mut screen,
 607:             move |m| {
 608:                 let c = act_count_clone.clone();
 609:                 async move {
 610:                     c.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);
 611:                     // Verify act receives absolute coordinates with score.
 612:                     assert!(m.score > 0.9, "expected high-confidence match, got {}", m.score);
 613:                     Ok(())
 614:                 }
 615:             },
 616:             ok_confirm(),
 617:         )
 618:         .await
 619:         .expect("execute should succeed");
 620: 
 621:         assert_eq!(act_count.load(Ordering::SeqCst), 1, "act must be called exactly once");
 622:         assert!(result.score > 0.9);
 623:         // Absolute coords match the synthesised needle position.
 624:         assert_eq!(result.x, 50);
 625:         assert_eq!(result.y, 80);
 626:     }
 627: 
 628:     #[tokio::test]
 629:     async fn execute_passes_haystack_local_coords_to_act_with_region_offset() {
 630:         // Place the needle at (50, 80) inside a haystack starting at (10, 20).
 631:         // After offset, the needle's absolute coord is (60, 100).
 632:         let mut img = RgbaImage::new(300, 300);
 633:         for p in img.pixels_mut() {
 634:             *p = Rgba([60, 60, 60, 255]);
 635:         }
 636:         // Needle pattern at absolute (60, 100), inside region starting (10, 20).
 637:         for y in 100u32..110 {
 638:             for x in 60u32..90 {
 639:                 let v = ((x.wrapping_mul(13)) % 200 + 30) as u8;
 640:                 img.put_pixel(x, y, Rgba([v, v, v, 255]));
 641:             }
 642:         }
 643:         let pre = DynamicImage::ImageRgba8(img);
 644:         let needle = pre.crop_imm(60, 100, 30, 10);
 645:         let post = pre.clone();
 646: 
 647:         let mut screen = StubScreen::new(vec![pre, post]);
 648: 
 649:         let op = VisualOp {
 650:             name: "offset_test".into(),
 651:             haystack_region: Rect::new(10, 20, 200, 200),
 652:             needle,
 653:             hint_y: None,
 654:             strict_threshold: 0.82,
 655:             soft_floor: 0.6,
 656:             stabilise_ms: 0,
 657:         };
 658: 
 659:         let observed_xy = Arc::new(std::sync::Mutex::new((0u32, 0u32)));
 660:         let observed_xy_clone = observed_xy.clone();
 661: 
 662:         op.execute(
 663:             &mut screen,
 664:             move |m| {
 665:                 let cell = observed_xy_clone.clone();
 666:                 async move {
 667:                     *cell.lock().unwrap() = (m.x, m.y);
 668:                     Ok(())
 669:                 }
 670:             },
 671:             ok_confirm(),
 672:         )
 673:         .await
 674:         .expect("execute");
 675: 
 676:         let (x, y) = *observed_xy.lock().unwrap();
 677:         assert_eq!((x, y), (60, 100), "act must receive absolute screen coords");
 678:     }

 682:     #[tokio::test]
 683:     async fn execute_returns_locate_error_when_target_not_visible() {
 684:         // Pre-snapshot is uniform; needle is distinctive — score will be ~0.91,
 685:         // below our strict threshold of 0.99 (and below the soft floor here too).
 686:         let mut buf = RgbaImage::new(200, 200);
 687:         for p in buf.pixels_mut() {
 688:             *p = Rgba([60, 60, 60, 255]);
 689:         }
 690:         let pre = DynamicImage::ImageRgba8(buf);
 691:         let needle = extract_needle(&synth(50, 80), 50, 80);
 692: 
 693:         // Single image — confirm must NOT be reached.
 694:         let mut screen = StubScreen::new(vec![pre]);
 695: 
 696:         let op = VisualOp {
 697:             name: "miss_test".into(),
 698:             haystack_region: Rect::new(0, 0, 200, 200),
 699:             needle,
 700:             hint_y: None,
 701:             strict_threshold: 0.99,
 702:             soft_floor: 0.95,
 703:             stabilise_ms: 0,
 704:         };
 705: 
 706:         let act_called = Arc::new(AtomicU32::new(0));
 707:         let act_called_c = act_called.clone();
 708: 
 709:         let result = op.execute(
 710:             &mut screen,
 711:             move |_m| {
 712:                 let c = act_called_c.clone();
 713:                 async move {
 714:                     c.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);
 715:                     Ok(())
 716:                 }
 717:             },
 718:             ok_confirm(),
 719:         )
 720:         .await;
 721: 
 722:         assert!(matches!(result, Err(VisualError::Locate { .. })));
 723:         assert_eq!(act_called.load(Ordering::SeqCst), 0,
 724:                    "act must NOT run when locate fails");
 725:     }

 727:     // ── Failure: confirm ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 728: 
 729:     #[tokio::test]
 730:     async fn execute_returns_confirm_error_when_post_state_wrong() {
 731:         let pre = synth(50, 80);
 732:         let needle = extract_needle(&pre, 50, 80);
 733:         let post = pre.clone();
 734:         let mut screen = StubScreen::new(vec![pre, post]);
 735: 
 736:         let op = VisualOp {
 737:             name: "confirm_fail".into(),
 738:             haystack_region: Rect::new(0, 0, 200, 200),
 739:             needle,
 740:             hint_y: None,
 741:             strict_threshold: 0.82,
 742:             soft_floor: 0.6,
 743:             stabilise_ms: 0,
 744:         };
 745: 
 746:         let act_called = Arc::new(AtomicU32::new(0));
 747:         let act_called_c = act_called.clone();
 748: 
 749:         let result = op.execute(
 750:             &mut screen,
 751:             move |_m| {
 752:                 let c = act_called_c.clone();
 753:                 async move {
 754:                     c.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);
 755:                     Ok(())
 756:                 }
 757:             },
 758:             fail_confirm("test failure"),
 759:         )
 760:         .await;
 761: 
 762:         match result {
 763:             Err(VisualError::Confirm { last: ConfirmError::PostStateUnchanged { reason }, op, .. }) => {
 764:                 assert_eq!(reason, "test failure");
 765:                 assert_eq!(op, "confirm_fail");
 766:             }
 767:             other => panic!("expected Confirm error, got {other:?}"),
 768:         }
 769:         // Act DID run before confirm rejected.
 770:         assert_eq!(act_called.load(Ordering::SeqCst), 1);
 771:     }

 864:     // ── Stabilisation delay ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
 865: 
 866:     #[tokio::test]
 867:     async fn execute_observes_stabilise_delay_between_act_and_confirm() {
 868:         let pre = synth(50, 80);
 869:         let needle = extract_needle(&pre, 50, 80);
 870:         let post = pre.clone();
 871:         let mut screen = StubScreen::new(vec![pre, post]);
 872:         let op = VisualOp {
 873:             name: "delay_test".into(),
 874:             haystack_region: Rect::new(0, 0, 200, 200),
 875:             needle,
 876:             hint_y: None,
 877:             strict_threshold: 0.82,
 878:             soft_floor: 0.6,
 879:             stabilise_ms: 80,
 880:         };
 881:         let start = Instant::now();
 882:         op.execute(&mut screen, |_| async { Ok(()) }, ok_confirm()).await.expect("ok");
 883:         let elapsed = start.elapsed();
 884:         assert!(elapsed >= Duration::from_millis(75),
 885:                 "expected ≥75ms (≈ stabilise_ms), got {:?}", elapsed);
 886:     }

 960:     // ── RecoveryLadder ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 961: 
 962:     use std::path::PathBuf;
 963: 
 964:     #[test]
 965:     fn ladder_next_advances_cursor_and_terminates() {
 966:         let mut l = RecoveryLadder::new(vec![
 967:             RecoveryRung::RefocusVscode,
 968:             RecoveryRung::Rollback,
 969:         ]);
 970:         assert_eq!(l.cursor(), 0);
 971:         assert_eq!(l.remaining(), 2);
 972: 
 973:         let first = l.next().expect("first");
 974:         assert!(matches!(first, RecoveryRung::RefocusVscode));
 975:         assert_eq!(l.cursor(), 1);
 976: 
 977:         let second = l.next().expect("second");
 978:         assert!(matches!(second, RecoveryRung::Rollback));
 979:         assert_eq!(l.cursor(), 2);
 980: 
 981:         assert!(l.next().is_none(), "exhausted ladder must return None");
 982:         // Cursor must NOT advance past length.
 983:         assert_eq!(l.cursor(), 2);
 984:     }
 985: 
 986:     #[test]
 987:     fn ladder_peek_does_not_advance() {
 988:         let mut l = RecoveryLadder::new(vec![RecoveryRung::RefocusVscode]);
 989:         assert!(matches!(l.peek().unwrap(), RecoveryRung::RefocusVscode));
 990:         assert!(matches!(l.peek().unwrap(), RecoveryRung::RefocusVscode));
 991:         assert_eq!(l.cursor(), 0);
 992:     }
 993: 
 994:     #[test]
 995:     fn ladder_rewind_resets_cursor() {
 996:         let mut l = RecoveryLadder::new(vec![
 997:             RecoveryRung::RefocusVscode,
 998:             RecoveryRung::Rollback,
 999:         ]);
1000:         l.next();
1001:         l.next();
1002:         assert_eq!(l.remaining(), 0);
1003:         l.rewind();
1004:         assert_eq!(l.cursor(), 0);
1005:         assert_eq!(l.remaining(), 2);
1006:     }

1009:     fn ladder_for_open_starts_with_parent_expansion() {
1010:         let mut l = RecoveryLadder::for_open(PathBuf::from("src/main.rs"));
1011:         match l.next().expect("first rung") {
1012:             RecoveryRung::EnsureParentsExpanded(p) => {
1013:                 assert_eq!(p, PathBuf::from("src/main.rs"));
1014:             }
1015:             other => panic!("expected EnsureParentsExpanded, got {other:?}"),
1016:         }
1017:     }
