 310:             make_file("alpha", FileCategory::Impl, &[]),
 311:             make_file("beta",  FileCategory::Impl, &[]),
 312:             make_file("gamma", FileCategory::Impl, &[]),
 313:         ];
 314:         let graph = make_graph(files);
 315:         let plan = plan(&graph);
 316: 
 317:         let impl_stages: Vec<_> = plan
 318:             .stages
 319:             .iter()
 320:             .filter(|s| s.name.starts_with("impl"))

 323:         assert_eq!(impl_stages.len(), 1, "expected 1 impl stage, got {}", impl_stages.len());

 383:             FileInfo {

 411:         let files = vec![
 412:             make_file("cycle_a", FileCategory::Impl, &["cycle_b"]),
 413:             make_file("cycle_b", FileCategory::Impl, &["cycle_a"]),
 414:         ];
 415:         let graph = make_graph(files);
 416:         // Should not panic.
 417:         let plan = plan(&graph);
 418:         // Both files must appear somewhere.
 419:         let total_slots: usize = plan.stages.iter().map(|s| s.slots.len()).sum();
 420:         assert_eq!(total_slots, 2, "both cycle files must be in plan");
 421:     }

 430:             make_file("f3", FileCategory::Impl, &["f2"]),
 431:             make_file("f4", FileCategory::Impl, &["f3"]),
 432:             make_file("f5", FileCategory::Impl, &["f4"]),
 433:         ];
 434:         let graph = make_graph(files);
 435:         let plan = plan(&graph);
 436:

 438:             .stages
 439:             .iter()

 441:             .count();
 442:         assert!(

===== /src/orchestrator/tasks.rs =====

   1: //! Typed AI task definitions and their responses.
   2: //!
   3: //! Rust creates `AiTask` values from the `StagePlan`. Each task maps to
   4: //! exactly one prompt template and one expected JSON shape. The LLM never
   5: //! sees more than one task at a time.
   6: 
   7: use serde::Deserialize;
   8: use std::path::PathBuf;
   9: 
  10: // ─── Tasks (input to AI) ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  11: 
  12: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
  13: pub enum AiTask {
  14:     /// Reduce a complete file to its skeleton (signatures only, todo!() bodies).
  15:     GenerateStub {
  16:         rel_path: PathBuf,
  17:         final_content: String,
  18:     },
  19: 
  20:     /// Produce N intermediate versions between a stub and the final file.
  21:     GenerateIntermediates {
  22:         rel_path: PathBuf,
  23:         stub_content: String,
  24:         final_content: String,
  25:         /// How many intermediates Rust wants (1–3 based on file complexity).
  26:         count: usize,
  27:     },
  28: 
  29:     /// Write a one-sentence description of what a developer is doing in this stage.
  30:     DescribeTransition {
  31:         stage_name: String,
  32:         /// Rust-generated change summaries per file ("added 3 fns", "new file", etc.)
  33:         changes: Vec<FileChangeSummary>,
  34:     },
  35: }
  36: 
  37: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
  38: pub struct FileChangeSummary {
  39:     pub rel_path: PathBuf,
  40:     pub summary: String,
  41: }
  42: 
  43: // ─── Responses (output from AI, parsed by Rust) ───────────────────────────────
  44: 
  45: #[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
  46: pub struct StubResponse {
  47:     pub stub: String,
  48: }
  49: 
  50: #[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
  51: pub struct IntermediatesResponse {
  52:     pub versions: Vec<String>,
  53: }
  54: 
  55: #[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
  56: pub struct DescribeResponse {
  57:     pub description: String,
  58: }
  59: 
  60: // ─── Parsed result (what Rust does with the response) ────────────────────────
  61: 
  62: #[derive(Debug)]
  63: pub enum AiResult {
  64:     Stub { rel_path: PathBuf, content: String },
  65:     Intermediates { rel_path: PathBuf, versions: Vec<String> },
  66:     Description { stage_name: String, text: String },
  67: }
  68: 
  69: impl AiResult {
  70:     pub fn parse(task: &AiTask, raw_json: &str) -> anyhow::Result<Self> {
  71:         match task {
  72:             AiTask::GenerateStub { rel_path, .. } => {
  73:                 let r: StubResponse = serde_json::from_str(raw_json)?;
  74:                 Ok(Self::Stub {
  75:                     rel_path: rel_path.clone(),
  76:                     content: r.stub,
  77:                 })
  78:             }
  79:             AiTask::GenerateIntermediates { rel_path, count, .. } => {
  80:                 let r: IntermediatesResponse = serde_json::from_str(raw_json)?;
  81:                 // Clamp to requested count so we never get more than expected.
  82:                 let versions = r.versions.into_iter().take(*count).collect();
  83:                 Ok(Self::Intermediates {
  84:                     rel_path: rel_path.clone(),
  85:                     versions,
  86:                 })
  87:             }
  88:             AiTask::DescribeTransition { stage_name, .. } => {
  89:                 let r: DescribeResponse = serde_json::from_str(raw_json)?;
  90:                 Ok(Self::Description {
  91:                     stage_name: stage_name.clone(),
  92:                     text: r.description,
  93:                 })
  94:             }
  95:         }
  96:     }
  97: }

===== /src/repo/clone.rs =====

   1: use crate::config::Config;
   2: use crate::error::GhostError;
   3: use anyhow::Result;
   4: use std::path::PathBuf;
   5: use tracing::info;
   6: 
   7: /// Clone a remote git repository to a temporary directory and return the path.
   8: pub async fn clone_repo(url: &str, _cfg: &Config) -> Result<PathBuf> {
   9:     let tmp = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(
  10:         "ghostwriter-{}",
  11:         url.split('/').last().unwrap_or("repo").trim_end_matches(".git")
  12:     ));
  13: 
  14:     if tmp.exists() {
  15:         info!("reusing cached clone at {}", tmp.display());
  16:         return Ok(tmp);
  17:     }
  18: 
  19:     info!("cloning {} → {}", url, tmp.display());
  20: 
  21:     // git2 clone — runs synchronously; wrap in spawn_blocking so we don't
  22:     // block the tokio executor.
  23:     let url_owned = url.to_string();
  24:     let dest = tmp.clone();
  25:     tokio::task::spawn_blocking(move || {
  26:         git2::Repository::clone(&url_owned, &dest)
  27:             .map_err(|e| GhostError::Clone(e.message().to_string()))
  28:     })
  29:     .await??;
  30: 
  31:     Ok(tmp)
  32: }

===== /src/repo/github.rs =====

   1: /// Helpers for the GitHub REST API (optional — used if git2 clone fails,
   2: /// or if you want to fetch a specific branch/tag/commit by archive ZIP).
   3: 
   4: use anyhow::{Context, Result};
   5: use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
   6: 
   7: #[allow(dead_code)]
   8: pub struct GithubRepo {
   9:     pub owner: String,
  10:     pub name: String,
  11:     pub branch: Option<String>,
  12: }
  13: 
  14: #[allow(dead_code)]
  15: impl GithubRepo {
  16:     /// Parse a GitHub HTTPS URL into its parts.
  17:     pub fn from_url(url: &str) -> Option<Self> {
  18:         // Expected: https://github.com/{owner}/{repo}[/tree/{branch}]
  19:         let parts: Vec<&str> = url
  20:             .trim_end_matches('/')
  21:             .trim_end_matches(".git")
  22:             .split('/')
  23:             .collect();
  24: 
  25:         if parts.len() < 5 {
  26:             return None;
  27:         }
  28:         Some(Self {
  29:             owner: parts[parts.len() - 2].to_string(),
  30:             name: parts[parts.len() - 1].to_string(),
  31:             branch: None,
  32:         })
  33:     }
  34: 
  35:     /// Download the repo as a ZIP archive, extract it into `dest`, and return `dest`.
  36:     ///
  37:     /// GitHub names the top-level directory `{repo}-{branch}/`; that prefix is
  38:     /// stripped so the extracted tree lands directly under `dest`.
  39:     pub async fn download_zip(&self, dest: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf> {
  40:         let branch = self.branch.as_deref().unwrap_or("main");
  41:         let url = format!(
  42:             "https://github.com/{}/{}/archive/refs/heads/{}.zip",
  43:             self.owner, self.name, branch
  44:         );
  45: 
  46:         let resp = reqwest::Client::new()
  47:             .get(&url)
  48:             .send()
  49:             .await
  50:             .context("sending GitHub ZIP request")?;
  51: 
  52:         if !resp.status().is_success() {
  53:             anyhow::bail!("GitHub ZIP download failed: HTTP {}", resp.status());
  54:         }
  55: 
  56:         let bytes: Vec<u8> = resp
  57:             .bytes()
  58:             .await
  59:             .context("reading ZIP response body")?
  60:             .to_vec();
  61: 
  62:         let cursor = std::io::Cursor::new(bytes);
  63:         let mut archive =
  64:             zip::ZipArchive::new(cursor).context("opening ZIP archive")?;
  65: 
  66:         // GitHub names the root entry "{repo}-{branch}/".
  67:         let root_prefix: String = {
  68:             let first = archive.by_index(0).context("reading ZIP root entry")?;
  69:             first.name().split('/').next().unwrap_or("").to_string()
  70:         };
  71: 
  72:         std::fs::create_dir_all(dest)
  73:             .with_context(|| format!("creating dest dir {}", dest.display()))?;
  74: 
  75:         for i in 0..archive.len() {
  76:             let mut entry = archive.by_index(i).context("reading ZIP entry")?;
  77: 
  78:             let name = entry.name().to_string();
  79:             let rel = Path::new(&name)
  80:                 .strip_prefix(&root_prefix)
  81:                 .map(|p| p.to_path_buf())
  82:                 .unwrap_or_else(|_| PathBuf::from(&name));
  83: 
  84:             if rel.as_os_str().is_empty() {
  85:                 continue; // the root dir entry itself
  86:             }
  87: 
  88:             let out = dest.join(&rel);
  89: 
  90:             if entry.is_dir() {
  91:                 std::fs::create_dir_all(&out)
  92:                     .with_context(|| format!("creating dir {}", out.display()))?;
  93:             } else {
  94:                 if let Some(parent) = out.parent() {
  95:                     std::fs::create_dir_all(parent)
  96:                         .with_context(|| format!("creating parent {}", parent.display()))?;
  97:                 }
  98:                 let mut f = std::fs::File::create(&out)
  99:                     .with_context(|| format!("creating file {}", out.display()))?;
 100:                 std::io::copy(&mut entry, &mut f)
 101:                     .with_context(|| format!("extracting {}", out.display()))?;
 102:             }
 103:         }
 104: 
 105:         Ok(dest.to_path_buf())
 106:     }
 107: }

===== /src/repo/history.rs =====

   1: //! Git history assessment and loading for commit-based replay mode.
   2: //!
   3: //! Commit-based replay skips the LLM entirely: ghostwriter walks the real git
   4: //! history and converts each commit into one playback stage. This module
   5: //! produces the raw commit data; `orchestrator::commit_planner` turns it into a
   6: //! `DevelopmentScript`.
   7: 
   8: use anyhow::Result;
   9: use git2::Repository;
  10: use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
  11: use tracing::debug;
  12: 
  13: /// Minimum commits required for commit mode to be considered usable.
  14: const MIN_COMMITS: usize = 3;
  15: /// Maximum average files-changed-per-commit before mode is rejected.
  16: const MAX_AVG_FILES: f64 = 20.0;
  17: 
  18: // ─── Public types ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  19: 
  20: /// Summary of a repository's git history quality.
  21: #[derive(Debug)]
  22: pub struct HistoryQuality {
  23:     #[allow(dead_code)]
  24:     pub commit_count: usize,
  25:     #[allow(dead_code)]
  26:     pub avg_files_per_commit: f64,
  27:     /// Whether this history is usable for commit-based replay.
  28:     pub is_usable: bool,
  29:     /// Human-readable explanation of the decision.
  30:     pub reason: String,
  31: }
  32: 
  33: /// Data extracted from a single commit.
  34: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
  35: pub struct CommitInfo {
  36:     pub hash: String,
  37:     pub message: String,
  38:     pub changes: Vec<FileChange>,
  39: }
  40: 
  41: /// A file that was touched in a commit.
  42: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
  43: pub struct FileChange {
  44:     pub path: PathBuf,
  45:     pub kind: ChangeKind,
  46:     /// UTF-8 content of the file *before* this commit (empty for Added).
  47:     pub content_before: String,
  48:     /// UTF-8 content of the file *after* this commit (empty for Deleted).
  49:     pub content_after: String,
  50: }
  51: 
  52: /// How a file was changed.
  53: #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
  54: pub enum ChangeKind {
  55:     Added,
  56:     Modified,
  57:     Deleted,
  58: }
  59: 
  60: // ─── Public API ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  61: 
  62: /// Check whether the repository has usable git history for commit-based replay.
  63: ///
  64: /// Returns a `HistoryQuality` with `is_usable = false` if:
  65: /// - `repo_root` is not a git repository
  66: /// - fewer than `MIN_COMMITS` commits exist
  67: /// - average files changed per commit exceeds `MAX_AVG_FILES`
  68: pub fn assess_history(repo_root: &Path) -> Result<HistoryQuality> {
  69:     let repo = match Repository::open(repo_root) {
  70:         Ok(r) => r,
  71:         Err(_) => {
  72:             return Ok(HistoryQuality {
  73:                 commit_count: 0,
  74:                 avg_files_per_commit: 0.0,
  75:                 is_usable: false,
  76:                 reason: "not a git repository".to_string(),
  77:             });
  78:         }
  79:     };
  80: 
  81:     let head = match repo.head() {
  82:         Ok(h) => h,
  83:         Err(_) => {
  84:             return Ok(HistoryQuality {
  85:                 commit_count: 0,
  86:                 avg_files_per_commit: 0.0,
  87:                 is_usable: false,
  88:                 reason: "repository has no commits".to_string(),
  89:             });
  90:         }
  91:     };
  92: 
  93:     let mut revwalk = repo.revwalk()?;
  94:     revwalk.push(head.target().ok_or_else(|| anyhow::anyhow!("HEAD has no target"))?)?;
  95: 
  96:     let mut commit_count = 0usize;
  97:     let mut total_files = 0usize;
  98: 
  99:     for oid in revwalk {
 100:         let oid = oid?;
 101:         let commit = repo.find_commit(oid)?;
 102:         let tree = commit.tree()?;
 103:         let parent_tree = if commit.parent_count() > 0 {
 104:             Some(commit.parent(0)?.tree()?)
 105:         } else {
 106:             None
 107:         };
 108:         let diff = repo.diff_tree_to_tree(parent_tree.as_ref(), Some(&tree), None)?;
 109:         total_files += diff.deltas().count();
 110:         commit_count += 1;
 111:     }
 112: 
 113:     let avg = if commit_count > 0 {
 114:         total_files as f64 / commit_count as f64
 115:     } else {
 116:         0.0
 117:     };
 118: 
 119:     if commit_count < MIN_COMMITS {
 120:         return Ok(HistoryQuality {
 121:             commit_count,
 122:             avg_files_per_commit: avg,
 123:             is_usable: false,
 124:             reason: format!("{commit_count} commits (need ≥ {MIN_COMMITS})"),
 125:         });
 126:     }
 127:     if avg > MAX_AVG_FILES {
 128:         return Ok(HistoryQuality {
 129:             commit_count,
 130:             avg_files_per_commit: avg,
 131:             is_usable: false,
 132:             reason: format!("{avg:.1} avg files/commit (threshold: {MAX_AVG_FILES})"),
 133:         });
 134:     }
 135: 
 136:     Ok(HistoryQuality {
 137:         commit_count,
 138:         avg_files_per_commit: avg,
 139:         is_usable: true,
 140:         reason: format!("{commit_count} commits, {avg:.1} avg files/commit"),
 141:     })
 142: }
 143: 
 144: /// Load the full commit history from oldest to newest.
 145: ///
 146: /// Each `CommitInfo` includes the file-level changes with before/after content.
 147: /// Binary files (non-UTF-8) produce empty content strings and are kept so
 148: /// `commit_planner` can emit `DeleteFile` actions for them.
 149: pub fn load_history(repo_root: &Path) -> Result<Vec<CommitInfo>> {
 150:     let repo = Repository::open(repo_root)?;
 151: 
 152:     let mut revwalk = repo.revwalk()?;
 153:     revwalk.push_head()?;
 154:     revwalk.set_sorting(git2::Sort::TOPOLOGICAL | git2::Sort::REVERSE)?;
 155: 
 156:     let mut commits = Vec::new();
 157: 
 158:     for oid in revwalk {
 159:         let oid = oid?;
 160:         let commit = repo.find_commit(oid)?;
 161:         let tree = commit.tree()?;
 162:         let parent_tree = if commit.parent_count() > 0 {
 163:             Some(commit.parent(0)?.tree()?)
 164:         } else {
 165:             None
 166:         };
 167: 
 168:         let diff = repo.diff_tree_to_tree(parent_tree.as_ref(), Some(&tree), None)?;
 169: 
 170:         let mut changes = Vec::new();
 171:         for delta in diff.deltas() {
 172:             let kind = match delta.status() {
 173:                 git2::Delta::Added => ChangeKind::Added,
 174:                 git2::Delta::Modified | git2::Delta::Renamed | git2::Delta::Copied => {
 175:                     ChangeKind::Modified
 176:                 }
 177:                 git2::Delta::Deleted => ChangeKind::Deleted,
 178:                 other => {
 179:                     debug!("skipping delta with status {:?}", other);
 180:                     continue;
 181:                 }
 182:             };
 183: 
 184:             // For deleted files the "new" path may be absent; fall back to old.
 185:             let path = delta
 186:                 .new_file()
 187:                 .path()
 188:                 .or_else(|| delta.old_file().path())
 189:                 .map(|p| p.to_path_buf())
 190:                 .unwrap_or_default();
 191: 
 192:             if path.as_os_str().is_empty() {
 193:                 continue;
 194:             }
 195: 
 196:             let content_after = if kind != ChangeKind::Deleted {
 197:                 blob_content(&repo, &tree, &path)
 198:             } else {
 199:                 String::new()
 200:             };
 201: 
 202:             let content_before = match kind {
 203:                 ChangeKind::Modified | ChangeKind::Deleted => {
 204:                     if let Some(pt) = &parent_tree {
 205:                         // For renames, old path differs; use old_file().path().
 206:                         let old_path = delta
 207:                             .old_file()
 208:                             .path()
 209:                             .map(|p| p.to_path_buf())
 210:                             .unwrap_or_else(|| path.clone());
 211:                         blob_content(&repo, pt, &old_path)
 212:                     } else {
 213:                         String::new()
 214:                     }
 215:                 }
 216:                 ChangeKind::Added => String::new(),
 217:             };
 218: 
 219:             changes.push(FileChange { path, kind, content_before, content_after });
 220:         }
 221: 
 222:         commits.push(CommitInfo {
 223:             hash: oid.to_string()[..7].to_string(),
 224:             message: commit.message().unwrap_or("").trim().to_string(),
 225:             changes,
 226:         });
 227:     }
 228: 
 229:     Ok(commits)
 230: }
 231: 
 232: // ─── Helpers ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 233: 
 234: /// Read file content from a git tree entry.
 235: ///
 236: /// Returns an empty string if the file is binary or the path is absent.
 237: fn blob_content(repo: &Repository, tree: &git2::Tree, path: &Path) -> String {
 238:     tree.get_path(path)
 239:         .ok()
 240:         .and_then(|e| repo.find_blob(e.id()).ok())
 241:         .map(|b| String::from_utf8_lossy(b.content()).into_owned())
 242:         .unwrap_or_default()
 243: }
 244: 
 245: // ─── Tests ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 246: 
 247: #[cfg(test)]
 248: mod tests {
 249:     use super::*;
 250:     use std::path::Path;
 251: 
 252:     /// Build a minimal 3-commit git repo in a temp directory.
 253:     fn make_test_repo() -> tempfile::TempDir {
 254:         let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
 255:         let repo = git2::Repository::init(dir.path()).unwrap();
 256:         let sig = git2::Signature::now("test", "test@test.com").unwrap();
 257: 
 258:         // Commit 1: add main.rs
 259:         std::fs::write(dir.path().join("main.rs"), "fn main() {}").unwrap();
 260:         let mut idx = repo.index().unwrap();
 261:         idx.add_path(Path::new("main.rs")).unwrap();
 262:         idx.write().unwrap();
 263:         let tree_id = idx.write_tree().unwrap();
 264:         let tree = repo.find_tree(tree_id).unwrap();
 265:         repo.commit(Some("HEAD"), &sig, &sig, "initial commit", &tree, &[])
 266:             .unwrap();
 267: 
 268:         // Commit 2: add lib.rs
 269:         std::fs::write(dir.path().join("lib.rs"), "pub fn add(a: i32, b: i32) -> i32 { a + b }")
 270:             .unwrap();
 271:         let mut idx = repo.index().unwrap();
 272:         idx.add_path(Path::new("lib.rs")).unwrap();
 273:         idx.write().unwrap();
 274:         let tree_id = idx.write_tree().unwrap();
 275:         let tree = repo.find_tree(tree_id).unwrap();
 276:         let parent = repo.head().unwrap().peel_to_commit().unwrap();
 277:         repo.commit(Some("HEAD"), &sig, &sig, "add lib", &tree, &[&parent])
 278:             .unwrap();
 279: 
 280:         // Commit 3: modify main.rs
 281:         std::fs::write(
 282:             dir.path().join("main.rs"),
 283:             "fn main() { println!(\"hello\"); }",
 284:         )
 285:         .unwrap();
 286:         let mut idx = repo.index().unwrap();
 287:         idx.add_path(Path::new("main.rs")).unwrap();
 288:         idx.write().unwrap();
 289:         let tree_id = idx.write_tree().unwrap();
 290:         let tree = repo.find_tree(tree_id).unwrap();
 291:         let parent = repo.head().unwrap().peel_to_commit().unwrap();
 292:         repo.commit(Some("HEAD"), &sig, &sig, "update main", &tree, &[&parent])
 293:             .unwrap();
 294: 
 295:         dir
 296:     }
 297: 
 298:     #[test]
 299:     fn test_assess_history_usable() {
 300:         let dir = make_test_repo();
 301:         let q = assess_history(dir.path()).unwrap();
 302:         assert!(q.is_usable, "expected usable, got: {}", q.reason);
 303:         assert_eq!(q.commit_count, 3);
 304:     }
 305: 
 306:     #[test]
 307:     fn test_assess_history_not_a_repo() {
 308:         let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
 309:         let q = assess_history(dir.path()).unwrap();
 310:         assert!(!q.is_usable);
 311:         assert!(q.reason.contains("not a git"));
 312:     }
 313: 
 314:     #[test]
 315:     fn test_assess_history_too_few_commits() {
 316:         let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
 317:         let repo = git2::Repository::init(dir.path()).unwrap();
 318:         let sig = git2::Signature::now("t", "t@t.com").unwrap();
 319:         std::fs::write(dir.path().join("a.rs"), "fn a() {}").unwrap();
 320:         let mut idx = repo.index().unwrap();
 321:         idx.add_path(Path::new("a.rs")).unwrap();
 322:         idx.write().unwrap();
 323:         let tid = idx.write_tree().unwrap();
 324:         let tree = repo.find_tree(tid).unwrap();
 325:         repo.commit(Some("HEAD"), &sig, &sig, "one", &tree, &[]).unwrap();
 326: 
 327:         let q = assess_history(dir.path()).unwrap();
 328:         assert!(!q.is_usable);
 329:         assert!(q.reason.contains("commits"));
 330:     }
 331: 
 332:     #[test]
 333:     fn test_load_history_chronological_order() {
 334:         let dir = make_test_repo();
 335:         let commits = load_history(dir.path()).unwrap();
 336:         assert_eq!(commits.len(), 3);
 337:         assert_eq!(commits[0].message, "initial commit");
 338:         assert_eq!(commits[2].message, "update main");
 339:     }
 340: 
 341:     #[test]
 342:     fn test_load_history_added_file() {
 343:         let dir = make_test_repo();
 344:         let commits = load_history(dir.path()).unwrap();
 345:         let first = &commits[0];
 346:         assert_eq!(first.changes.len(), 1);
 347:         assert_eq!(first.changes[0].kind, ChangeKind::Added);
 348:         assert_eq!(first.changes[0].path, Path::new("main.rs"));
 349:         assert!(first.changes[0].content_before.is_empty());
 350:         assert!(!first.changes[0].content_after.is_empty());
 351:     }
 352: 
 353:     #[test]
 354:     fn test_load_history_modified_file_has_before_and_after() {
 355:         let dir = make_test_repo();
 356:         let commits = load_history(dir.path()).unwrap();
 357:         let third = &commits[2];
 358:         let change = &third.changes[0];
 359:         assert_eq!(change.kind, ChangeKind::Modified);
 360:         assert!(!change.content_before.is_empty());
 361:         assert!(!change.content_after.is_empty());
 362:         assert_ne!(change.content_before, change.content_after);
 363:     }
 364: }

===== /src/repo/mod.rs =====

   1: mod clone;
   2: mod github;
   3: pub mod history;
   4: 
   5: use crate::config::Config;
   6: use anyhow::Result;
   7: use std::path::PathBuf;
   8: 
   9: /// Fetch the repository to a local temp directory and return its root path.
  10: ///
  11: /// Supports:
  12: ///   - HTTPS GitHub URLs   https://github.com/owner/repo
  13: ///   - SSH GitHub URLs     git@github.com:owner/repo.git
  14: ///   - Local paths         /absolute/or/relative/path
  15: pub async fn fetch(repo: &str, cfg: &Config) -> Result<PathBuf> {
  16:     if looks_like_local_path(repo) {
  17:         // Already local — just return it as-is.
  18:         return Ok(PathBuf::from(repo));
  19:     }
  20:     clone::clone_repo(repo, cfg).await
  21: }
  22: 
  23: fn looks_like_local_path(s: &str) -> bool {
  24:     s.starts_with('/') || s.starts_with('.') || s.starts_with('~')
  25: }

===== /src/session.rs =====

   1: //! Session state — `.ghostwriter/session.toml`
   2: //!
   3: //! Separates *run-specific* state (what repo, what dest, which stages are done)
   4: //! from the behaviour config in `ghostwriter.toml` (typing speed, typo rate, …).
   5: //!
   6: //! **Written by:** `ghostwriter run` at start, then updated after each stage.
   7: //! **Read by:** `ghostwriter resume` to know where to restart.
   8: //! **Never edited by hand** — treat as a machine-generated file.
   9: //!
  10: //! ## File location
  11: //! Always at `<dest>/.ghostwriter/session.toml`.
  12: //! The `<dest>` directory is the recording destination, not the source repo.
  13: 
  14: use anyhow::{Context, Result};
  15: use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
  16: use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
  17: use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};
  18: 
  19: /// File name written inside `<dest>/.ghostwriter/`
  20: const SESSION_FILE: &str = "session.toml";
  21: 
  22: // ─── Top-level struct ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  23: 
  24: /// Complete session state, persisted to `.ghostwriter/session.toml`.
  25: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
  26: pub struct SessionState {
  27:     pub session: SessionMeta,
  28:     pub run: RunParams,
  29:     pub checkpoint: CheckpointState,
  30: }
  31: 
  32: // ─── [session] ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  33: 
  34: /// Immutable metadata written once when the session starts.
  35: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
  36: pub struct SessionMeta {
  37:     /// Short random ID for log correlation (e.g. "a3f7b2").
  38:     pub id: String,
  39: 
  40:     /// Original repo URL or local path that was passed to `ghostwriter run`.
  41:     pub repo: String,
  42: 
  43:     /// Absolute path to the destination directory.
  44:     pub dest: PathBuf,
  45: 
  46:     /// ISO-8601 timestamp when the session was created.
  47:     pub started_at: String,
  48: 
  49:     /// ghostwriter version that created this session file.
  50:     pub ghostwriter_version: String,
  51: }
  52: 
  53: // ─── [run] ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  54: 
  55: /// Run-time parameters that were resolved at session start.
  56: /// Some of these can be overridden by `ghostwriter resume` flags,
  57: /// but the original values are preserved here for auditability.
  58: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
  59: pub struct RunParams {
  60:     /// Seed used for all randomised timing and typos. `None` → seeded from entropy.
  61:     pub seed: Option<u64>,
  62: 
  63:     /// Effective WPM after applying --speed multiplier.
  64:     pub wpm_effective: u32,
  65: 
  66:     /// Whether typo simulation was disabled.
  67:     pub no_typos: bool,
  68: 
  69:     /// Path to the DevelopmentScript JSON that is being executed.
  70:     pub script_path: PathBuf,
  71: 
  72:     /// Absolute path to the source repo (after cloning/resolving).
  73:     pub source_root: PathBuf,
  74: }
  75: 
  76: // ─── [checkpoint] ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  77: 
  78: /// Mutable state — updated after every completed stage.
  79: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
  80: pub struct CheckpointState {
  81:     /// Stage names that have been fully completed.
  82:     pub completed_stages: Vec<String>,
  83: 
  84:     /// The stage that will run next (or `None` if all stages are done).
  85:     pub next_stage: Option<String>,
  86: 
  87:     /// ISO-8601 timestamp of the last checkpoint write.
  88:     pub last_updated_at: String,
  89: 
  90:     /// Lifecycle status of the session.
  91:     pub status: SessionStatus,
  92: }
  93: 
  94: #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
  95: #[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
  96: pub enum SessionStatus {
  97:     /// Session started but no stages have completed yet.
  98:     Started,
  99:     /// At least one stage has completed; more remain.
 100:     InProgress,
 101:     /// All stages finished successfully.
 102:     Completed,
 103:     /// Session was interrupted (process killed, error, Ctrl-C).
 104:     Interrupted,
 105:     /// A non-recoverable error occurred; resuming may not be possible.
 106:     Failed,
 107: }
 108: 
 109: // ─── impl SessionState ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 110: 
 111: impl SessionState {
 112:     /// Create a new session state for a fresh `ghostwriter run`.
 113:     pub fn new(
 114:         repo: impl Into<String>,
 115:         dest: PathBuf,
 116:         source_root: PathBuf,
 117:         script_path: PathBuf,
 118:         seed: Option<u64>,
 119:         wpm_effective: u32,
 120:         no_typos: bool,
 121:         all_stage_names: &[String],
 122:     ) -> Self {
 123:         let id = random_id();
 124:         let now = now_iso8601();
 125:         let next_stage = all_stage_names.first().cloned();
 126: 
 127:         Self {
 128:             session: SessionMeta {
 129:                 id,
 130:                 repo: repo.into(),
 131:                 dest: dest.clone(),
 132:                 started_at: now.clone(),
 133:                 ghostwriter_version: env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION").to_string(),
 134:             },
 135:             run: RunParams {
 136:                 seed,
 137:                 wpm_effective,
 138:                 no_typos,
 139:                 script_path,
 140:                 source_root,
 141:             },
 142:             checkpoint: CheckpointState {
 143:                 completed_stages: Vec::new(),
 144:                 next_stage,
 145:                 last_updated_at: now,
 146:                 status: SessionStatus::Started,
 147:             },
 148:         }
 149:     }
 150: 
 151:     // ── Persistence ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 152: 
 153:     /// Path to the session file given a destination directory.
 154:     pub fn path_for(dest: &Path) -> PathBuf {
 155:         dest.join(".ghostwriter").join(SESSION_FILE)
 156:     }
 157: 
 158:     /// Write the session state to `<dest>/.ghostwriter/session.toml`.
 159:     pub fn save(&self) -> Result<()> {
 160:         let path = Self::path_for(&self.session.dest);
 161:         if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
 162:             std::fs::create_dir_all(parent)
 163:                 .with_context(|| format!("create .ghostwriter dir at {}", parent.display()))?;
 164:         }
 165:         let toml = toml::to_string_pretty(self)
 166:             .context("serialise session state to TOML")?;
 167:         std::fs::write(&path, toml)
 168:             .with_context(|| format!("write session file at {}", path.display()))?;
 169:         Ok(())
 170:     }
 171: 
 172:     /// Load a session from `<dest>/.ghostwriter/session.toml`.
 173:     pub fn load(dest: &Path) -> Result<Self> {
 174:         let path = Self::path_for(dest);
 175:         let raw = std::fs::read_to_string(&path)
 176:             .with_context(|| format!("read session file at {}", path.display()))?;
 177:         toml::from_str(&raw)
 178:             .with_context(|| format!("parse session file at {}", path.display()))
 179:     }
 180: 
 181:     /// Returns `true` if a session file exists in the given destination.
 182:     pub fn exists(dest: &Path) -> bool {
 183:         Self::path_for(dest).exists()
 184:     }
 185: 
 186:     // ── Checkpoint updates ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
 187: 
 188:     /// Mark a stage as complete and advance the checkpoint.
 189:     /// Call this immediately after a stage finishes, before starting the next.
 190:     pub fn complete_stage(
 191:         &mut self,
 192:         stage_name: &str,
 193:         all_stage_names: &[String],
 194:     ) -> Result<()> {
 195:         self.checkpoint.completed_stages.push(stage_name.to_string());
 196: 
 197:         // Find the next uncompleted stage.
 198:         let completed_set: std::collections::HashSet<&str> = self
 199:             .checkpoint
 200:             .completed_stages
 201:             .iter()
 202:             .map(|s| s.as_str())
 203:             .collect();
 204: 
 205:         self.checkpoint.next_stage = all_stage_names
 206:             .iter()
 207:             .find(|s| !completed_set.contains(s.as_str()))
 208:             .cloned();
 209: 
 210:         self.checkpoint.last_updated_at = now_iso8601();
 211:         self.checkpoint.status = if self.checkpoint.next_stage.is_none() {
 212:             SessionStatus::Completed
 213:         } else {
 214:             SessionStatus::InProgress
 215:         };
 216: 
 217:         self.save()
 218:     }
 219: 
 220:     /// Mark the session as interrupted (e.g. Ctrl-C handler).
 221:     #[allow(dead_code)]
 222:     pub fn mark_interrupted(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
 223:         self.checkpoint.status = SessionStatus::Interrupted;
 224:         self.checkpoint.last_updated_at = now_iso8601();
 225:         self.save()
 226:     }
 227: 
 228:     /// Mark the session as failed (non-recoverable error).
 229:     #[allow(dead_code)]
 230:     pub fn mark_failed(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
 231:         self.checkpoint.status = SessionStatus::Failed;
 232:         self.checkpoint.last_updated_at = now_iso8601();
 233:         self.save()
 234:     }
 235: 
 236:     // ── Resume helpers ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 237: 
 238:     /// Returns the stage to start from during a resume.
 239:     /// If `override_stage` is provided (from `--from-stage`), that wins.
 240:     /// Otherwise uses `checkpoint.next_stage`.
 241:     pub fn resume_from_stage<'a>(&'a self, override_stage: Option<&'a str>) -> Option<&'a str> {
 242:         if let Some(s) = override_stage {
 243:             return Some(s);
 244:         }
 245:         self.checkpoint.next_stage.as_deref()
 246:     }
 247: 
 248:     /// Returns `true` if the given stage has already been completed.
 249:     pub fn is_stage_done(&self, stage_name: &str) -> bool {
 250:         self.checkpoint
 251:             .completed_stages
 252:             .iter()
 253:             .any(|s| s == stage_name)
 254:     }
 255: }
 256: 
 257: // ─── Helpers ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 258: 
 259: fn now_iso8601() -> String {
 260:     // Produce a basic RFC-3339 UTC timestamp from std::time (no external crate).
 261:     // Format: 1970-01-01T00:00:00Z
 262:     let secs = SystemTime::now()
 263:         .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
 264:         .map(|d| d.as_secs())
 265:         .unwrap_or(0);
 266: 
 267:     let s = secs % 60;
 268:     let m = (secs / 60) % 60;
 269:     let h = (secs / 3600) % 24;
 270:     let days = secs / 86400;
 271: 
 272:     // Gregorian calendar reconstruction from day count since 1970-01-01
 273:     let (year, month, day) = days_to_ymd(days);
 274: 
 275:     format!("{year:04}-{month:02}-{day:02}T{h:02}:{m:02}:{s:02}Z")
 276: }
 277: 
 278: /// Convert a count of days since the Unix epoch to (year, month, day).
 279: fn days_to_ymd(days: u64) -> (u64, u64, u64) {
 280:     // Shift epoch to 1 Mar 0000 for simpler leap-year math (Euclidean calendrics).
 281:     let days = days + 719468;
 282:     let era = days / 146097;
 283:     let doe = days % 146097;
 284:     let yoe = (doe - doe / 1460 + doe / 36524 - doe / 146096) / 365;
 285:     let y = yoe + era * 400;
 286:     let doy = doe - (365 * yoe + yoe / 4 - yoe / 100);
 287:     let mp = (5 * doy + 2) / 153;
 288:     let d = doy - (153 * mp + 2) / 5 + 1;
 289:     let m = if mp < 10 { mp + 3 } else { mp - 9 };
 290:     let y = if m <= 2 { y + 1 } else { y };
 291:     (y, m, d)
 292: }
 293: 
 294: fn random_id() -> String {
 295:     // Six hex chars — enough for log correlation, not a security identifier.
 296:     // Mix nanoseconds + PID for sufficient uniqueness between runs.
 297:     use std::collections::hash_map::DefaultHasher;
 298:     use std::hash::Hasher;
 299: 
 300:     let nanos = SystemTime::now()
 301:         .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
 302:         .map(|d| d.subsec_nanos())
 303:         .unwrap_or(0);
 304:     let pid = std::process::id();
 305: 
 306:     let mut h = DefaultHasher::new();
 307:     h.write_u32(nanos);
 308:     h.write_u32(pid);
 309:     format!("{:06x}", h.finish() & 0x00FF_FFFF)
 310: }
 311: 
 312: #[cfg(test)]
 313: mod tests {
 314:     use super::*;
 315:     use tempfile::TempDir;
 316: 
 317:     fn fake_stages() -> Vec<String> {
 318:         vec![
 319:             "skeleton".into(),
 320:             "types".into(),
 321:             "impl_0".into(),
 322:             "tests".into(),
 323:         ]
 324:     }

 326:     fn make_session(dest: &Path) -> SessionState {
 327:         SessionState::new(
 328:             "https://github.com/test/repo",
 329:             dest.to_path_buf(),
 330:             dest.join("source"),
 331:             dest.join(".ghostwriter/script.json"),
 332:             Some(42),
 333:             75,
 334:             false,
 335:             &fake_stages(),
 336:         )
 337:     }
 338: 
 339:     #[test]
 340:     fn round_trip_save_load() {
 341:         let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
 342:         let mut s = make_session(tmp.path());
 343:         s.save().unwrap();
 344: 
 345:         let loaded = SessionState::load(tmp.path()).unwrap();
 346:         assert_eq!(loaded.session.repo, "https://github.com/test/repo");
 347:         assert_eq!(loaded.checkpoint.status, SessionStatus::Started);
 348:         assert_eq!(
 349:             loaded.checkpoint.next_stage.as_deref(),
 350:             Some("skeleton")
 351:         );
 352:     }
 353: 
 354:     #[test]
 355:     fn complete_stages_advances_checkpoint() {
 356:         let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
 357:         let stages = fake_stages();
 358:         let mut s = make_session(tmp.path());
 359: 
 360:         s.complete_stage("skeleton", &stages).unwrap();
 361:         assert!(s.is_stage_done("skeleton"));
 362:         assert_eq!(s.checkpoint.next_stage.as_deref(), Some("types"));
 363:         assert_eq!(s.checkpoint.status, SessionStatus::InProgress);
 364: 
 365:         s.complete_stage("types", &stages).unwrap();
 366:         s.complete_stage("impl_0", &stages).unwrap();
 367:         s.complete_stage("tests", &stages).unwrap();
 368:         assert_eq!(s.checkpoint.status, SessionStatus::Completed);
 369:         assert!(s.checkpoint.next_stage.is_none());
 370:     }
 371: 
 372:     #[test]
 373:     fn resume_from_stage_respects_override() {
 374:         let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
 375:         let stages = fake_stages();
 376:         let mut s = make_session(tmp.path());
 377:         s.complete_stage("skeleton", &stages).unwrap();
 378: 
 379:         // Without override → uses checkpoint
 380:         assert_eq!(s.resume_from_stage(None), Some("types"));
 381:         // With override → override wins
 382:         assert_eq!(s.resume_from_stage(Some("impl_0")), Some("impl_0"));
 383:     }
 384: 
 385:     #[test]
 386:     fn exists_returns_false_when_no_file() {
 387:         let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
 388:         assert!(!SessionState::exists(tmp.path()));
 389:     }
 390: }

===== /src/typer/editor.rs =====

   1: //! Higher-level editor operations built on top of `InputEmitter`.
   2: //!
   3: //! Knows how to navigate VSCode using keyboard shortcuts so the engine
   4: //! doesn't have to think about keystroke sequences.
   5: 
   6: use crate::typer::input::InputEmitter;
   7: use anyhow::Result;
   8: use enigo::Key;
   9: use tokio::time::{sleep, Duration};
  10: 
  11: /// Platform modifier key (Cmd on macOS, Ctrl elsewhere).
  12: #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
  13: const MOD: Key = Key::Meta;
  14: #[cfg(not(target_os = "macos"))]
  15: const MOD: Key = Key::Control;
  16: 
  17: pub struct Editor<'a> {
  18:     input: &'a mut InputEmitter,
  19: }
  20: 
  21: impl<'a> Editor<'a> {
  22:     pub fn new(input: &'a mut InputEmitter) -> Self {
  23:         Self { input }
  24:     }
  25: 
  26:     /// Open the Go-to-Line palette and jump to `line` (1-based).
  27:     pub async fn goto_line(&mut self, line: usize) -> Result<()> {
  28:         // Ctrl+G / Cmd+G
  29:         self.input.chord(MOD, Key::Unicode('g'))?;
  30:         sleep(Duration::from_millis(120)).await;
  31:         self.input.type_str(&line.to_string())?;
  32:         self.input.press(Key::Return)?;
  33:         sleep(Duration::from_millis(80)).await;
  34:         Ok(())
  35:     }
  36: 
  37:     /// Select from the current cursor position down `lines` lines.
  38:     pub fn select_lines(&mut self, lines: usize) -> Result<()> {
  39:         for _ in 0..lines {
  40:             self.input.chord(Key::Shift, Key::DownArrow)?;
  41:         }
  42:         Ok(())
  43:     }
  44: 
  45:     /// Select to the end of the current line.
  46:     #[allow(dead_code)]
  47:     pub fn select_to_eol(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
  48:         self.input.chord(Key::Shift, Key::End)?;
  49:         Ok(())
  50:     }
  51: 
  52:     /// Delete selected text.
  53:     pub fn delete_selection(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
  54:         self.input.press(Key::Delete)?;
  55:         Ok(())
  56:     }
  57: 
  58:     /// Open VSCode's integrated terminal (Ctrl+` on all platforms).
  59:     pub async fn open_terminal(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
  60:         self.input.chord(Key::Control, Key::Unicode('`'))?;
  61:         sleep(Duration::from_millis(500)).await;
  62:         Ok(())
  63:     }
  64: 
  65:     /// Type a shell command into the integrated terminal and press Enter.
  66:     pub async fn run_command(&mut self, cmd: &str) -> Result<()> {
  67:         self.open_terminal().await?;
  68:         self.input.type_str(cmd)?;
  69:         sleep(Duration::from_millis(60)).await;
  70:         self.input.press(Key::Return)?;
  71:         Ok(())
  72:     }
  73: 
  74:     /// Move to the home position of a line (Ctrl+Home then Ctrl+G).
  75:     #[allow(dead_code)]
  76:     pub async fn goto_line_home(&mut self, line: usize) -> Result<()> {
  77:         self.goto_line(line).await?;
  78:         self.input.press(Key::Home)?;
  79:         Ok(())
  80:     }
  81: 
  82:     /// Select all content in the currently focused input (Ctrl+A / Cmd+A).
  83:     pub fn select_all(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
  84:         self.input.chord(MOD, Key::Unicode('a'))?;
  85:         Ok(())
  86:     }
  87: 
  88:     /// Open VSCode's Find & Replace panel (Ctrl+H / Cmd+H).
  89:     pub async fn open_find_replace(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
  90:         self.input.chord(MOD, Key::Unicode('h'))?;
  91:         sleep(Duration::from_millis(180)).await;
  92:         Ok(())
  93:     }
  94: 
  95:     /// Move focus from the find box to the replace box (Tab).
  96:     pub fn focus_replace_box(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
  97:         self.input.press(Key::Tab)?;
  98:         Ok(())
  99:     }
 100: 
 101:     /// Trigger Replace Next (Enter in the replace box — one match at a time).
 102:     pub fn replace_next(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
 103:         self.input.press(Key::Return)?;
 104:         Ok(())
 105:     }
 106: 
 107:     /// Close the find+replace panel (Escape).
 108:     pub fn close_find_replace(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
 109:         self.input.press(Key::Escape)?;
 110:         Ok(())
 111:     }
 112: 
 113:     /// Open the Command Palette (Ctrl+Shift+P / Cmd+Shift+P).
 114:     pub async fn open_command_palette(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
 115:         self.input.chord2(MOD, Key::Shift, Key::Unicode('p'))?;
 116:         sleep(Duration::from_millis(300)).await;
 117:         Ok(())
 118:     }
 119: 
 120:     /// Run "Explorer: Expand All" via the Command Palette.
 121:     pub async fn expand_all(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
 122:         self.open_command_palette().await?;
 123:         self.input.type_str("Explorer: Expand All")?;
 124:         sleep(Duration::from_millis(200)).await;
 125:         self.input.press(Key::Return)?;
 126:         sleep(Duration::from_millis(500)).await;
 127:         Ok(())
 128:     }
 129: }

===== /src/typer/engine.rs =====

   1: //! Multi-stage playback engine.
   2: //!
   3: //! Iterates over a `DevelopmentScript`, executing each `Action` with
   4: //! human-like timing and QWERTY-accurate typos.
   5: 
   6: use crate::config::Config;
   7: use crate::navigator::Navigator;
   8: use crate::orchestrator::script::{Action, Edit};
   9: use crate::typer::{
  10:     editor::Editor,
  11:     human::{HumanSimulator, TypoEvent},
  12:     input::InputEmitter,
  13:     vscode,
  14: };
  15: use anyhow::{bail, Result};
  16: use std::collections::HashMap;
  17: use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
  18: use tokio::time::{sleep, Duration};
  19: use tracing::{info, warn};
  20: 
  21: pub struct ScriptRunner<'a> {
  22:     cfg: Config,
  23:     dest: PathBuf,
  24:     sim: HumanSimulator,
  25:     input: InputEmitter,
  26:     nav: &'a mut Navigator,
  27:     /// Tracks which files are currently open in VSCode.
  28:     open_files: HashMap<PathBuf, bool>,
  29: }
  30: 
  31: impl<'a> ScriptRunner<'a> {
  32:     pub fn new(dest: PathBuf, cfg: Config, seed: Option<u64>, nav: &'a mut Navigator) -> Result<Self> {
  33:         let sim = HumanSimulator::new(&cfg, seed);
  34:         let input = InputEmitter::new()?;
  35:         Ok(Self {
  36:             cfg,
  37:             dest,
  38:             sim,
  39:             input,
  40:             nav,
  41:             open_files: HashMap::new(),
  42:         })
  43:     }
  44: 
  45:     async fn execute(&mut self, action: &Action) -> Result<()> {
  46:         match action {
  47:             Action::CreateFile { path, stub, intermediates, final_content: _, final_edits } => {
  48:                 self.create_file(path, stub, intermediates, final_edits).await?;
  49:             }
  50:             Action::EditFile { path, edits } => {
  51:                 self.edit_file(path, edits).await?;
  52:             }
  53:             Action::DeleteFile { path } => {
  54:                 let full = self.dest.join(path);
  55:                 if full.exists() {
  56:                     std::fs::remove_file(&full)?;
  57:                     info!("deleted {}", path.display());
  58:                 }
  59:             }
  60:             Action::MoveCursor { path, line } => {
  61:                 self.ensure_open(path).await?;
  62:                 vscode::focus()?;
  63:                 let mut ed = Editor::new(&mut self.input);
  64:                 ed.goto_line(*line).await?;
  65:                 // Simulate reading: pause proportional to how far we jump.
  66:                 let pause_ms = ((*line as f64).ln() * 200.0) as u64;
  67:                 sleep(Duration::from_millis(pause_ms.clamp(300, 3000))).await;
  68:             }
  69:             Action::RunCommand { command, cwd, expect_success: _ } => {
  70:                 let full_command = match cwd {
  71:                     Some(rel_cwd) => format!("cd {} && {}", self.dest.join(rel_cwd).display(), command),
  72:                     None => command.clone(),
  73:                 };
  74:                 info!("run: {}", full_command);
  75:                 vscode::focus()?;
  76:                 let mut ed = Editor::new(&mut self.input);
  77:                 ed.run_command(&full_command).await?;
  78:                 // Wait for the command to finish (simple timeout — improve with regex later).
  79:                 sleep(Duration::from_millis(3000)).await;
  80:             }
  81:             Action::ThinkPause { min_ms, max_ms } => {
  82:                 let ms = self.sim.think_pause_ms(*min_ms, *max_ms);
  83:                 info!("thinking ({ms} ms)…");
  84:                 sleep(Duration::from_millis(ms)).await;
  85:             }
  86:         }
  87:         Ok(())
  88:     }
  89: 
  90:     /// Create a file on disk, open it in VSCode, and type through stub → intermediates → final.
  91:     async fn create_file(
  92:         &mut self,
  93:         rel: &Path,
  94:         stub: &str,
  95:         intermediates: &[String],
  96:         final_edits: &[Edit],
  97:     ) -> Result<()> {
  98:         let full = self.dest.join(rel);
  99:         if let Some(parent) = full.parent() {
 100:             std::fs::create_dir_all(parent)?;
 101:         }
 102:         // 1. Create empty file on disk.
 103:         std::fs::write(&full, "")?;
 104: 
 105:         // 2. Open in VSCode.
 106:         info!("create {}", rel.display());
 107:         self.nav.open(rel).await?;
 108: 
 109:         // 3. Wait for file to open.
 110:         sleep(Duration::from_millis(self.cfg.vscode.file_open_delay_ms)).await;
 111: 
 112:         // 4. Focus VSCode.
 113:         vscode::focus()?;
 114: 
 115:         // 5. Type the stub char-by-char.
 116:         self.type_content(stub).await?;
 117: 
 118:         // 6. Save.
 119:         self.input.save()?;
 120: 
 121:         // 7. Mark file open.
 122:         self.open_files.insert(rel.to_path_buf(), true);
 123: 
 124:         // 8. For each intermediate: think pause, re-open, select_all, retype, save.
 125:         for intermediate in intermediates {
 126:             let pause_ms = self.sim.think_pause_ms(800, 2000);
 127:             sleep(Duration::from_millis(pause_ms)).await;
 128: 
 129:             self.nav.open(rel).await?;
 130:             vscode::focus()?;
 131: 
 132:             {
 133:                 let mut ed = Editor::new(&mut self.input);
 134:                 ed.select_all()?;
 135:             }
 136:             sleep(Duration::from_millis(300)).await;
 137: 
 138:             self.type_content(intermediate).await?;
 139:             self.input.save()?;
 140:         }
 141: 
 142:         // 9. Apply final edits if any.
 143:         if !final_edits.is_empty() {
 144:             let pause_ms = self.sim.think_pause_ms(500, 1500);
 145:             sleep(Duration::from_millis(pause_ms)).await;
 146:             self.edit_file(rel, final_edits).await?;
 147:         }
 148: 
 149:         // 10. Capture sidebar template for this new file.
 150:         if let Err(e) = self.nav.capture_new_file(rel).await {
 151:             warn!("failed to capture template for {}: {}", rel.display(), e);
 152:         }
 153:         Ok(())
 154:     }
 155: 
 156:     /// Navigate to a file, apply each find→replace edit with typing simulation.
 157:     ///
 158:     /// Single-line finds use VSCode's Find & Replace panel (Ctrl+H) for realism.
 159:     /// Multi-line finds fall back to goto+select+delete since Ctrl+H can't span lines.
 160:     async fn edit_file(&mut self, rel: &Path, edits: &[Edit]) -> Result<()> {
 161:         let full = self.dest.join(rel);
 162:         if !full.exists() {
 163:             bail!("edit_file: {} does not exist", full.display());
 164:         }
 165: 
 166:         self.ensure_open(rel).await?;
 167:         vscode::focus()?;
 168: 
 169:         // Mirror kept for multi-line fallback position tracking.
 170:         let mut current = std::fs::read_to_string(&full)?;
 171: 
 172:         for edit in edits {
 173:             if edit.find.contains('\n') {
 174:                 // Multi-line find: Ctrl+H can't handle it — goto+select+delete.
 175:                 let pos = match current.find(&edit.find) {
 176:                     Some(p) => p,
 177:                     None => {
 178:                         warn!("edit_file: could not find {:?} in {}", edit.find, rel.display());
 179:                         current = current.replacen(&edit.find, &edit.replace, 1);
 180:                         continue;
 181:                     }
 182:                 };
 183:                 let line_no = current[..pos].chars().filter(|&c| c == '\n').count() + 1;
 184:                 let find_lines = edit.find.chars().filter(|&c| c == '\n').count();
 185:                 {
 186:                     let mut ed = Editor::new(&mut self.input);
 187:                     ed.goto_line(line_no).await?;
 188:                     sleep(Duration::from_millis(200)).await;
 189:                     ed.select_lines(find_lines + 1)?;
 190:                     sleep(Duration::from_millis(400)).await;
 191:                     ed.delete_selection()?;
 192:                 }
 193:                 self.type_content(&edit.replace).await?;
 194:             } else {
 195:                 // Single-line find: use Ctrl+H Find & Replace (realistic).
 196:                 {
 197:                     let mut ed = Editor::new(&mut self.input);
 198:                     ed.open_find_replace().await?;
 199:                     // Clear any previous content in the find box.
 200:                     ed.select_all()?;
 201:                 }
 202:                 self.input.type_str(&edit.find)?;
 203:                 sleep(Duration::from_millis(80)).await;
 204: 
 205:                 {
 206:                     let mut ed = Editor::new(&mut self.input);
 207:                     ed.focus_replace_box()?;
 208:                 }
 209:                 sleep(Duration::from_millis(80)).await;
 210:                 {
 211:                     let mut ed = Editor::new(&mut self.input);
 212:                     ed.select_all()?;
 213:                 }
 214:                 self.input.type_str(&edit.replace)?;
 215: 
 216:                 // Brief pause — simulates checking the replacement looks right.
 217:                 sleep(Duration::from_millis(300)).await;
 218: 
 219:                 {
 220:                     let mut ed = Editor::new(&mut self.input);
 221:                     ed.replace_next()?;
 222:                 }
 223:                 sleep(Duration::from_millis(200)).await;
 224:                 {
 225:                     let mut ed = Editor::new(&mut self.input);
 226:                     ed.close_find_replace()?;
 227:                 }
 228:                 sleep(Duration::from_millis(100)).await;
 229:             }
 230: 
 231:             current = current.replacen(&edit.find, &edit.replace, 1);
 232:         }
 233: 
 234:         self.input.save()?;
 235:         Ok(())
 236:     }
 237: 
 238:     /// Ensure a file is open in VSCode, opening it via Navigator if necessary.
 239:     async fn ensure_open(&mut self, rel: &Path) -> Result<()> {
 240:         if !self.open_files.contains_key(rel) {
 241:             self.nav.open(rel).await?;
 242:             sleep(Duration::from_millis(self.cfg.vscode.file_open_delay_ms)).await;
 243:             self.open_files.insert(rel.to_path_buf(), true);
 244:         }
 245:         Ok(())
 246:     }
 247: 
 248:     /// Type `content` through the human simulator, handling all typo patterns.
 249:     async fn type_content(&mut self, content: &str) -> Result<()> {
 250:         let chars: Vec<char> = content.chars().collect();
 251:         let mut i = 0;
 252:         let mut prev = ' ';
 253: 
 254:         while i < chars.len() {
 255:             let ch = chars[i];
 256:             let next = chars.get(i + 1).copied();
 257: 
 258:             let delay = self.sim.delay_for(prev, ch);
 259:             sleep(delay).await;
 260: 
 261:             match self.sim.maybe_typo(ch, next) {
 262:                 None => {
 263:                     self.input.type_char(ch)?;
 264:                 }
 265:                 Some(TypoEvent::AdjacentSub { wrong, correct, notice_after }) => {
 266:                     // Type the wrong key.
 267:                     self.input.type_char(wrong)?;
 268:                     // Type `notice_after` more correct chars before noticing.
 269:                     let notice = notice_after.min((chars.len() - i - 1) as u8) as usize;
 270:                     for j in 0..notice {
 271:                         let c = chars.get(i + 1 + j).copied().unwrap_or(' ');
 272:                         sleep(self.sim.delay_for(prev, c)).await;
 273:                         self.input.type_char(c)?;
 274:                     }
 275:                     // Correction: backspace back, retype.
 276:                     sleep(self.sim.correction_delay()).await;
 277:                     for _ in 0..=notice {
 278:                         self.input.backspace()?;
 279:                         sleep(Duration::from_millis(40)).await;
 280:                     }
 281:                     // Retype correct chars.
 282:                     self.input.type_char(correct)?;
 283:                     for j in 0..notice {
 284:                         let c = chars.get(i + 1 + j).copied().unwrap_or(' ');
 285:                         sleep(self.sim.delay_for(prev, c)).await;
 286:                         self.input.type_char(c)?;
 287:                     }
 288:                     i += notice;
 289:                 }
 290:                 Some(TypoEvent::ExtraChar { correct, extra, notice_after: _ }) => {
 291:                     self.input.type_char(correct)?;
 292:                     sleep(Duration::from_millis(60)).await;
 293:                     self.input.type_char(extra)?;
 294:                     sleep(self.sim.correction_delay()).await;
 295:                     self.input.backspace()?;
 296:                 }
 297:                 Some(TypoEvent::AccidentalEnter { notice_after: _ }) => {
 298:                     self.input.type_char(ch)?;
 299:                     self.input.press(enigo::Key::Return)?;
 300:                     sleep(self.sim.correction_delay()).await;
 301:                     self.input.backspace()?;
 302:                 }
 303:                 Some(TypoEvent::Transposition { first, second }) => {
 304:                     // Type second first, then first.
 305:                     self.input.type_char(second)?;
 306:                     sleep(Duration::from_millis(50)).await;
 307:                     self.input.type_char(first)?;
 308:                     sleep(self.sim.correction_delay()).await;
 309:                     // Backspace both, retype correctly.
 310:                     self.input.backspace()?;
 311:                     self.input.backspace()?;
 312:                     self.input.type_char(first)?;
 313:                     self.input.type_char(second)?;
 314:                     i += 1; // consumed next char too
 315:                 }
 316:             }
 317: 
 318:             prev = ch;
 319:             i += 1;
 320:         }
 321: 
 322:         Ok(())
 323:     }
 324: }
 325: 
 326: /// Run a single stage and return. Called by main's checkpoint loop.
 327: pub async fn run_stage(
 328:     stage: &crate::orchestrator::script::Stage,
 329:     dest: &Path,
 330:     cfg: &Config,
 331:     seed: Option<u64>,
 332:     nav: &mut Navigator,
 333: ) -> Result<()> {
 334:     let mut runner = ScriptRunner::new(dest.to_path_buf(), cfg.clone(), seed, nav)?;
 335:     for action in &stage.actions {
 336:         runner.execute(action).await?;
 337:     }
 338:     Ok(())
 339: }

===== /src/typer/human.rs =====

   1: //! Human-like typing simulation.
   2: //!
   3: //! Provides:
   4: //!  - Log-normal per-character delay (always positive, right-skewed).
   5: //!  - Context-aware pauses (end of line, block boundaries, spontaneous thinks).
   6: //!  - QWERTY-accurate typo model with four error patterns.
   7: 
   8: use crate::config::Config;
   9: use rand::prelude::*;
  10: use rand::rngs::SmallRng;
  11: use rand_distr::{LogNormal, Uniform};
  12: use std::time::Duration;
  13: 
  14: // ─── QWERTY adjacency map ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  15: //
  16: // Physical rows (ISO QWERTY):
  17: //
  18: //   ` 1 2 3 4 5 6 7 8 9 0 - =
  19: //   q w e r t y u i o p [ ] \
  20: //   a s d f g h j k l ; '
  21: //   z x c v b n m , . /
  22: //
  23: // Each entry lists keys that are directly left, right, or diagonally adjacent.
  24: // Shift variants (uppercase) are handled separately at call time.
  25: 
  26: const QWERTY: &[(char, &[char])] = &[
  27:     // Top row
  28:     ('1', &['2', 'q', 'w']),
  29:     ('2', &['1', '3', 'q', 'w', 'e']),
  30:     ('3', &['2', '4', 'w', 'e', 'r']),
  31:     ('4', &['3', '5', 'e', 'r', 't']),
  32:     ('5', &['4', '6', 'r', 't', 'y']),
  33:     ('6', &['5', '7', 't', 'y', 'u']),
  34:     ('7', &['6', '8', 'y', 'u', 'i']),
  35:     ('8', &['7', '9', 'u', 'i', 'o']),
  36:     ('9', &['8', '0', 'i', 'o', 'p']),
  37:     ('0', &['9', '-', 'o', 'p', '[']),
  38:     // Second row
  39:     ('q', &['1', '2', 'w', 'a', 's']),
  40:     ('w', &['q', 'e', 'a', 's', 'd', '1', '2', '3']),
  41:     ('e', &['w', 'r', 's', 'd', 'f', '2', '3', '4']),
  42:     ('r', &['e', 't', 'd', 'f', 'g', '3', '4', '5']),
  43:     ('t', &['r', 'y', 'f', 'g', 'h', '4', '5', '6']),
  44:     ('y', &['t', 'u', 'g', 'h', 'j', '5', '6', '7']),
  45:     ('u', &['y', 'i', 'h', 'j', 'k', '6', '7', '8']),
  46:     ('i', &['u', 'o', 'j', 'k', 'l', '7', '8', '9']),
  47:     ('o', &['i', 'p', 'k', 'l', ';', '8', '9', '0']),
  48:     ('p', &['o', '[', 'l', ';', '9', '0']),
  49:     // Home row
  50:     ('a', &['q', 'w', 's', 'z', 'x']),
  51:     ('s', &['a', 'd', 'w', 'e', 'z', 'x', 'c', 'q']),
  52:     ('d', &['s', 'f', 'e', 'r', 'x', 'c', 'v']),
  53:     ('f', &['d', 'g', 'r', 't', 'c', 'v', 'b']),
  54:     ('g', &['f', 'h', 't', 'y', 'v', 'b', 'n']),
  55:     ('h', &['g', 'j', 'y', 'u', 'b', 'n', 'm']),
  56:     ('j', &['h', 'k', 'u', 'i', 'n', 'm', ',']),
  57:     ('k', &['j', 'l', 'i', 'o', 'm', ',', '.']),
  58:     ('l', &['k', ';', 'o', 'p', ',', '.', '/']),
  59:     (';', &['l', '\'', 'p', '[', '.', '/']),
  60:     // Bottom row
  61:     ('z', &['a', 's', 'x']),
  62:     ('x', &['z', 'c', 'a', 's', 'd']),
  63:     ('c', &['x', 'v', 's', 'd', 'f']),
  64:     ('v', &['c', 'b', 'd', 'f', 'g']),
  65:     ('b', &['v', 'n', 'f', 'g', 'h']),
  66:     ('n', &['b', 'm', 'g', 'h', 'j']),
  67:     ('m', &['n', ',', 'h', 'j', 'k']),
  68:     (',', &['m', '.', 'j', 'k', 'l']),
  69:     ('.', &[',', '/', 'k', 'l', ';']),
  70:     ('/', &['.', 'l', ';']),
  71:     // Space bar (neighbours: c, v, b, n, m)
  72:     (' ', &['c', 'v', 'b', 'n', 'm']),
  73: ];
  74: 
  75: /// Look up keys physically adjacent to `ch` on a QWERTY layout.
  76: /// Returns an empty slice for chars not in the map (e.g. Unicode, control).
  77: fn adjacent_keys(ch: char) -> &'static [char] {
  78:     // Normalise to lowercase for lookup; we'll re-apply case at call site.
  79:     let lower = ch.to_lowercase().next().unwrap_or(ch);
  80:     QWERTY
  81:         .iter()
  82:         .find(|(k, _)| *k == lower)
  83:         .map(|(_, v)| *v)
  84:         .unwrap_or(&[])
  85: }
  86: 
  87: // ─── Typo patterns ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  88: 
  89: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
  90: enum TypoKind {
  91:     /// Press a QWERTY-adjacent key instead of the intended one.
  92:     AdjacentSub,
  93:     /// Type the correct key, then also press an adjacent key immediately after.
  94:     ExtraChar,
  95:     /// Press Enter unexpectedly in the middle of a word.
  96:     AccidentalEnter,
  97:     /// Type the next two chars in swapped order.
  98:     Transposition,
  99: }
 100: 
 101: impl TypoKind {
 102:     fn sample(rng: &mut impl Rng) -> Self {
 103:         match rng.gen_range(0..100u8) {
 104:             0..=59 => Self::AdjacentSub,
 105:             60..=79 => Self::ExtraChar,
 106:             80..=89 => Self::AccidentalEnter,
 107:             _ => Self::Transposition,
 108:         }
 109:     }
 110: }
 111: 
 112: // ─── Public API ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 113: 
 114: pub struct HumanSimulator {
 115:     rng: SmallRng,
 116:     base_ms: f64,
 117:     jitter: f64,
 118:     typo_rate: f64,
 119:     /// Chars remaining until next spontaneous think pause.
 120:     think_countdown: u64,
 121:     pause_after_newline: (u64, u64),
 122:     pause_after_open_block: (u64, u64),
 123:     pause_after_close_block: (u64, u64),
 124:     think_every_chars: (u64, u64),
 125:     think_duration: (u64, u64),
 126: }
 127: 
 128: impl HumanSimulator {
 129:     pub fn new(cfg: &Config, seed: Option<u64>) -> Self {
 130:         let mut rng = match seed {
 131:             Some(s) => SmallRng::seed_from_u64(s),
 132:             None => SmallRng::from_entropy(),
 133:         };
 134: 
 135:         // 1 word ≈ 5 chars; WPM → ms/char
 136:         let chars_per_min = cfg.typing.wpm as f64 * 5.0;
 137:         let base_ms = 60_000.0 / chars_per_min;
 138: 
 139:         let (clo, chi) = cfg.typing.pause.think_every_chars;
 140:         let think_countdown = Uniform::new_inclusive(clo, chi).sample(&mut rng);
 141: 
 142:         let p = &cfg.typing.pause;
 143:         Self {
 144:             rng,
 145:             base_ms,
 146:             jitter: cfg.typing.jitter,
 147:             typo_rate: cfg.typing.typo_rate,
 148:             think_countdown,
 149:             pause_after_newline: p.after_newline,
 150:             pause_after_open_block: p.after_open_block,
 151:             pause_after_close_block: p.after_close_block,
 152:             think_every_chars: p.think_every_chars,
 153:             think_duration: p.think_duration,
 154:         }
 155:     }
 156: 
 157:     /// Delay to wait *before* emitting `ch`, given the previous char `prev`.
 158:     pub fn delay_for(&mut self, _prev: char, ch: char) -> Duration {
 159:         let sigma = (1.0 + self.jitter * self.jitter).ln().sqrt();
 160:         let mu = self.base_ms.ln() - sigma * sigma / 2.0;
 161:         let dist = LogNormal::new(mu as f32, sigma as f32).unwrap();
 162:         let mut ms = dist.sample(&mut self.rng) as u64;
 163: 
 164:         ms += self.context_extra(ch);
 165: 
 166:         self.think_countdown = self.think_countdown.saturating_sub(1);
 167:         if self.think_countdown == 0 {
 168:             let (tlo, thi) = self.think_duration;
 169:             ms += Uniform::new_inclusive(tlo, thi).sample(&mut self.rng);
 170:             let (clo, chi) = self.think_every_chars;
 171:             self.think_countdown = Uniform::new_inclusive(clo, chi).sample(&mut self.rng);
 172:         }
 173: 
 174:         Duration::from_millis(ms.max(10))
 175:     }
 176: 
 177:     /// Decide whether a typo happens on this character.
 178:     ///
 179:     /// Returns `None` for no typo, or `Some(TypoEvent)` describing what to do.
 180:     pub fn maybe_typo(&mut self, ch: char, next_ch: Option<char>) -> Option<TypoEvent> {
 181:         if !self.rng.gen_bool(self.typo_rate) {
 182:             return None;
 183:         }
 184:         let kind = TypoKind::sample(&mut self.rng);
 185:         // Characters typed before the dev notices the mistake (1–4).
 186:         let notice_after = Uniform::new_inclusive(1u8, 4).sample(&mut self.rng);
 187: 
 188:         match kind {
 189:             TypoKind::AdjacentSub => {
 190:                 let neighbours = adjacent_keys(ch);
 191:                 if neighbours.is_empty() {
 192:                     return None;
 193:                 }
 194:                 let wrong = *neighbours.choose(&mut self.rng).unwrap();
 195:                 let wrong = if ch.is_uppercase() {
 196:                     wrong.to_uppercase().next().unwrap_or(wrong)
 197:                 } else {
 198:                     wrong
 199:                 };
 200:                 Some(TypoEvent::AdjacentSub { wrong, correct: ch, notice_after })
 201:             }
 202:             TypoKind::ExtraChar => {
 203:                 let neighbours = adjacent_keys(ch);
 204:                 if neighbours.is_empty() {
 205:                     return None;
 206:                 }
 207:                 let extra = *neighbours.choose(&mut self.rng).unwrap();
 208:                 Some(TypoEvent::ExtraChar { correct: ch, extra, notice_after })
 209:             }
 210:             TypoKind::AccidentalEnter => Some(TypoEvent::AccidentalEnter { notice_after }),
 211:             TypoKind::Transposition => {
 212:                 let second = next_ch?;
 213:                 // Only transpose printable, non-whitespace adjacent chars.
 214:                 if second.is_whitespace() || !second.is_ascii() {
 215:                     return None;
 216:                 }
 217:                 Some(TypoEvent::Transposition { first: ch, second })
 218:             }
 219:         }
 220:     }
 221: 
 222:     fn context_extra(&mut self, ch: char) -> u64 {
 223:         let range = match ch {
 224:             '\n' => Some(self.pause_after_newline),
 225:             '{' | ':' => Some(self.pause_after_open_block),
 226:             '}' => Some(self.pause_after_close_block),
 227:             _ => None,
 228:         };
 229:         range
 230:             .map(|(lo, hi)| Uniform::new_inclusive(lo, hi).sample(&mut self.rng))
 231:             .unwrap_or(0)
 232:     }
 233: 
 234:     /// Short delay used between a typo keypress and the backspace.
 235:     pub fn correction_delay(&mut self) -> Duration {
 236:         Duration::from_millis(Uniform::new_inclusive(80u64, 200).sample(&mut self.rng))
 237:     }
 238: 
 239:     /// Sample a think-pause duration from the given range using the seeded RNG.
 240:     /// Use this instead of `rand::thread_rng()` to preserve `--seed` reproducibility.
 241:     pub fn think_pause_ms(&mut self, min_ms: u64, max_ms: u64) -> u64 {
 242:         if min_ms >= max_ms {
 243:             return min_ms;
 244:         }
 245:         Uniform::new_inclusive(min_ms, max_ms).sample(&mut self.rng)
 246:     }
 247: }
 248: 
 249: /// Describes what to do for one typo event.
 250: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
 251: pub enum TypoEvent {
 252:     /// Type `wrong` instead of `correct`, then (after `notice_after` more chars)
 253:     /// backspace back and retype correctly.
 254:     AdjacentSub {
 255:         wrong: char,
 256:         correct: char,
 257:         notice_after: u8,
 258:     },
 259:     /// Type `correct` then also type `extra`, then backspace `extra`.
 260:     ExtraChar {
 261:         correct: char,
 262:         extra: char,
 263:         #[allow(dead_code)]
 264:         notice_after: u8,
 265:     },
 266:     /// Press Enter unexpectedly, then immediately backspace it.
 267:     AccidentalEnter {
 268:         #[allow(dead_code)]
 269:         notice_after: u8,
 270:     },
 271:     /// Emit `second` then `first` (swapped), then backspace twice and retype both.
 272:     Transposition {
 273:         first: char,
 274:         second: char,
 275:     },
 276: }
 277: 
 278: #[cfg(test)]
 279: mod tests {
 280:     use super::*;
 281:     use crate::config::Config;
 282:     use std::time::Duration;
 283: 
 284:     fn default_cfg() -> Config {
 285:         Config::default()
 286:     }
 287: 
 288:     // ── seeded determinism ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
 289: 
 290:     #[test]
 291:     fn test_seeded_determinism() {
 292:         let cfg = default_cfg();
 293:         let input = "fn main() { }";
 294: 
 295:         let mut sim_a = HumanSimulator::new(&cfg, Some(42));
 296:         let mut sim_b = HumanSimulator::new(&cfg, Some(42));
 297: 
 298:         let chars: Vec<char> = input.chars().collect();
 299:         for i in 0..chars.len() {
 300:             let prev = if i == 0 { ' ' } else { chars[i - 1] };
 301:             let ch = chars[i];
 302:             let next = chars.get(i + 1).copied();
 303: 
 304:             let delay_a = sim_a.delay_for(prev, ch);
 305:             let delay_b = sim_b.delay_for(prev, ch);
 306:             assert_eq!(delay_a, delay_b, "delay mismatch at char {i} '{ch}'");
 307: 
 308:             // Compare maybe_typo outcomes by matching on the same discriminant.
 309:             // We can't easily compare TypoEvent by value without PartialEq, so
 310:             // we use a simple tag comparison via Debug output.
 311:             let typo_a = sim_a.maybe_typo(ch, next);
 312:             let typo_b = sim_b.maybe_typo(ch, next);
 313:             let tag = |opt: &Option<TypoEvent>| -> &'static str {
 314:                 match opt {
 315:                     None => "None",
 316:                     Some(TypoEvent::AdjacentSub { .. }) => "AdjacentSub",
 317:                     Some(TypoEvent::ExtraChar { .. }) => "ExtraChar",
 318:                     Some(TypoEvent::AccidentalEnter { .. }) => "AccidentalEnter",
 319:                     Some(TypoEvent::Transposition { .. }) => "Transposition",
 320:                 }
 321:             };
 322:             assert_eq!(tag(&typo_a), tag(&typo_b), "typo mismatch at char {i} '{ch}'");
 323:         }
 324:     }
 325: 
 326:     // ── no seed varies (smoke — just don't crash) ────────────────────────────
 327: 
 328:     #[test]
 329:     #[allow(clippy::assertions_on_constants)]
 330:     fn test_no_seed_varies() {
 331:         let cfg = default_cfg();
 332:         let mut sim_a = HumanSimulator::new(&cfg, None);
 333:         let mut sim_b = HumanSimulator::new(&cfg, None);
 334: 
 335:         let input = "fn main() { }";
 336:         let chars: Vec<char> = input.chars().collect();
 337:         for i in 0..chars.len() {
 338:             let prev = if i == 0 { ' ' } else { chars[i - 1] };
 339:             let ch = chars[i];
 340:             let next = chars.get(i + 1).copied();
 341:             let _ = sim_a.delay_for(prev, ch);
 342:             let _ = sim_b.delay_for(prev, ch);
 343:             let _ = sim_a.maybe_typo(ch, next);
 344:             let _ = sim_b.maybe_typo(ch, next);
 345:         }
 346:         // Test completes without panic — variability is probabilistic.
 347:         assert!(true);
 348:     }

 350:     // ── delay always positive ────────────────────────────────────────────────
 351: 
 352:     #[test]
 353:     fn test_delay_positive() {
 354:         let cfg = default_cfg();
 355:         let mut sim = HumanSimulator::new(&cfg, Some(7));
 356:         let chars: Vec<char> = "abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRS12345".chars().collect();
 357:         let mut prev = ' ';
 358:         for &ch in &chars {
 359:             let d = sim.delay_for(prev, ch);
 360:             assert!(d > Duration::ZERO, "expected positive delay for '{ch}', got {d:?}");
 361:             prev = ch;
 362:         }
 363:     }

 366: 
 367:     #[test]
 368:     fn test_delay_reasonable_upper() {
 369:         let cfg = default_cfg();
 370:         let mut sim = HumanSimulator::new(&cfg, Some(99));
 371:         let chars: Vec<char> = "fn main() { let x = 42; println!(\"{x}\"); }".chars().collect();
 372:         let mut prev = ' ';
 373:         for &ch in &chars {
 374:             let d = sim.delay_for(prev, ch);
 375:             assert!(
 376:                 d < Duration::from_secs(5),
 377:                 "delay unreasonably large: {d:?} for char '{ch}'"
 378:             );
 379:             prev = ch;
 380:         }

 383:     // ── think_pause_ms stays within range ────────────────────────────────────
 384: 
 385:     #[test]
 386:     fn test_think_pause_range() {
 387:         let cfg = default_cfg();
 388:         let mut sim = HumanSimulator::new(&cfg, Some(1));
 389:         for _ in 0..100 {
 390:             let ms = sim.think_pause_ms(300, 1200);
 391:             assert!(ms >= 300, "think_pause_ms below minimum: {ms}");
 392:             assert!(ms <= 1200, "think_pause_ms above maximum: {ms}");
 393:         }
