 396:     // ── AdjacentSub produces chars from QWERTY map ───────────────────────────
 397: 
 398:     #[test]
 399:     fn test_typo_adjacent_chars_only() {
 400:         let cfg = Config {
 401:             typing: crate::config::TypingConfig {
 402:                 typo_rate: 1.0, // force a typo every char
 403:                 ..Config::default().typing
 404:             },
 405:             ..Config::default()
 406:         };
 407: 
 408:         // Only test chars that are in the QWERTY map and have neighbours.
 409:         let test_chars = "asdfghjklqwertyuiopzxcvbnm";
 410:         let mut sim = HumanSimulator::new(&cfg, Some(123));
 411: 
 412:         let mut saw_adjacent_sub = false;
 413:         for ch in test_chars.chars() {
 414:             let neighbours = adjacent_keys(ch);
 415:             if neighbours.is_empty() {
 416:                 continue;
 417:             }
 418:             // Run many times to get an AdjacentSub event.
 419:             for _ in 0..20 {
 420:                 let mut trial = HumanSimulator::new(&cfg, Some(ch as u64 * 7 + 3));
 421:                 if let Some(TypoEvent::AdjacentSub { wrong, correct, .. }) =
 422:                     trial.maybe_typo(ch, Some('x'))
 423:                 {
 424:                     assert_eq!(correct, ch);
 425:                     // `wrong` must be in the adjacency map for `ch` (case-folded).
 426:                     let expected_neighbours = adjacent_keys(ch);
 427:                     let wrong_lower = wrong.to_lowercase().next().unwrap_or(wrong);
 428:                     assert!(
 429:                         expected_neighbours.contains(&wrong_lower),
 430:                         "wrong char '{wrong}' not in QWERTY neighbours of '{ch}': {expected_neighbours:?}"
 431:                     );
 432:                     saw_adjacent_sub = true;
 433:                     break;
 434:                 }
 435:             }
 436:         }
 437:         // We must have exercised at least one AdjacentSub event.
 438:         assert!(saw_adjacent_sub, "no AdjacentSub events produced in test");
 439:         let _ = sim;
 440:     }

 442:     // ── slow distribution tests ─────────────────────────────────────────────
 443: 
 444:     #[test]
 445:     #[ignore = "statistical — run with GHOSTWRITER_SLOW_TESTS=1"]
 446:     fn test_slow_distribution_delay() {
 447:         if std::env::var("GHOSTWRITER_SLOW_TESTS").is_err() {
 448:             return;
 449:         }
 450: 
 451:         let cfg = default_cfg();
 452:         // 60 WPM → ~200ms/char mean
 453:         let target_mean_ms = 200.0_f64;
 454: 
 455:         let input: String = "a".repeat(2000);
 456:         let chars: Vec<char> = input.chars().collect();
 457: 
 458:         let mut all_delays: Vec<u64> = Vec::new();
 459: 
 460:         for seed in 0..10u64 {
 461:             let mut sim = HumanSimulator::new(&cfg, Some(seed));
 462:             let mut prev = ' ';
 463:             for &ch in &chars {
 464:                 let d = sim.delay_for(prev, ch);
 465:                 all_delays.push(d.as_millis() as u64);
 466:                 assert!(d.as_millis() >= 10, "delay below 10ms minimum");
 467:                 prev = ch;
 468:             }
 469:         }
 470: 
 471:         let mean = all_delays.iter().sum::<u64>() as f64 / all_delays.len() as f64;
 472:         let deviation = ((mean - target_mean_ms) / target_mean_ms).abs();
 473:         // Allow 10× tolerance due to think pauses inflating mean; just no crash/negative.
 474:         assert!(deviation < 10.0, "mean {mean:.1}ms extremely far from {target_mean_ms}ms");
 475: 
 476:         assert!(
 477:             all_delays.iter().all(|&d| d >= 10),
 478:             "some delay < 10ms"
 479:         );
 480: 
 481:         // 95th percentile < 2000ms (ignoring think pauses: accept up to 10s).
 482:         let mut sorted = all_delays.clone();
 483:         sorted.sort_unstable();
 484:         let p95 = sorted[sorted.len() * 95 / 100];
 485:         assert!(p95 < 10_000, "95th percentile delay {p95}ms too large");
 486:     }
 487: 
 488:     #[test]
 489:     #[ignore = "statistical — run with GHOSTWRITER_SLOW_TESTS=1"]
 490:     fn test_slow_distribution_typo_rate() {
 491:         if std::env::var("GHOSTWRITER_SLOW_TESTS").is_err() {
 492:             return;
 493:         }
 494: 
 495:         let cfg = default_cfg(); // typo_rate = 0.008
 496:         let input: String = "a".repeat(2000);
 497:         let chars: Vec<char> = input.chars().collect();
 498: 
 499:         let mut total_chars = 0usize;
 500:         let mut total_typos = 0usize;
 501:         let mut adjacent_sub_count = 0usize;
 502: 
 503:         for seed in 0..10u64 {
 504:             let mut sim = HumanSimulator::new(&cfg, Some(seed));
 505:             for (i, &ch) in chars.iter().enumerate() {
 506:                 let next = chars.get(i + 1).copied();
 507:                 total_chars += 1;
 508:                 if let Some(event) = sim.maybe_typo(ch, next) {
 509:                     total_typos += 1;
 510:                     if matches!(event, TypoEvent::AdjacentSub { .. }) {
 511:                         adjacent_sub_count += 1;
 512:                     }
 513:                 }
 514:             }
 515:         }
 516: 
 517:         let rate = total_typos as f64 / total_chars as f64;
 518:         assert!(
 519:             (0.005..=0.012).contains(&rate),
 520:             "typo rate {rate:.4} outside expected [0.005, 0.012]"
 521:         );
 522: 
 523:         if total_typos > 0 {
 524:             let adjacent_frac = adjacent_sub_count as f64 / total_typos as f64;
 525:             assert!(
 526:                 (0.55..=0.65).contains(&adjacent_frac),
 527:                 "AdjacentSub fraction {adjacent_frac:.2} outside [0.55, 0.65]"
 528:             );
 529:         }
 530:     }
 531: }

===== /src/typer/input.rs =====

   1: //! Low-level input emission via `enigo`.
   2: //!
   3: //! Wraps the cross-platform keyboard simulation so the rest of the codebase
   4: //! stays independent of `enigo` internals.
   5: 
   6: use crate::error::GhostError;
   7: use anyhow::Result;
   8: use enigo::{Enigo, Key, Keyboard, Settings};
   9: 
  10: pub struct InputEmitter {
  11:     enigo: Enigo,
  12: }
  13: 
  14: impl InputEmitter {
  15:     pub fn new() -> Result<Self> {
  16:         let enigo = Enigo::new(&Settings::default())
  17:             .map_err(|e| GhostError::Input(e.to_string()))?;
  18:         Ok(Self { enigo })
  19:     }
  20: 
  21:     /// Type a single Unicode character.
  22:     pub fn type_char(&mut self, ch: char) -> Result<()> {
  23:         self.enigo
  24:             .text(&ch.to_string())
  25:             .map_err(|e| GhostError::Input(format!("{:?}", e)))?;
  26:         Ok(())
  27:     }
  28: 
  29:     /// Type a string of text at once.
  30:     pub fn type_str(&mut self, s: &str) -> Result<()> {
  31:         self.enigo
  32:             .text(s)
  33:             .map_err(|e| GhostError::Input(format!("{:?}", e)))?;
  34:         Ok(())
  35:     }
  36: 
  37:     /// Press Backspace once.
  38:     pub fn backspace(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
  39:         self.enigo
  40:             .key(Key::Backspace, enigo::Direction::Click)
  41:             .map_err(|e| GhostError::Input(format!("{:?}", e)))?;
  42:         Ok(())
  43:     }
  44: 
  45:     /// Press and release a single key.
  46:     pub fn press(&mut self, key: Key) -> Result<()> {
  47:         self.enigo
  48:             .key(key, enigo::Direction::Click)
  49:             .map_err(|e| GhostError::Input(format!("{:?}", e)))?;
  50:         Ok(())
  51:     }
  52: 
  53:     /// Press modifier+key and release both.
  54:     pub fn chord(&mut self, modifier: Key, key: Key) -> Result<()> {
  55:         self.enigo.key(modifier, enigo::Direction::Press)
  56:             .map_err(|e| GhostError::Input(format!("{:?}", e)))?;
  57:         self.enigo.key(key, enigo::Direction::Click)
  58:             .map_err(|e| GhostError::Input(format!("{:?}", e)))?;
  59:         self.enigo.key(modifier, enigo::Direction::Release)
  60:             .map_err(|e| GhostError::Input(format!("{:?}", e)))?;
  61:         Ok(())
  62:     }
  63: 
  64:     /// Press mod1+mod2+key and release all (e.g. Ctrl+Shift+P).
  65:     pub fn chord2(&mut self, mod1: Key, mod2: Key, key: Key) -> Result<()> {
  66:         self.enigo.key(mod1, enigo::Direction::Press)
  67:             .map_err(|e| GhostError::Input(format!("{:?}", e)))?;
  68:         self.enigo.key(mod2, enigo::Direction::Press)
  69:             .map_err(|e| GhostError::Input(format!("{:?}", e)))?;
  70:         self.enigo.key(key, enigo::Direction::Click)
  71:             .map_err(|e| GhostError::Input(format!("{:?}", e)))?;
  72:         self.enigo.key(mod2, enigo::Direction::Release)
  73:             .map_err(|e| GhostError::Input(format!("{:?}", e)))?;
  74:         self.enigo.key(mod1, enigo::Direction::Release)
  75:             .map_err(|e| GhostError::Input(format!("{:?}", e)))?;
  76:         Ok(())
  77:     }
  78: 
  79:     /// Save the current file (Cmd+S on macOS, Ctrl+S elsewhere).
  80:     pub fn save(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
  81:         #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
  82:         let modifier = Key::Meta;
  83:         #[cfg(not(target_os = "macos"))]
  84:         let modifier = Key::Control;
  85: 
  86:         self.enigo
  87:             .key(modifier, enigo::Direction::Press)
  88:             .ok();
  89:         self.enigo
  90:             .key(Key::Unicode('s'), enigo::Direction::Click)
  91:             .ok();
  92:         self.enigo
  93:             .key(modifier, enigo::Direction::Release)
  94:             .ok();
  95:         Ok(())
  96:     }
  97: }

===== /src/typer/vscode.rs =====

   1: //! VSCode orchestration.
   2: //!
   3: //! Uses the `code` CLI to open the workspace and individual files, then
   4: //! platform-specific APIs to bring the window to the front before typing.
   5: 
   6: use crate::error::GhostError;
   7: use anyhow::Result;
   8: use std::path::Path;
   9: use std::process::Command;
  10: use tracing::info;
  11: 
  12: /// Open VSCode at `dest`, waiting for it to be ready.
  13: pub fn open(dest: &Path) -> Result<()> {
  14:     which_code()?;
  15: 
  16:     info!("opening VSCode at {}", dest.display());
  17:     Command::new("code")
  18:         .arg(dest)
  19:         .spawn()
  20:         .map_err(|e| GhostError::Input(e.to_string()))?;
  21: 
  22:     Ok(())
  23: }
  24: 
  25: /// Open a specific file in the already-running VSCode instance.
  26: pub fn open_file(file: &Path) -> Result<()> {
  27:     Command::new("code")
  28:         .args(["--goto", &file.to_string_lossy()])
  29:         .spawn()
  30:         .map_err(|e| GhostError::Input(e.to_string()))?;
  31:     Ok(())
  32: }
  33: 
  34: /// Bring the VSCode window to the foreground (platform-specific).
  35: pub fn focus() -> Result<()> {
  36:     #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
  37:     {
  38:         let script = r#"tell application "Visual Studio Code" to activate"#;
  39:         Command::new("osascript")
  40:             .args(["-e", script])
  41:             .output()
  42:             .map_err(|e| GhostError::WindowFocus(e.to_string()))?;
  43:     }
  44: 
  45:     #[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
  46:     {
  47:         // Requires wmctrl: `sudo apt install wmctrl`
  48:         Command::new("wmctrl")
  49:             .args(["-a", "Visual Studio Code"])
  50:             .output()
  51:             .map_err(|e| GhostError::WindowFocus(e.to_string()))?;
  52:     }
  53: 
  54:     #[cfg(target_os = "windows")]
  55:     {
  56:         // VSCode window title varies: "file.rs — project — Visual Studio Code".
  57:         // FindWindow requires an exact match so we enumerate via Get-Process instead.
  58:         let script = r#"
  59:             Add-Type -TypeDefinition @'
  60:                 using System.Runtime.InteropServices;
  61:                 public class Win32 {
  62:                     [DllImport("user32.dll")]
  63:                     public static extern bool SetForegroundWindow(IntPtr hWnd);
  64:                 }
  65: '@
  66:             $proc = Get-Process -Name "Code" -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
  67:                     Where-Object { $_.MainWindowTitle -like "*Visual Studio Code*" } |
  68:                     Select-Object -First 1
  69:             if ($proc) { [Win32]::SetForegroundWindow($proc.MainWindowHandle) }
  70:         "#;
  71:         Command::new("powershell")
  72:             .args(["-Command", script])
  73:             .output()
  74:             .map_err(|e| GhostError::WindowFocus(e.to_string()))?;
  75:     }
  76: 
  77:     Ok(())
  78: }
  79: 
  80: fn which_code() -> Result<()> {
  81:     #[cfg(target_os = "windows")]
  82:     let cmd = "where";
  83:     #[cfg(not(target_os = "windows"))]
  84:     let cmd = "which";
  85: 
  86:     Command::new(cmd)
  87:         .arg("code")
  88:         .output()
  89:         .map_err(|_| GhostError::VscodeNotFound)?
  90:         .status
  91:         .success()
  92:         .then_some(())
  93:         .ok_or(GhostError::VscodeNotFound.into())
  94: }

===== /tests/bootstrap_error_test.rs =====

   1: //! `BootstrapError` Display-trait stability tests.
   2: //!
   3: //! `BootstrapError::print_fatal()` writes a labelled error box to stderr.
   4: //! The box content is derived from the `Display` impl that thiserror
   5: //! generates. Capturing stderr from inside a process is awkward, so we
   6: //! exercise the underlying `Display` strings directly — every variant must
   7: //! mention its step number ("1/9", "2/9" …) and include actionable
   8: //! guidance plus (where relevant) a diagnostic-screenshot path.
   9: //!
  10: //! These assertions are tight enough to catch accidental message rewording
  11: //! that would lose actionable detail, but loose enough not to constrain
  12: //! cosmetic phrasing.
  13: 
  14: use ghostwriter::navigator::bootstrap_error::{BootstrapError, STEPS};
  15: use std::path::PathBuf;
  16: 
  17: fn diag() -> PathBuf {
  18:     PathBuf::from("/tmp/diag.png")
  19: }
  20: 
  21: // ─── STEPS constant is stable + indexed correctly ───────────────────────────
  22: 
  23: #[test]
  24: fn steps_constant_has_nine_entries() {
  25:     assert_eq!(STEPS.len(), 9, "the 9-step bootstrap must have 9 step labels");
  26:     for (i, label) in STEPS.iter().enumerate() {
  27:         assert!(label.starts_with(&format!("{}/9", i + 1)),
  28:                 "STEPS[{i}] should start with '{}/9', got {label:?}", i + 1);
  29:     }
  30: }
  31: 
  32: // ─── Each variant's Display includes its step number ───────────────────────
  33: 
  34: #[test]
  35: fn vscode_cli_missing_mentions_step_one() {
  36:     let s = BootstrapError::VscodeCliMissing.to_string();
  37:     assert!(s.contains("1/9"), "step number missing: {s}");
  38:     assert!(s.contains("PATH"), "actionable guidance (PATH) missing: {s}");
  39: }
  40: 
  41: #[test]
  42: fn screen_capture_permission_denied_mentions_screen_recording() {
  43:     let s = BootstrapError::ScreenCapturePermissionDenied.to_string();
  44:     assert!(s.contains("1/9"));
  45:     assert!(s.contains("Screen Recording") || s.contains("screen capture"),
  46:             "screen recording guidance missing: {s}");
  47: }
  48: 
  49: #[test]
  50: fn input_injection_permission_denied_mentions_accessibility() {
  51:     let s = BootstrapError::InputInjectionPermissionDenied.to_string();
  52:     assert!(s.contains("1/9"));
  53:     assert!(s.contains("Accessibility") || s.contains("accessibility"));
  54: }
  55: 
  56: #[test]
  57: fn vscode_not_visible_mentions_step_two_and_diag_path() {
  58:     let err = BootstrapError::VscodeNotVisible {
  59:         waited_ms: 6000,
  60:         path: PathBuf::from("/some/repo"),
  61:         diag_path: diag(),
  62:     };
  63:     let s = err.to_string();
  64:     assert!(s.contains("2/9"));
  65:     assert!(s.contains("6000"), "waited_ms must be interpolated: {s}");
  66:     assert!(s.contains("/some/repo"));
  67:     assert!(s.contains("/tmp/diag.png"), "diag path missing: {s}");
  68: }
  69: 
  70: #[test]
  71: fn fullscreen_failed_includes_window_and_display_areas() {
  72:     let err = BootstrapError::FullscreenFailed {
  73:         window_area: 100,
  74:         display_area: 1000,
  75:         diag_path: diag(),
  76:     };
  77:     let s = err.to_string();
  78:     assert!(s.contains("3/9"));
  79:     assert!(s.contains("100"));
  80:     assert!(s.contains("1000"));
  81:     assert!(s.contains("/tmp/diag.png"));
  82: }
  83: 
  84: #[test]
  85: fn explorer_not_open_mentions_step_five_and_attempts() {
  86:     let err = BootstrapError::ExplorerNotOpen { attempts: 3, diag_path: diag() };
  87:     let s = err.to_string();
  88:     assert!(s.contains("5/9"));
  89:     assert!(s.contains("3"), "attempts count missing: {s}");
  90: }
  91: 
  92: #[test]
  93: fn sidebar_bounds_not_found_mentions_step_six() {

  95:         scan_width: 600, diag_path: diag(),
  96:     };
  97:     let s = err.to_string();
  98:     assert!(s.contains("6/9"));
  99:     assert!(s.contains("600"));
 100: }

 179: // ─── print_fatal smoke ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 180: 
 181: /// `print_fatal` writes to stderr; capturing it from a unit test is fragile.
 182: /// We just verify it doesn't panic across every variant.
 183: #[test]
 184: fn print_fatal_does_not_panic_for_any_variant() {
 185:     let variants: Vec<BootstrapError> = vec![
 186:         BootstrapError::VscodeCliMissing,
 187:         BootstrapError::ScreenCapturePermissionDenied,
 188:         BootstrapError::InputInjectionPermissionDenied,
 189:         BootstrapError::WmctrlMissing,
 190:         BootstrapError::VscodeNotVisible {
 191:             waited_ms: 1, path: PathBuf::from("/x"), diag_path: diag(),
 192:         },
 193:         BootstrapError::FullscreenFailed {
 194:             window_area: 0, display_area: 1, diag_path: diag(),
 195:         },
 196:         BootstrapError::ExplorerNotOpen { attempts: 1, diag_path: diag() },
 197:         BootstrapError::SidebarBoundsNotFound { scan_width: 0, diag_path: diag() },
 198:         BootstrapError::SidebarEmpty { x: 0, width: 0, diag_path: diag() },
 199:         BootstrapError::ExpandAllFailed { diag_path: diag() },
 200:         BootstrapError::TooFewTemplates {
 201:             captured: 0, total: 0, min_required: 0, min_pct: 0,
 202:             failed_list: "".into(), diag_dir: diag(),
 203:         },
 204:         BootstrapError::VerificationFailed {
 205:             failures: 0, sample_size: 0, threshold: 0.0,
 206:             failed_list: "".into(), diag_dir: diag(),
 207:         },
 208:         BootstrapError::DictionarySaveFailed {
 209:             path: diag(), reason: "x".into(),
 210:         },
 211:         BootstrapError::Io {
 212:             step: "x".into(),
 213:             source: std::io::Error::new(std::io::ErrorKind::Other, "x"),
 214:         },
 215:     ];
 216: 
 217:     // 14 variants matches the "14 typed error variants" claim in CLAUDE.md.
 218:     assert_eq!(variants.len(), 14);
 219: 
 220:     for v in &variants {
 221:         v.print_fatal(); // panics if any variant's format string mismatches
 222:     }
 223: }

===== /tests/cli_smoke_test.rs =====

   1: //! End-to-end CLI smoke tests.
   2: //!
   3: //! These tests spawn the actual `ghostwriter` binary via
   4: //! `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_ghostwriter")` (Cargo provides this path automatically
   5: //! for integration tests) and assert on stdout / exit code. They exercise
   6: //! the full chain — clap parsing → `pick_orchestration_mode` →
   7: //! `repo::fetch` (local-path branch) → orchestrate / commit_planner →
   8: //! dry-run print loop — without touching VSCode, the screen, or the network.
   9: //!
  10: //! All tests use `--dry-run` so playback never starts. Tests that exercise
  11: //! AI mode point `GHOSTWRITER_LLM_BASE_URL` at a wiremock instance.
  12: //!
  13: //! These are gated as `#[serial]` because they all manipulate the same
  14: //! `GHOSTWRITER_LLM_BASE_URL` env var.
  15: 
  16: mod helpers;
  17: 
  18: use helpers::{git_helpers, mock_api};
  19: use serial_test::serial;
  20: use std::process::Command;
  21: 
  22: /// Path to the compiled `ghostwriter` binary. Cargo populates this for any
  23: /// integration test under `tests/`.
  24: const BIN: &str = env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_ghostwriter");
  25: 
  26: // ─── Shared setup ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  27: 
  28: /// Build a git tempdir with three small commits — enough to satisfy
  29: /// `assess_history`'s `MIN_COMMITS = 3` threshold so commit-mode auto-detect
  30: /// kicks in, but small enough not to trip the `MAX_AVG_FILES = 20` ceiling.
  31: fn build_three_commit_repo() -> tempfile::TempDir {
  32:     let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().expect("tempdir");
  33:     git_helpers::build_repo(tmp.path(), &[
  34:         ("init",      vec![("Cargo.toml", "[package]\nname=\"smoke\"\n")]),
  35:         ("add main",  vec![("src/main.rs", "fn main(){}\n")]),
  36:         ("expand",    vec![("src/main.rs", "fn main(){ println!(\"hi\"); }\n")]),
  37:     ]);
  38:     tmp
  39: }
  40: 
  41: // ─── 1. AI mode dry-run ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  42: 
  43: /// `ghostwriter run --dry-run --no-commits` against the tiny_repo fixture
  44: /// must (a) exit 0, (b) print at least one stage line, (c) not require
  45: /// VSCode. The mock API serves canned LLM responses.
  46: #[tokio::test]
  47: #[serial]
  48: async fn cli_run_dry_run_ai_mode_prints_stage_plan() {
  49:     let mock = mock_api::MockApiServer::start().await;
  50: 
  51:     // Use the same fixture-copy helper integration_test uses.
  52:     let (_dir, repo) = helpers::fixture_repo::copy_tiny_repo();
  53:     let dest = tempfile::tempdir().expect("dest tempdir");
  54: 
  55:     let output = Command::new(BIN)
  56:         .env("GHOSTWRITER_LLM_BASE_URL", mock.base_url())
  57:         .env("ANTHROPIC_API_KEY", "test-key")
  58:         // Quieten tracing during the assertions for cleaner failure output.
  59:         .env("GHOSTWRITER_LOG", "ghostwriter=warn")
  60:         .args([
  61:             "run",
  62:             "--repo", repo.to_str().unwrap(),
  63:             "--dest", dest.path().to_str().unwrap(),
  64:             "--no-commits",
  65:             "--no-cache",  // skip writing cache (parent .ghostwriter/ doesn't exist)
  66:             "--dry-run",
  67:         ])
  68:         .output()
  69:         .expect("spawn ghostwriter");
  70: 
  71:     let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
  72:     let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
  73: 
  74:     assert!(
  75:         output.status.success(),
  76:         "ghostwriter exited {:?}\n--- stdout ---\n{stdout}\n--- stderr ---\n{stderr}",
  77:         output.status.code()
  78:     );
  79:     assert!(
  80:         stdout.contains("Stage plan"),
  81:         "expected 'Stage plan' header in stdout, got:\n{stdout}"
  82:     );
  83:     // The fixture has multiple stages; require at least one numbered entry.
  84:     assert!(
  85:         stdout.contains("1."),
  86:         "expected at least one numbered stage line in stdout, got:\n{stdout}"
  87:     );
  88: }

  90: // ─── 2. Commit mode dry-run ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
  91: 
  92: /// `ghostwriter run --dry-run --from-commits` against a built git tempdir
  93: /// must exit 0 and print one stage per commit. AI key is intentionally NOT
  94: /// set — commit mode must not require it.
  95: #[tokio::test]
  96: #[serial]
  97: async fn cli_run_dry_run_commit_mode_prints_one_stage_per_commit() {
  98:     let repo = build_three_commit_repo();
  99:     let dest = tempfile::tempdir().expect("dest tempdir");
 100: 
 101:     let output = Command::new(BIN)
 102:         .env("GHOSTWRITER_LOG", "ghostwriter=warn")
 103:         // Deliberately don't set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY — commit mode must not need it.
 104:         .env_remove("ANTHROPIC_API_KEY")
 105:         .env_remove("GHOSTWRITER_LLM_BASE_URL")
 106:         .args([
 107:             "run",
 108:             "--repo", repo.path().to_str().unwrap(),
 109:             "--dest", dest.path().to_str().unwrap(),
 110:             "--from-commits",
 111:             "--dry-run",
 112:         ])
 113:         .output()
 114:         .expect("spawn ghostwriter");
 115: 
 116:     let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
 117:     let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
 118: 
 119:     assert!(
 120:         output.status.success(),
 121:         "ghostwriter exited {:?}\nstdout:\n{stdout}\nstderr:\n{stderr}",
 122:         output.status.code()
 123:     );
 124: 
 125:     // Three commits → three stages (commit_planner emits one stage per commit).
 126:     let stage_lines: Vec<&str> = stdout
 127:         .lines()
 128:         .filter(|l| l.trim_start().starts_with(|c: char| c.is_ascii_digit()) && l.contains('.'))
 129:         .collect();
 130:     assert_eq!(
 131:         stage_lines.len(), 3,
 132:         "expected 3 stage lines, got {} — full stdout:\n{stdout}",
 133:         stage_lines.len()
 134:     );
 135: }

===== /tests/commit_replay_test.rs =====

   1: //! Integration tests for the commit-replay path.
   2: //!
   3: //! Builds real git repos in tempdirs and exercises:
   4: //!   - `repo::history::assess_history` — auto-detect thresholds
   5: //!   - `repo::history::load_history` — full content extraction per commit
   6: //!   - end-to-end through `orchestrator::commit_planner::plan_from_commits`
   7: 
   8: use ghostwriter::orchestrator::commit_planner;
   9: use ghostwriter::orchestrator::script::Action;
  10: use ghostwriter::repo::history::{self, ChangeKind};
  11: 
  12: use git2::{IndexAddOption, Repository, Signature, Time};
  13: use std::fs;
  14: use std::path::Path;
  15: use tempfile::TempDir;
  16: 
  17: /// Build a git repo in `dir`. Each entry is `(commit_msg, [(rel_path, content)])`.
  18: /// An empty `content` means "delete this file" for that commit.
  19: ///
  20: /// Each commit gets a strictly-increasing timestamp so revwalk ordering is
  21: /// unambiguous (without this, three commits in the same second can return
  22: /// in arbitrary order under `Sort::TIME`).
  23: fn build_repo(dir: &Path, commits: &[(&str, Vec<(&str, &str)>)]) {
  24:     let repo = Repository::init(dir).expect("git init");
  25:     let mut parent: Option<git2::Oid> = None;
  26:     // Start in 2024 so we never collide with the system clock.
  27:     let mut epoch: i64 = 1_700_000_000;
  28: 
  29:     for (msg, files) in commits {
  30:         for (rel, content) in files {
  31:             let full = dir.join(rel);
  32:             if let Some(p) = full.parent() {
  33:                 fs::create_dir_all(p).expect("mkdir parent");
  34:             }
  35:             if content.is_empty() {
  36:                 let _ = fs::remove_file(&full);
  37:             } else {
  38:                 fs::write(&full, content).expect("write file");
  39:             }
  40:         }
  41: 
  42:         let mut idx = repo.index().expect("index");
  43:         idx.add_all(["*"].iter(), IndexAddOption::DEFAULT, None).expect("add_all");
  44:         idx.update_all(["*"].iter(), None).expect("update_all");
  45:         idx.write().expect("write index");
  46:         let tree_oid = idx.write_tree().expect("write_tree");
  47:         let tree = repo.find_tree(tree_oid).expect("find_tree");
  48: 
  49:         let sig = Signature::new("Tester", "tester@example.com", &Time::new(epoch, 0))
  50:             .expect("sig");
  51:         epoch += 60; // bump by a minute per commit
  52: 
  53:         let parents: Vec<git2::Commit> = parent
  54:             .iter()
  55:             .filter_map(|oid| repo.find_commit(*oid).ok())
  56:             .collect();
  57:         let parents_ref: Vec<&git2::Commit> = parents.iter().collect();
  58: 
  59:         let oid = repo
  60:             .commit(Some("HEAD"), &sig, &sig, msg, &tree, &parents_ref)
  61:             .expect("commit");
  62:         parent = Some(oid);
  63:     }
  64: }
  65: 
  66: // ── assess_history ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  67: 
  68: #[test]
  69: fn test_assess_empty_repo_not_usable() {
  70:     let dir = TempDir::new().unwrap();
  71:     Repository::init(dir.path()).unwrap();
  72:     let q = history::assess_history(dir.path()).expect("assess");
  73:     assert_eq!(q.commit_count, 0);
  74:     assert!(!q.is_usable);
  75:     assert!(q.reason.contains("HEAD") || q.reason.contains("commits"),
  76:             "reason should mention empty/no-HEAD: {:?}", q.reason);
  77: }

  84:     ]);

  87:     assert!(!q.is_usable, "1 commit must not be usable: {:?}", q.reason);
  88: }

  94:         ("init",     vec![("a.rs", "fn a(){}\n")]),
  95:         ("add main", vec![("main.rs", "fn main(){}\n")]),
  96:     ]);
  97:     let q = history::assess_history(dir.path()).expect("assess");
  98:     assert_eq!(q.commit_count, 2);
  99:     assert!(!q.is_usable, "2 commits must not be usable: {:?}", q.reason);

 102: #[test]
 103: fn test_assess_three_small_commits_is_usable() {
 104:     let dir = TempDir::new().unwrap();
 105:     build_repo(dir.path(), &[
 106:         ("init",   vec![("a.rs", "1\n")]),
 107:         ("add b",  vec![("b.rs", "2\n")]),
 108:         ("add c",  vec![("c.rs", "3\n")]),
 109:     ]);
 110:     let q = history::assess_history(dir.path()).expect("assess");
 111:     assert_eq!(q.commit_count, 3);
 112:     assert!(q.is_usable, "3 small commits should be usable: {:?}", q.reason);
 113:     assert!((q.avg_files_per_commit - 1.0).abs() < 0.01,
 114:             "avg should be ~1 file/commit, got {}", q.avg_files_per_commit);
 115: }

 117: #[test]
 118: fn test_assess_huge_initial_commit_not_usable() {
 119:     // 3 commits where the first dumps 80 files — pushes the average above the
 120:     // 20-files/commit threshold, so commit replay would be too coarse.
 121:     let mut huge_first: Vec<(&str, &str)> = (0..80)
 122:         .map(|i| (Box::leak(format!("file_{i}.rs").into_boxed_str()) as &str, "x\n"))
 123:         .collect();
 124:     huge_first.push(("README.md", "hello\n"));
 125: 
 126:     let dir = TempDir::new().unwrap();
 127:     build_repo(dir.path(), &[
 128:         ("massive initial", huge_first),
 129:         ("tweak",  vec![("README.md", "hello world\n")]),
 130:         ("tweak2", vec![("README.md", "hello world!\n")]),
 131:     ]);
 132:     let q = history::assess_history(dir.path()).expect("assess");
 133:     assert_eq!(q.commit_count, 3);
 134:     assert!(!q.is_usable, "huge initial commit should fail threshold: {:?}", q.reason);
 135:     assert!(q.avg_files_per_commit > 20.0,
 136:             "avg must exceed 20-files/commit threshold, got {}", q.avg_files_per_commit);
 137: }

 141: #[test]
 142: fn test_load_history_orders_oldest_to_newest() {

 176:     ]);

 200: #[test]
 201: fn test_load_history_short_hash_is_seven_chars() {
 202:     let dir = TempDir::new().unwrap();
 203:     build_repo(dir.path(), &[("init", vec![("a", "x")])]);
 204:     let commits = history::load_history(dir.path()).expect("load");
 205:     // Current `load_history` truncates to 7-char short hashes.
 206:     assert_eq!(commits[0].hash.len(), 7,
 207:                "hash should be 7 chars (short SHA), got {:?}", commits[0].hash);
 208: }

 210: // ── End-to-end: real git history → DevelopmentScript ─────────────────────────
 211: 
 212: #[test]
 213: fn test_e2e_real_history_to_script() {
 214:     let dir = TempDir::new().unwrap();
 215:     build_repo(dir.path(), &[
 216:         ("init project",  vec![("Cargo.toml", "[package]\nname=\"x\"\n")]),
 217:         ("add main",      vec![("src/main.rs", "fn main(){}\n")]),
 218:         ("expand main",   vec![("src/main.rs", "fn main(){ println!(\"hi\"); }\n")]),
 219:         ("drop lockfile", vec![("Cargo.toml", "")]), // simulate deletion
 220:     ]);
 221: 
 222:     let q = history::assess_history(dir.path()).expect("assess");
 223:     assert!(q.is_usable, "history should be usable: {:?}", q.reason);
 224: 
 225:     let commits = history::load_history(dir.path()).expect("load");
 226:     assert_eq!(commits.len(), 4);
 227: 
 228:     let script = commit_planner::plan_from_commits(&commits, "proj");
 229:     assert_eq!(script.stages.len(), 4);
 230: 
 231:     // Stage 4 must contain a DeleteFile.
 232:     let stage4 = &script.stages[3].actions;
 233:     assert!(stage4.iter().any(|a| matches!(a, Action::DeleteFile { .. })),
 234:             "stage 4 should contain DeleteFile: {stage4:?}");
 235: 
 236:     // Stage 3 (modify main) must contain MoveCursor + EditFile, not CreateFile.
 237:     let stage3 = &script.stages[2].actions;
 238:     assert!(stage3.iter().any(|a| matches!(a, Action::EditFile { .. })));
 239:     assert!(stage3.iter().all(|a| !matches!(a, Action::CreateFile { .. })));
 240: 
 241:     // Every stage description must include the commit message AND be prefixed
 242:     // with the short hash (current `commit_planner` format: `"[hash] msg"`).
 243:     for (s, c) in script.stages.iter().zip(commits.iter()) {
 244:         assert!(s.description.contains(&c.message),
 245:                 "stage description {:?} should contain commit msg {:?}", s.description, c.message);
 246:         assert!(s.description.contains(&c.hash),
 247:                 "stage description {:?} should contain short hash {:?}", s.description, c.hash);
 248:     }
 249: }
 250: 
 251: // ─── Commit-mode golden file (mirror of test_golden_tiny_repo for AI mode) ──
 252: 
 253: /// Build a repo with a fixed sequence of commits, run it through the
 254: /// commit-mode pipeline, and snapshot the result. The whole point: catch
 255: /// silent shape regressions in commit_planner output the same way
 256: /// test_golden_tiny_repo does for AI mode.
 257: ///
 258: /// The deterministic commit timestamps in `build_repo` mean the only place
 259: /// non-determinism leaks in is `commit.hash` (7-char SHA depends on tree
 260: /// content + author + parent). To keep the golden stable we strip the
 261: /// `[hash] ` prefix from each stage description before snapshotting, and
 262: /// blank `repo_summary` since it includes the commit count which is
 263: /// already implied by stage count.
 264: ///
 265: /// Regenerate with: `UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 cargo test --test commit_replay_test test_golden_commit_mode`
 266: #[test]
 267: fn test_golden_commit_mode() {
 268:     let dir = TempDir::new().unwrap();
 269:     build_repo(dir.path(), &[
 270:         ("init project",
 271:             vec![("Cargo.toml", "[package]\nname=\"x\"\nversion=\"0.1.0\"\n")]),
 272:         ("add main",
 273:             vec![("src/main.rs", "fn main(){}\n")]),
 274:         ("expand main",
 275:             vec![("src/main.rs", "fn main(){ println!(\"hi\"); }\n")]),
 276:         ("drop lockfile",
 277:             vec![("Cargo.toml", "")]),
 278:     ]);
 279: 
 280:     let commits = history::load_history(dir.path()).expect("load");
 281:     let mut script = commit_planner::plan_from_commits(&commits, "tiny_commits");
 282: 
 283:     // Strip the non-deterministic `[hash] ` prefix from each stage description
 284:     // so the golden is reproducible across machines.
 285:     for stage in &mut script.stages {
 286:         if let Some(close) = stage.description.find(']') {
 287:             // Format is "[hash] message"; keep everything after "] ".
 288:             stage.description = stage.description[close + 1..].trim_start().to_string();
 289:         }
 290:     }
 291:     // Blank repo_summary too (depends on commit count format text).
 292:     script.repo_summary.clear();
 293: 
 294:     let actual = serde_json::to_string_pretty(&script).expect("serialise");
 295: 
 296:     let golden_path = std::path::Path::new(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"))
 297:         .join("tests")
 298:         .join("golden")
 299:         .join("tiny_commits_script.json");
 300: 
 301:     if std::env::var("UPDATE_GOLDEN").is_ok() {
 302:         std::fs::create_dir_all(golden_path.parent().unwrap()).unwrap();
 303:         std::fs::write(&golden_path, &actual).expect("write golden");
 304:         return;
 305:     }
 306: 
 307:     let expected = std::fs::read_to_string(&golden_path).unwrap_or_else(|_| {
 308:         panic!(
 309:             "golden file missing at {} — regenerate with: \
 310:              UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 cargo test --test commit_replay_test test_golden_commit_mode",
 311:             golden_path.display()
 312:         );
 313:     });
 314: 
 315:     assert_eq!(actual, expected, "commit-mode script does not match golden");
 316: }

===== /tests/dictionary_test.rs =====

   1: //! Integration tests for `VisualDictionary` persistence.
   2: //!
   3: //! Bootstrap captures templates and saves a dictionary to
   4: //! `.ghostwriter/dictionary.json` + `.ghostwriter/templates/*.png`. Subsequent
   5: //! `Navigator::bootstrap()` runs short-circuit by loading that dictionary.
   6: //! These tests exercise that disk round-trip end-to-end without ever
   7: //! starting VSCode.
   8: 
   9: use ghostwriter::navigator::dictionary::VisualDictionary;
  10: use ghostwriter::navigator::screen::Rect;
  11: use image::{DynamicImage, Rgba, RgbaImage};
  12: use std::path::PathBuf;
  13: use tempfile::TempDir;
  14: 
  15: fn synth_template(seed: u32) -> DynamicImage {
  16:     // Tiny deterministic 20×8 grey-shifted pattern keyed off `seed` so each
  17:     // template's PNG bytes differ — proves the disk round-trip preserves
  18:     // image content, not just metadata.
  19:     let mut img = RgbaImage::new(20, 8);
  20:     for (i, p) in img.pixels_mut().enumerate() {
  21:         let v = ((i as u32 + seed * 17) % 200 + 30) as u8;
  22:         *p = Rgba([v, v, v, 255]);
  23:     }
  24:     DynamicImage::ImageRgba8(img)
  25: }
  26: 
  27: fn images_equal(a: &DynamicImage, b: &DynamicImage) -> bool {
  28:     let a = a.to_rgba8();
  29:     let b = b.to_rgba8();
  30:     a.dimensions() == b.dimensions() && a.into_raw() == b.into_raw()
  31: }
  32: 
  33: #[test]
  34: fn empty_dictionary_load_returns_none_when_no_file() {
  35:     let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
  36:     let result = VisualDictionary::load(tmp.path()).expect("load empty");
  37:     assert!(result.is_none(),
  38:             "load on a directory with no .ghostwriter/dictionary.json must return None");
  39: }
  40: 
  41: #[test]
  42: fn round_trip_preserves_sidebar_region_and_row_height() {
  43:     let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
  44:     let mut dict = VisualDictionary::new(tmp.path(), Rect::new(48, 0, 260, 1000));
  45:     // Bump row_height_px from default before saving — load should restore it.
  46:     dict.row_height_px = 24;
  47:     dict.save().unwrap();
  48: 
  49:     let loaded = VisualDictionary::load(tmp.path()).expect("load").expect("Some");
  50:     assert_eq!(loaded.sidebar_region, Rect::new(48, 0, 260, 1000));
  51:     assert_eq!(loaded.row_height_px, 24);
  52: }
  53: 
  54: #[test]
  55: fn round_trip_preserves_inserted_entries_byte_identical() {
  56:     let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
  57:     let mut dict = VisualDictionary::new(tmp.path(), Rect::new(48, 0, 260, 1000));
  58: 
  59:     // Insert three different templates at different y coords.
  60:     let entries: Vec<(PathBuf, DynamicImage, u32)> = vec![
  61:         (PathBuf::from("src/main.rs"),    synth_template(1), 100),
  62:         (PathBuf::from("src/lib.rs"),     synth_template(2), 130),
  63:         (PathBuf::from("src/helpers.rs"), synth_template(3), 160),
  64:     ];
  65:     for (rel, tmpl, y) in &entries {
  66:         dict.insert(rel, tmpl.clone(), Some(*y)).unwrap();
  67:     }
  68:     dict.save().unwrap();
  69:     drop(dict); // ensure no in-memory cache leaks influence the load
  70: 
  71:     let loaded = VisualDictionary::load(tmp.path()).expect("load").expect("Some");
  72:     assert_eq!(loaded.len(), 3, "all 3 entries should round-trip");
  73: 
  74:     for (rel, original_template, y) in &entries {
  75:         let entry = loaded.get(rel).unwrap_or_else(|| {
  76:             panic!("entry for {} missing after reload", rel.display())
  77:         });
  78:         assert!(images_equal(&entry.template, original_template),
  79:                 "template for {} differs after round-trip", rel.display());
  80:         assert_eq!(entry.meta.last_known_y, Some(*y));
  81:     }
  82: }
  83: 
  84: #[test]
  85: fn save_creates_templates_subdirectory_with_one_png_per_entry() {
  86:     let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
  87:     let mut dict = VisualDictionary::new(tmp.path(), Rect::new(0, 0, 200, 200));
  88:     dict.insert(&PathBuf::from("a.rs"), synth_template(1), Some(50)).unwrap();
  89:     dict.insert(&PathBuf::from("b.rs"), synth_template(2), Some(72)).unwrap();
  90:     dict.save().unwrap();
  91: 
  92:     let templates_dir = tmp.path().join(".ghostwriter").join("templates");
  93:     assert!(templates_dir.exists(), ".ghostwriter/templates must exist");
  94: 
  95:     let pngs: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(&templates_dir)
  96:         .unwrap()
  97:         .filter_map(|e| e.ok())
  98:         .filter(|e| e.path().extension().map(|x| x == "png").unwrap_or(false))
  99:         .collect();
 100:     assert_eq!(pngs.len(), 2, "one PNG per inserted entry");
 101: }
 102: 
 103: #[test]
 104: fn slashes_in_paths_are_sanitised_into_filenames() {
 105:     // Insert `src/foo/bar.rs` — the on-disk filename must replace separators
 106:     // so the file lives in templates/ (not as a nested tree).
 107:     let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
 108:     let mut dict = VisualDictionary::new(tmp.path(), Rect::new(0, 0, 200, 200));
 109:     dict.insert(&PathBuf::from("src/foo/bar.rs"), synth_template(1), None).unwrap();
 110:     dict.save().unwrap();
 111: 
 112:     let templates_dir = tmp.path().join(".ghostwriter").join("templates");
 113:     let pngs: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(&templates_dir).unwrap()
 114:         .filter_map(|e| e.ok())
 115:         .map(|e| e.file_name().to_string_lossy().into_owned())
 116:         .collect();
 117:     assert_eq!(pngs.len(), 1);
 118:     assert!(!pngs[0].contains('/'),
 119:             "path separators must be replaced in PNG filename: {pngs:?}");
 120: }
 121: 
 122: #[test]
 123: fn update_position_persists_through_round_trip() {
 124:     let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
 125:     let mut dict = VisualDictionary::new(tmp.path(), Rect::new(0, 0, 200, 200));
 126:     dict.insert(&PathBuf::from("a.rs"), synth_template(1), Some(50)).unwrap();
 127:     dict.update_position(&PathBuf::from("a.rs"), 200, 0.91);
 128:     dict.save().unwrap();
 129: 
 130:     let loaded = VisualDictionary::load(tmp.path()).unwrap().unwrap();
 131:     let entry = loaded.get(&PathBuf::from("a.rs")).unwrap();
 132:     assert_eq!(entry.meta.last_known_y, Some(200));
 133:     assert!((entry.meta.confidence - 0.91).abs() < 1e-6,
 134:             "confidence should round-trip: got {}", entry.meta.confidence);
 135: }

   2: ///
   3: /// No local crate dependencies.
   4: use std::fmt;
   5: 
   6: #[derive(Debug)]
   7: pub enum AppError {
   8:     Io(std::io::Error),
   9:     InvalidInput(String),
  10:     ProcessingFailed { reason: String },
  11: }
  12: 
  13: impl fmt::Display for AppError {
  14:     fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
  15:         match self {
  16:             AppError::Io(e) => write!(f, "I/O error: {e}"),
  17:             AppError::InvalidInput(msg) => write!(f, "Invalid input: {msg}"),
  18:             AppError::ProcessingFailed { reason } => write!(f, "Processing failed: {reason}"),
  19:         }
  20:     }
  21: }
  22: 
  23: impl From<std::io::Error> for AppError {
  24:     fn from(e: std::io::Error) -> Self {
  25:         AppError::Io(e)
  26:     }
  27: }

===== /tests/fixtures/tiny_repo/src/processor.rs =====

   1: /// Record processing logic for tiny_repo.
   2: ///
   3: /// Depends on: crate::config::Config, crate::types::{Record, UserId}, crate::error::AppError
   4: use crate::config::Config;
   5: use crate::error::AppError;
   6: use crate::types::{OutputFormat, Record, UserId};
   7: 
   8: pub struct Processor {
   9:     config: Config,
  10: }
  11: 
  12: impl Processor {
  13:     pub fn new(config: Config) -> Self {
  14:         Self { config }
  15:     }
  16: 
  17:     pub fn run(&self, records: &[Record]) -> Result<Vec<String>, AppError> {
  18:         if records.len() > self.config.max_records {
  19:             return Err(AppError::InvalidInput(format!(
  20:                 "too many records: {} > {}",
  21:                 records.len(),
  22:                 self.config.max_records
  23:             )));
  24:         }
  25: 
  26:         let output = records.iter().map(|r| self.format_record(r)).collect();
  27: 
  28:         if self.config.verbose {
  29:             eprintln!("processed {} records", records.len());
  30:         }
  31: 
  32:         Ok(output)
  33:     }
  34: 
  35:     pub fn format_record(&self, record: &Record) -> String {
  36:         match self.config.output_format {
  37:             OutputFormat::Json => {
  38:                 format!(
  39:                     r#"{{"id":{},"name":"{}","value":{}}}"#,
  40:                     record.id.0, record.name, record.value
  41:                 )
  42:             }
  43:             OutputFormat::Csv => {
  44:                 format!("{},{},{}", record.id.0, record.name, record.value)
  45:             }
  46:             OutputFormat::Plain => {
  47:                 format!("[{}] {} = {}", record.id.0, record.name, record.value)
  48:             }
  49:         }
  50:     }
  51: 
  52:     pub fn filter_by_user(&self, records: &[Record], user_id: &UserId) -> Vec<Record> {
  53:         records
  54:             .iter()
  55:             .filter(|r| r.id == *user_id)
  56:             .cloned()
  57:             .collect()
  58:     }
  59: }

===== /tests/fixtures/tiny_repo/src/types.rs =====

   1: /// Shared domain types for tiny_repo.
   2: ///
   3: /// No local crate dependencies.
   4: 
   5: /// Unique identifier for a user record.
   6: #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
   7: pub struct UserId(pub u64);
   8: 
   9: /// A single data record.
  10: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
  11: pub struct Record {
  12:     pub id: UserId,
  13:     pub name: String,
  14:     pub value: f64,
  15: }
  16: 
  17: /// Output format options.
  18: #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
  19: pub enum OutputFormat {
  20:     Json,
  21:     Csv,
  22:     Plain,
  23: }
  24: 
  25: impl Record {
  26:     pub fn new(id: u64, name: impl Into<String>, value: f64) -> Self {
  27:         Self {
  28:             id: UserId(id),
  29:             name: name.into(),
  30:             value,
  31:         }
  32:     }
  33: }
